# Engine architecture reference (`engine/src/`)

Reference detail, **not loaded automatically**: read the entry for a file before changing it. The short, always-relevant version is `engine/CLAUDE.md`; this is the per-file detail it points into. Keep it current when a file's shape changes, but it is a map of the code as it is, not a changelog.

## The workspace

- `engine/` — a TypeScript library package, ESM (`"type": "module"`), NodeNext resolution. Emits to `dist/` with declarations; `package.json` `exports`/`main`/`types` point there. No I/O, no UI — a pure state machine driven entirely through `dispatch`.

## What's implemented

Full turn/step loop, casting + targeting + fizzle, a LIFO stack, SBAs, full combat (first
strike/double strike/trample/deathtouch/lifelink/menace, multi-blocker damage assignment as a
player choice, a first-strike priority window, must-be-blocked), activated/triggered abilities
(intervening-ifs, `{X}` costs, loyalty abilities), the full continuous-effects layer pipeline
(1 copy, 2 control, 3 text, 4 type-change, 5 colour, 6 keywords/ability-loss, 7 P/T incl. a CDA
layer), tokens (with a `stackCount` compaction scheme so a self-replicating generator can't blow
up object count — see "Token stacking" below), Auras & Equipment (an Aura put onto the battlefield without being cast chooses what it enchants, rule 303.4f), Commander (command-zone casting
with tax, the command zone as its owner's choice — a state-based action once it's in a graveyard or exile (903.9a), a replacement for a hand or library (903.9b) — commander damage, Partner and every variant of
it — Partner with, Partner—[text]/Friends forever, Choose a Background, Doctor's companion — as a
declarative `CardDefinition.pairing`, the legend rule),
an opt-in simultaneous-mulligan phase, 2-4 player tables (APNAP ordering, per-defender blocker
declarations), a full replacement-effects pipeline (`replacements.ts`), modal/"you may"/targeted-
modal spells, mass effects (wraths, edicts) and tutors/scry/surveil, a rich effect vocabulary
(bounce/exile/mill/discard, fight, counter, control-change, clone, animate,
count-scaled and trigger-supplied amounts, token-for-target and token-copy effects, conditional
effects, sacrifice costs incl. filtered ones, additional costs & kicker), planeswalkers, alternate
casting zones (flashback, retrace, suspend, foretell, plot, escape, disturb, adventure, MDFCs, transforming DFCs
+ Day/Night, Channel from hand, Overload, a conditional free-cast permission, Convoke), extra
turns/combats, cascade/storm/spell-copying, Sagas, station cards (Spacecraft — rule 702.184, 721), the Monarch/Energy/Emblems, damage-prevention
shields, and a color-identity-aware Commander deck validator (`identity.ts`, `deck-validation.ts`).

**Every player decision is a dispatched action**, never a synchronous controller callback. Each of the 31 kinds is a module under `decisions/` owning its whole *answer* half — offer, validator, controller arm, bot candidates — registered in a total table, so adding one fails the build at that table rather than several hundred lines away. Applying stays on `Game`. See `docs/plans/decision-registry.md`. The kinds:
`declare-attackers` / `declare-blockers` / `assign-combat-damage` / `discard` /
`choose-from-zone` / `mulligan` (answered by `mulligan`, then `put-on-bottom`) / `commander-replacement` /
`pay-life-for-untapped` / `miracle-reveal` (rule 702.94a: a drawn miracle card's "you may reveal", asked by `placePendingTriggers` (`askMiracleReveal`) before its trigger is placed — no `decisionSource`, and the cost redacted from other seats, since the card is still hidden; declined, the trigger is dropped) / `reveal-for-untapped` (a reveal land's "you may reveal a [type] card from your hand", asked before it moves by `askEnterChoice`; the offer's `options` are redacted from other seats) / `choose-copy` / `choose-enchant` (offering permanents, or for an "Enchant player" Aura players — rule 303.4f — answered with either) / `legend-rule` / `order-triggers` (rule 603.3b: a player who orders their own triggers — `GameState.ordersOwnTriggers`, set by `Game.setOrdersOwnTriggers` from the client's setting — is asked by `placePendingTriggers`, before any of the batch goes on the stack, when two or more of theirs wait that aren't all one ability; the answer lists them in resolution order and marks them `ordered`, so `triggerPlacement` keeps it; everyone else, bots included, gets the engine's order: `stackFirst`, then triggers that read the board (`countOf`, an `aggregate`/`controls` condition), then the rest, then those that add to it (a token, a copy, counters, P/T), each group in the order they triggered — `triggerBoardRole` — so what grows the board resolves before what counts it) / `choose-creature-type` / `choose-modes` / `choose-targets` /
`sacrifice` / `scry` / `proliferate` / `time-travel` (rule 701.56a: two disjoint subsets of `eligible` — add a time counter, remove one — applied at once; a suspended card left with none triggers its free play (`suspendTrigger`); no bot candidates, v2 takes v1's `chooseTimeTravel`: remove from suspended and impending, add to the rest) / `remove-counters` (which counters come off which objects, `min` to `max` in all, `single` all from one, `perObject` a cap each — `decisions/remove-counters.ts`, whose `removalOffer` reads each eligible object's allowed counters off the state, a token stack's every token's unless `single`, a suspended card's time counters; raised by `Game.beginRemoveCounters` for a chosen `AbilityCost.removeCounter` once the ability is on the stack, `priorityTo` its activator, or for the `remove-counters` effect, unasked where one object holds one allowed kind and the count is fixed; answered by `applyCounterRemovals`, which peels a stack's tokens one at a time; candidates: the plain answer, the cheapest counters first — an opponent's object, by each eligible entry's `controller`, its best first — and with a range the fewest too) / `split-mana` (how many of each colour, summing to N, for "N mana in any combination of …" past the 35 splits `addManaChoice` lists as modes; no bot candidates — the evaluation can't tell colours apart, so v2 takes v1's `chooseManaSplit`: the coloured pips of the hand and command zone less the pool, then the commander's colours) / `choose-permanents` (a choice made as an effect resolves, no targeting — "untap up to two lands"; its `chooser` may be another player, the filter read from their side; a forced pick of some offers the search each option, least valuable first; with `maxTotalPower` a keep under a total-power cap, Slaughter the Strong's `keep-total-power`, its offer carrying each eligible permanent's `powers` and a controller's `choosePermanents` the `PowerCap`; with `exchangeText` an entering permanent's "you may exchange its text box and another creature's" — `CardDefinition.exchangeTextOnEnter`, Deadpool — asked by `askEnterChoice`, recorded as `enterChoice.exchangeText` and done by `moveObject` as it enters; a choice of at most one gives the bots each eligible permanent as a candidate) / `choose-number` (a `choose-value` effect's number, chosen as it resolves: any whole number from `min`, up to `max`; resolution resumes through `ResolutionResume` with the number read as `"x"`; the bots search `min` up to eleven past it) / `separate-piles` and `choose-pile` (rule 700.3 — the `separate-into-piles` effect's two halves, `decisions/separate-piles.ts` and `choose-pile.ts` over `decisions/shared/piles.ts`: the separator's partition of `objects` into two piles, checked by `whyNotTwoPiles`, then the chooser's pile index; both raised from the effect's `ResolutionContext` (`askSeparatePiles`, `askChoosePile` — which reveals the face-up pile) and answered into `GameState.pilesAnswer`, the effect resuming through `resumeAfterDecisions`; the view shows the objects to the separator and a face-down pile only to `lookers`; offers carry each object's rough `worth` and `whose`, and the bots split by `bestSplitFor` — every split of a dozen or fewer weighed against the chooser's best reply, more balanced greedily — and choose by `bestPileFor`; v2 searches each pile, and the best split with the two lopsided ones) / `venture` (rule 701.49 — which dungeon to enter, or which room at a fork; the offer carries the `DungeonMap`s a driver draws, `Game.ventureInto`/`applyVenture`) / `enter-attacking` (what each creature put onto the battlefield attacking attacks — rule 508.4: `Game.putIntoAttack`, reached from a `create-token`'s `attacking` (`EnterAttacking`: `"choose"`, or `{ player, orTheirPlaneswalker? }` for "attacking that player" — resolved, `{ defender }` too, one exact player or planeswalker for ninjutsu, rule 702.49c, attacked only while it still can be, 508.4a — with the `for-each-player` effect for "for each opponent"), applies rule 506.3a-c — only a creature, under the attacking player, during combat — sets `attacking` to its first option, and queues the question in `pendingEnterAttacking` only where there's more than one; `promptNextEnterAttacking` asks it in the `prepareForPriority` fixpoint. It emits `entered-attacking`, never `attacker-declared`, so no attack trigger sees it (508.3a). A card enters attacking the same way through `look-and-choose`'s and `reveal-until`'s `attacking` (Kaalia, Winota, Raph & Mikey), and a token copy through `create-token-copy`'s `tapped`/`attacking`/`exileAtEndOfCombat` — the last a real delayed trigger at the new `"end-of-combat"` timing. `for-each-player` parks the players still to go when one's effect stops to ask something, and a `may` with `aboutThatPlayer` puts that player on its `choose-modes` offer (`about`): myriad (`cards/helpers.ts`'s `myriad()`) asks once per other opponent) / `cast-now` (`upTo`/`totalManaValue` with `repeat` are limits kept in `CastNowProgress` and shown on the offer as `castsLeft`/`manaValueLeft`; "you may cast [a card]" during a resolution, rule 608.2g — Chandra, Acolyte of Flame's one target card; hideaway's linked card (`from: "exiled-with-source"` — `GameObject.exiledWith`, rule 607.2a), where `play: true` also offers a land card, as a `play-land` in the decision's `lands`, played on its player's own turn with a land play left, which it uses (rules 305.2a, 305.2b, 305.3 — `Game.whyCannotPlayLandNow`); a card picked from a hand, graveyard or looked-at library top — Baral's Expertise, Diviner of Mist, Velomachus Lorehold: the offer is the `cast-spell` offers with `via: "effect"` of every card on offer (`cards`, plus `looked` — library cards shown to that player alone, castable or not), timing ignored, worked out as it's raised on its terms (`free`: "without paying its mana cost", the only way offered — an alternative cost of {0} that kicker may be paid on top of, rule 118.9d; `spell`: a filter the spell must match as it would be cast — its face, its prototype, rule 601.3e — both held to by `whyCannotCastSpell` through `Game.castNowOffer`); the answer carries one built into a `cast-spell`, or `null`, and `Game.applyCastNow` casts it through the ordinary `castSpell`; the client sends a `via: "effect"` cast as this answer, and `decisions/shared/random-cast.ts` builds the fuzzer's; the v1 bot casts the dearest card on offer) alongside the priority actions. `legalActions(player)` enumerates what's
playable right now with per-slot target options; `viewFor(player)` produces a redacted,
self-contained snapshot for one seat.

**Not modeled**, and every other known rules gap, is listed under "Engine rules gaps" in
`BACKLOG.md` — Battles, dungeons/Initiative/Ring, banding, Companion, replacement
ordering, static dependency ordering and snow sources among them.

**Token stacking** (an engine resource-safety mechanism, not a rule — added after the fuzzer hung
on a self-replicating-token card): `GameObject.stackCount` lets one object stand in for many
fully interchangeable, still-pristine token copies instead of minting one object per copy.
`Game.mintTokenBatch` folds new batches into a matching existing stack; a fresh batch only
originates a stack past `STACK_ORIGIN_THRESHOLD` (8), so ordinary "create two tokens" cards are
untouched. Only tokens with no activated ability and only count-scalable, non-targeted triggered
abilities are ever stacked (`Game.isStackableTokenName`). `Game.splitOneFromStack` peels one off
into an ordinary object the instant anything singles it out (a chosen sacrifice; a target is split
*once, as it's chosen* — `Game.lockInTargets` at cast, activation and trigger placement, after
costs are paid — so every step of a multi-step effect, and any copy of the spell, reaches the same
token); `Game.materializeStack` expands a stack into real objects for combat, up to
`MAX_MATERIALIZED` = 100; past that an attacking stack fights as **one counted attacker**
(`attackingStackPart`, split off with its count by `splitCountFromStack` when it's part of the
stack): every token declared attacks (rule 508.1a), each creature blocking it splits off a token
of its own to block (or a shared one, a declaration's `attackerMember`; the offer's
`attackerTokens` says which attackers are stacks, and `combat/blocking.ts`'s `blockedTokens` /
`whyMembersInvalid` count menace and limits per token), so a counted attacker is always unblocked
and deals its damage as one event from that many sources (`dealDamage`'s `sources`, the
`damage-dealt` event's `sources`: per-source replacements apply to each, the life, poison and
lifelink are the total, a "deals damage" trigger fires once per source for its share, an attack
trigger once per token, and "attacks alone" or "attacked with N" count tokens). A blocking stack
still wakes at most the cap; `recompactTokens` (end of cleanup) folds tokens back into stacks once nothing tells them apart (`tokenFoldKey`, shared with `findMergeableStack`) and nothing else names them by id (`pinnedTokenIds` — an attachment's host, a delayed trigger's target, a prevention shield's) — a group of eight or more, one holding a stack, or one holding a token split off a stack (`GameObject.splitFromStack`, set by `splitOneFromStack` on the token it peels off and on a stack drained to its last). Mid-turn, `refoldSplitTokens` does the same as soon as nothing is waiting (run by `prepareForPriority` just before priority is granted, with an empty stack and no trigger or decision pending), so a trigger that fires once per token and puts counters on "it" (Tribute to the World Tree) leaves one stack once its last copy resolves, not one object per token (each fold emits `tokens-folded` {`into`, `folded`}, so a client finds what earlier events named by the folded ids on the stack: the counters' float, 2026-11-11): it folds only tokens the same in *everything* (`token-fold.ts`'s `exactTokenShape` — per-turn records included), only groups holding a split-off token, and never folds away a token the rest of the state names (`referencedObjectIds` — the turn's history of what entered keeps counting it), folding the others into it instead, and leaves a token that attacked this turn to cleanup (the journal's `attackers` counts by object). Counters put on a whole stack were put on each token: a `counters-put` watcher fires once per token (`counter-added`'s `count` when a batch entering with counters joins a stack already there). **Anything that counts permanents counts a stack as every token in it** — `permanentCount` (`state.ts`), behind `countOf`, cost reductions, "controls N or more" conditions, count-scaled P/T and the died-this-turn count; counting objects instead had Krenko, Mob Boss making two Goblins forever. An "each **other** …" count bonus (`grantPtPerCount.excludeSelf`) beside a stacked source still counts the rest of that stack (`othersInOwnStack`, of the affected permanent's stack when the count is `relativeToAffected` — the filter's source and "other" are then each affected permanent, as Sliver Legion and Coat of Arms read them), since each token in it is "other" to the rest. Because that makes counts of a million reachable, loops that mint one real thing per unit (individually made tokens, mana units, unscalable trigger copies) stop at `Game.MAX_EFFECT_INSTANCES` (1000), and a draw loop stops at the first empty-library draw. The same holds on the stack: a triggered ability that chooses no targets and isn't count-scalable, fired once per token of a stack (Authority of the Consuls seeing twenty Goblins enter), is queued once with `PendingTrigger.copies` and goes on the stack as one ability object with that `stackCount` — each copy still its own resolution, one at a time with priority between (`removeOneAbilityCopy`), a counter taking one. See
`token-stacking.test.ts`, `token-stack-counts.test.ts`, `token-stack-refold.test.ts`, `token-stack-targets.test.ts` and `random-demo.mjs` for the original repro.

## File map

Tests live in `engine/src/test/*.test.ts` (one directory level deeper than the source they cover,
so relative imports need an extra `../`), except `cards/pool.test.ts`, which stays colocated with
the `cards/pool/`/`cards/tokens/` layout it guards. `server/src/test/*.test.ts` mirrors this. Discovery is
vitest's default glob, which finds them wherever they are; `tsconfig` excludes `src/**/*.test.ts`
and all of `src/test/`, so the shared card-test table **`src/test/harness.ts`** (`table`,
`spawn`/`enter`, `cast`/`activate` that throw `Game.canDispatch`'s refusal, the `pick*`/`watchTargets`
seat answers, `attack`/`blockOffer`/`toStep`, `destroy`/`grant`, and computed reads) never reaches
`dist/`. Vitest doesn't type-check tests, so `engine/tsconfig.tests.json` does: everything the build
compiles plus every test file, nothing emitted (`npm run typecheck:tests -w engine`, which is all the
engine's `typecheck` — and so the root's, CI's and `npm run verify`'s — runs: since 2026-10-26 without
a separate pass over the build's config first, which the build itself already checks). It is
incremental (2026-10-31), its state in the gitignored `engine/.cache/tsconfig.tests.tsbuildinfo`: ~3.5 s
warm after a small edit against ~15 s cold (a change to a signature the whole pool imports, like
`cards/helpers.ts`'s, re-checks the pool and costs a cold run); CI starts cold.
Tests read `Game.registry`, public for them and for tools, directly or through the harness's `registryOf`. `engine/vitest.config.ts` sets four things and
deliberately leaves discovery alone. A plugin, `scripts/vitest-card-bundle.mjs`, serves the card
barrel (`cards/generated.ts`) as one module with every card file inlined (rolldown, once per run,
from the sources as they are), its shared imports left as imports, and gives each shard module its
cards from the barrel's `CARD_FILES` — one object per card however a test reaches it. Vite
transforms a module graph a file at a time, so the barrel's 16,000 imports cost each worker over a
minute before its first test (2026-10-20: a single test file 65 s, the suite 166 s); with it, ~9 s
and ~53 s. `cards/pool.test.ts` lists the card files on disk and reads their sources with `fs`
instead of globbing 16,000 imports, checking `CARD_FILES` names exactly those files. It raises `testTimeout` to 30s: these are CPU-bound
simulations rather than the async I/O vitest's 5s default is sized for, and several sat close
enough to that limit to pass alone and fail on a busy machine. The two real long-runners
(`bot-plan.test.ts`, `eval-bot.test.ts`) keep their own larger per-file overrides. And it turns
off `isolate`, so each worker imports the card barrel once rather than once per test file,
which took the suite from 4m30s to under a minute. That is safe only while no engine test
mocks, spies, stubs a global or fakes timers. **While authoring, run just the files you touched**
(`npx vitest run cmdr-sokka pool.test` from `engine/`, ~10-20s), and the whole suite once per batch
before committing. The third is a reporter beside the default one, `scripts/vitest-progress.mjs`,
which writes the run's progress (one unit per test file) through `scripts/progress.mjs`;
`server/vitest.config.ts` adds the same reporter and nothing else — nothing there runs long,
since its pacing tests drive a fake clock.

**Progress files** (`scripts/progress.mjs`): a long job writes
`.claude/progress/<label>-<pid>-<n>.json` at the repo root (git-ignored) — `{ label, done, total,
failed, startedAt, updatedAt, finished }`, at most twice a second, written then renamed so a
reader never sees half of one, and closed on process exit. The engine and server test runs, the
client's Playwright suite (`client/e2e/progress-reporter.mjs`, one unit per test),
`worker-pool.mjs` (so `bot:ab`, `bot:diff`, `bot:decks`), the fuzzer, `bot:bench`/`bot:tune`
(one per match), `bot:behaviour` and `bot:crackback` write one. A Claude Code mod (`job-progress`,
kept outside the repo) polls the folder and draws a bar per job above the prompt; nothing else
reads them, and a failed write is ignored. Each new job sweeps finished files over a minute old.

- **`filter.ts` / `effects.ts`** — `bindDynamicCompares` binds a bare `n: "x"` to the resolution's X and `isSource` / `sameNameAsSource` to its source (`CardFilter.appliedBy`), so every mass effect reads them however its matching is done (2026-11-09). A source that has left is read by its last-known names (rules 113.7a, 608.2h).
- **`state.ts` / `game.ts`** — `ChoosePermanentsResolution` (2026-11-09): a `choose-permanents` effect's `then` keeps the resolution's trigger value and object and its kept targets' zones.
- **`actions.ts` / `target-count.ts`** — `ConvokeOffer.proofByTargetCount` (a payment for each count of targets, Strive-style) and `targetTaxes.taxes[].once` (`TargetTaxShare`: a "that target …" tax shared by targets, counted once by `targetsWithinTaxes`) (2026-11-09).
- **`state.ts`** — `GameState.namedOffSlot` (2026-11-09): the objects the resolution under way has named other than by a target slot, each with its zone-change count then; `objectRefAct` reads it so a slot whose object an earlier step moved under another name no longer means it (rule 400.7), while a slot named first keeps following its object. `GameObject.putOnStackBy` and `spellCaster` (who put a spell on the stack, kept once a `gain-control-spell` changes its controller: "you" on a spell is its controller, "a spell you cast" this; each such effect is on the spell's `controlEffects`, which `Game.keepSpellControl` carries to the permanent it becomes, rule 110.2b; `exchange-control` with a spell as one side, Sudden Substitution, goes through the same `gainControlOfSpell`, and `change-targets`' `by: "its-controller"` has the spell's controller choose). `GameObject.seatTargetWaiting` (a spell with a new controller, or another player's copy: `Game.rebindSeatTargets` moves each `{ seat }` target to its player's new slot, and this is the slot holding the one whose player now has none). `SpellSnapshot.controller`. `DelayedTrigger.sourceCopy` (a copied spell as it set up a `whenever`: the copy ceases to exist, rule 707.10a, and its ability runs on). `PlayerState.turnsTaken` (a player's own turns this game, the one under way included; read by the `turns-taken` condition). the journal's `attackers` (each `attacked` entry's `was`, as declared), `"turned-face-up"`, `regenerated`, `surveilled`, `ownLibrarySearched`, `cardsPutIntoExile` (`recordJournal`, `beginLibrarySearch` for the search); `GameObject.turnedFaceUpOnTurn`; `GameObject.asCastFacts` (`CardDefinition.asCast` as a spell finished being cast, rule 601.2i: a count, or 1 or 0; never copied to a copy of it, read by the condition `as-cast` and the amount `asCast`); `CopyEnds.whileSource`: a temporary copy ended as a condition stops holding, `endWhileSourceCondition`/`endWhileSourceEffects` reading it beside modifiers and control effects; `WhileSource.attachedTo`, ended by `permanent-unattached`. `PtModifier.goadedBy`: a goad for as long as that modifier lasts, its end one of the durations a modifier has (read by `goadersOf`); `cantAttackYou` (`cantAttackYouCovers` narrowing it to "you or planeswalkers you control", Promise of Loyalty) and `cantBlockCreaturesOf`, which `combat/eligibility.ts` reads. `PtModifier.restrictionsLater`: restrictions that apply only during that player's next turn (Wall of Dust), which become an ordinary until-end-of-turn modifier as that turn begins.spell finished being cast, rule 601.2i: a count, or 1 or 0; never copied to a copy of it, read by the condition `as-cast` and the amount `asCast`); `GameObject.foretoldReducedBy` (a foretell cost given, read on the face cast, in place of `foretoldCost`); `choose-from-zone`'s `faceDownLooks` and `looksAtFaceDown`, and its `leftoverCounters` (a `look-and-choose`'s leftover exiled with counters — Karn, Scion of Urza).`GameObject.attackedOnTurns` (pruned as each turn begins, in `tokenFoldKey`), `PlayerState.turnTakenBefore`, and `controllersLastTurn`: "their controller's last turn", the one before the turn under way on their own.`GameObject.enteredDevoured` (rule 702.82b): what it devoured this stint, each as a `LastKnownInfo`, carried into its own snapshot; `LastKnownInfo.manaCost`. `createdWith` on a token (`TokenLink`, the permanent it was created with and that permanent's stint, in `tokenFoldKey`): set by `linkedToSource` through `Game.mintingLink` on all an effect mints, read by the filter clause `createdWithSource`, bound by `bindDynamicCompares` (a `delayed-trigger`'s effect as it's created), and the count `countersOnMaker`. `attackingNothing` on an attacker: what it attacked stopped being a planeswalker or battle and was removed from combat (rule 506.4c), `removeNoncreaturesFromCombat` sets it, and `dealCombatDamage` deals nothing to `attacking` while it's set. `GameObject.linkedExile` is what a token's ability means by "the exiled card" (`link-exiled-to-created`, rule 607.2a): on the token, so a stack split keeps it and a copy doesn't, in `tokenFoldKey` and `LastKnownInfo` so it outlives the token. `DelayedWheneverWatch` carries `watching`, `forGame`, `once` and `orLosesControl` (`fireWheneverWatchers` and `watchedSubject` read them; a watched permanent that has left is read through `lastKnown`, 608.2h). `GameState` is one plain, `structuredClone`-able tree (no class instances, `Map`/`Set`, or functions inside it). Holds `rules` (`startingLife`, `openingHandSize`, `maxHandSize`, `maxLandsPerTurn`, `skipFirstDraw`), players, `objects` (card/ability instances — `GameObject.kind` `"card"`|`"ability"`, `abilityKind` `"activated"`|`"triggered"`|`"chapter"`, `counters` (with `counterTimestamps`, a keyword counter kind's latest placement), `modifiers`, combat fields — all cleared by `moveObject` on any zone change, bar a `countersPersistAcrossZones` card's counters on any move but to a hand or library — Skullbriar), `zones` (`perPlayer` library/hand/graveyard + `shared` battlefield/stack/exile/command as ordered `ObjectId[]`; stack top = last), `turn` (its `round` — each player's turn once, from the starting seat round to it again, an extra turn in the round it's taken in; set by `beginTurn` and on `turn-began`, and what the client says where a turn number would read as a longer game), `priority`, `result`, `awaiting` (a discriminated `AwaitingDecision` union — one variant per decision kind listed above, each carrying whatever context that decision needs, e.g. `assign-combat-damage {attacker, blockers, power, lethal, trample}`, `mulligan {player, hands}` where every player still deciding may act at once, `commander-replacement {commander, intendedZone}`, raised once a commander is in a graveyard or exile, or *before* a move to a hand or library), plus a grab-bag of pending-decision queues (`pendingBlockerDeclarations`, `pendingTriggers`, `pendingTargetedTrigger`, `commanderArrivals` (commanders put into a graveyard or exile since the last state-based check, each with the `zoneChangeCount` it arrived with — the next check queues its owner's 903.9a offer, `offerArrivedCommanders`, APNAP) and `deferredCommanderMove` + `pendingCommanderMoves` (a commander's 903.9 choice is never skipped: a 903.9a offer waits in its graveyard or exile, and a 903.9b move that can't be asked yet waits on the battlefield in the queue — or, for a move to a hand or library from the stack, a graveyard or exile, in that zone — and a question another decision overwrote is re-asked — `raiseNextCommanderChoice`, run at the top of `prepareForPriority` and after each state-based check), `pendingPayLifeForUntapped` (a shock land that entered while another decision was being answered — a fetch land's own search — sits there tapped until `raiseNextPayLifeOffer` offers the life, one land at a time), `pendingDestruction`, `pendingDiscards` (a scoped "each opponent discards" asks one player at a time; the rest wait here), `pendingSacrifices`/`pendingSacrificeVictims` — an entry's `rest` is the parts still to come of a "sacrifice a creature, an artifact, and a land", each paid by a different permanent), `heldChoices` (rule 101.4: while an `each-player-may` asks its players, the discards they choose wait here, hidden in hand, and the sacrifice victims aren't drained; `releaseHeldChoices` does all of it at once after the last player), `pendingCostSacrifice` (a sacrifice *cost* of several being chosen part by part, its picks still on the battlefield until the last part — see `game.ts`), `pendingTurnFaceUp` (a face-down permanent whose turn-face-up cost has a choice still open: it turns once that's answered, rule 702.37e) and `pendingAsTurnedFaceUp` (permanents just turned whose card's `asTurnedFaceUp.effect` is still to be done — Gift of Doom's "you may attach it to a creature" — each at the top of `prepareForPriority`, an Aura listed there spared 704.5m until its choice is answered), `suspendedResolutions` (a resolution that stopped partway to ask someone something: a `sequence` step that raised a decision parks the steps after it here, and a resolution ending with a decision still open parks an empty remainder; `prepareForPriority` resumes the newest once nothing is awaited or queued, and while any is parked no SBAs are performed and no triggers placed — rule 608.2c, 704.3; an instant, sorcery or ability whose steps are parked parks a `leaveStack` entry beneath them, so it leaves the stack as the final part of its resolution, rule 608.2n — a spell cast as it resolves is cast with it still on the stack, and may target an ability that's resolving; its `spell-resolved`/`ability-resolved` is emitted then too (`announceResolved`), as it leaves, not while it waits — a client plays the entry's exit off that event), `resolutionSince` (the `eventSeq` the resolution under way began at, across its parked steps: "this way" — the `thisWay` amount and `this-way` condition — is read off the discard/draw/mill/sacrifice events since) and `resolvingSource` (beside it while an *ability* resolves: its source and that source's timestamp, stamped as `entry.by` on whatever it puts onto the battlefield), `revealedThisTurn` (cards shown to every player — rule 701.16; a reveal is momentary but a client only ever sees whole *frames*, so the identity has to outlive the instant or the event names a card nobody can render. Turn-scoped, so it doesn't become a permanent window into a hand), `delayedTriggers` (delayed triggered abilities — rule 603.7; they belong to no permanent, so `detectTriggers`' battlefield scan can't see them: `enterStep` fires the step-keyed ones directly, and a `permanent-left-battlefield` event fires the ones keyed to that permanent leaving (`DelayedLeaveWatch` — earthbend's "when it dies or is exiled, return it", "when that creature dies this turn"; used up by its first departure, stint-keyed so a round trip isn't watched), and `detectTriggers` matches the "until end of turn, whenever …" ones against every event (`DelayedWheneverWatch`, `fireWheneverWatchers` — any `TriggerSpec`, its controller as "you" and its source as "self"; not used up; one with targets of its own queued as a `modifier` granted ability that chooses them; "until your next turn" ones kept past the turn and pruned beside the other next-turn effects), minting a stack object that carries the whole `DelayedTrigger` record because there is no card ability to look up by index) that let a mass effect or multi-step decision resume correctly after a player answers something mid-resolution, `decisionSource` (what raised `awaiting`; whether a decision *names* a source is `decisions/registry.ts`'s `decisionHasSource`, read off each module rather than a switch here — set around every resolution by `withDecisionSource`, carried across the sacrifice queue's deferral, cleared when `prepareForPriority` hands priority off with nothing pending; a decision the rules raise rather than a card does, like declaring blockers, is filtered out by the `decisionHasSource` selector rather than by clearing this), turn-scoped state (`preventAllCombatDamage`, `preventionShields` (but those lasting until a player's next turn or while an object stays), `damageUnpreventableThisTurn` (615.12 — `Game.damageUnpreventable` also reads a static's `damageCantBePrevented` and a `cantBePrevented` damage effect under way), `combatDamage`, `extraTurns`, `extraCombats`, `spellsCastThisTurn`, `PlayerEffect.combat` (a `restrict` effect's rule over the game, read by `restrictionsOf` and `combat/rules.ts`'s `recordsOn`: Kardur's goad-like rule of the game — Kardur, Doomscourge — lapsing as its controller's next turn begins; not a goad, rule 611.2c), `turnProhibitions` (a `prohibit` effect's "can't cast / activate this turn"), `playerEffects` (a `player-effect`'s cost reduction, free-from-hand permission, flash permission — `castAsThoughFlash`, for the turn or with `next` for the next matching spell, which `Game.spendNextSpellFlash` ends as that spell is cast — or damage multiplier, gone at cleanup or as its owner's next turn begins), `dayNight`, `monarch`, `emblems` — each an `EmblemState`; one with triggered abilities also has an `object`, a `GameObject` of kind `"emblem"` (registry name `Emblem`, `cards/emblem.ts`) in the command zone but in no zone list, owned and controlled by the emblem's owner, its abilities on one permanent `grantsTriggered` modifier, which `detectTriggers` scans beside the battlefield (rule 114.4); `createdBy` marks one an opponent gave, which the bots' `emblems` feature doesn't count as an asset), and `eventLog`. `PlayerState` carries `energy`, `counters` (every other kind a player has — poison, where ten lose the game as an SBA, and experience; given by `add-player-counters`, read by the `playerCounters` amount/count/cost reduction and the `player-counters` condition, grown by proliferate), `commanderCastCounts` (per-commander tax, Partner-aware), `spellsCastThisTurnAs` (each spell cast this turn as it was cast — a `CastSpellRecord`, the spell's last-known snapshot on the stack with its {X} in its mana value and how it was cast as its `entry.cast` (who, from where, `castVia` — so void's "a spell was warped this turn", rule 702.185c, is a `cast-this-turn` `{ castVia: "warp" }` over everyone, a countered one included) — which the `cast-this-turn` and `trigger-spell-first` conditions, a cast trigger's first/Nth matching spell and a `costModification`'s `firstEachTurn` read, so an Adventure cast as its instant was a noncreature spell), `commanderDamageTaken` (keyed by the **commander's object id**, not its controller — rule 903.10a counts each commander separately, so two Partners at 11 apiece isn't a loss, and a stolen commander's damage still counts toward that commander; a commander keeps its id through every zone change), `spellsCastThisTurn`. `GameObject` carries the full set of per-permanent flags the layer/effect system needs: `markedByDeathtouch` (dealt deathtouch damage since state-based actions were last checked — each check clears it, 704.5h), `regenerationShields` (rule 701.15a — each replaces one destruction this turn, an effect's or lethal/deathtouch damage's, with removing the damage, tapping and removing from combat, unless it "can't be regenerated" (`pendingDestructionNoRegen` for a queued wipe); gone at cleanup), `summoningSick`, `attacking` (`PlayerId | ObjectId`), `isToken`, `attachedTo`, `isCommander`, `controlEffects` (timestamped layer-2 control effects from resolved spells/abilities — `recomputeControl` weighs them against attached control Auras, latest wins, rule 613.7; a new one drops only older ones for the same player, so when a holder leaves the game the player still in it who most recently had control gets it back, 800.4a — a mass change shares one timestamp) + `controlEndsAtCleanup`, `copyOf` (+ `copyEndsAtCleanup`, a copy until end of turn — Cursed Mirror; the cleanup step clears both, its exceptions having been made until-end-of-turn modifiers), `faces`/`face` (multi-face cards — `faceName(object) = faces[face]`, `printedCardName(object) = copyOf ?? faceName(object)` is what every name/cost/color/type/P/T read goes through, and `rulesTextName(object) = textFrom ?? printedCardName(object)` every ability read — `textFrom` being the card whose rules text an exchange of text boxes gave it, layer 3, rule 612.5, cleared on a zone change and kept in last-known information as `textName`; and `doors` — rule 709.5, a Room's unlocked designations: set by `moveObject` as it enters (the half cast unlocked, 709.5d; `castHalf`), kept in step with `face` (`doorsFace`: one door unlocked is that half, both the whole card), and both locked makes `printedCardName` `LOCKED_ROOM` (`cards/locked-room.ts`, an enchantment — Room with no name, cost or text, registered as the face-down 2/2s are); `unlockDoor`/`lockDoor` change them, emitting `door-unlocked` (709.5h, `fully` per 709.5i), and the `unlock-door` special action (`whyCannotUnlock`, `payToUnlock`, sorcery timing, the half's cost less any `abilityCostModification.unlock` — `unlockCost`) and effect use them; `classLevel` — rule 716, a Class's level, absent for level 1 (716.2d): set by the `gain-class-level` effect (`ctx.setClassLevel`, emitting `class-level-gained`), cleared on a zone change (716.2b) and kept in last-known information, read by the `class-level` static condition (`characteristics.ts`), which is how `classLevel()`/`atLevel()` gate a Class's bars and abilities — `faceDown` — rule 708, a manifested or cloaked permanent, or a card cast face down with morph or disguise (`via: "face-down"`, {3}, a face-down spell on the stack too — 708.4; `Game.faceDef` and the view read the 2/2 for it, `withFaceDown` turns a card down to price and check the cast; turned face up for its morph cost, megamorph with a counter; `turned-face-up` triggers; manifest dread is a `look-and-choose` to `"manifest"`) — makes `printedCardName` one of `FACE_DOWN_CARDS`, the internal face-down 2/2 definitions in `cards/face-down.ts` that `createDefaultRegistry` registers beside the pool, so every characteristic, ability and copiable-value read sees the 2/2 and its own enters abilities never happen, 708.3; cleared on a zone change, where `moveObject` reveals it, 708.9, and its public stint is closed and reopened under its real name — `revealFaceDown`, also at turning face up and at the end of the game), `modifiers` (a `PtModifier[]` spanning layers 3–7d), `xValue`, `chosenModes`, `kicked`, `castVia` + the alt-zone markers (`grantedFlashback`, `suspended`+`counters.time`, `hastyUntilItLeaves`, `foretold`+`foretoldOnTurn`, `playPermission` — a permission to play it from exile, whoever owns it, ended by any zone change, its `expiry` from an `PermissionDuration` (`Game.impulseWindow`; `until-next-exile` ended by that stint's next exile, a `while-source`'s `controlledBy` swept before each priority by `lapseImpulsesOfLostControl`, `your-next-combat` in `expireUntilEndOfCombat`), and `sourceDid` a live gate off the journal's `attacked` and lore `counters-put` entries; with `source` + `whileSource`/`oncePerTurn` it's a static ability of the permanent that exiled it, whose `impulseCastOnTurn` spends the once-a-turn cast; `spendAs` is its "mana of any type can be spent to cast that spell", rule 118.14), `exiledByPlayer` (who exiled it — Haldan's "cards you exiled"; set by an `exile` or `exile-from-library` effect, cleared by any zone change), `isCopy`, `stormCount`, `onAdventure`, `exiledBy`/`exiledUntil`/`flickerLink` (what links an O-Ring's or a delayed flicker's return to its exile — `exiledUntil` holds the stint of the object it waits on, the source or another target, with `returnTo: "hand"` for a card taken from a hand, and `returnUntilLeft` returns the card as a one-shot effect right after that object leaves, no trigger, rule 610.3; `exiledUntilMonarch` is the player whose opponent becoming the monarch ends it, read by `returnUntilMonarch` in `setMonarch`, with `GameState.monarchBecameAt` for an event that came first, 610.3b), the designations `goadedBy`/`goadedForGameBy` (goads until the goader's next turn / for the rest of the game — read only through `goad.ts`) and `suspectedAt` (rule 701.60, the timestamp its menace and can't-block take in layer 6), `entry` (an `EntryRecord`: how it came onto the battlefield this stint — the zone it came from, whether it was cast and by whom from where (`castFrom`, set as it's cast) and under which permission (`via`, its `castVia`), and whose ability put it there — read by the `cast`/`castBy`/`castFrom`/`castVia`/`enteredFrom`/`putThereBySource` filter clauses) — all cleared by `moveObject` on any zone change unless noted otherwise. The exception is `lastKnown`, a `LastKnownInfo`: the permanent as it last existed on the battlefield (rules 603.10a, 608.2h) — computed name, P/T and base P/T, types, subtypes, supertypes, colours, keywords, whether it had a mana ability or any ability, counters, controller, mana value, token/commander, tapped/attacking/blocking, whether it entered kicked (`enteredKicked` — "if it was kicked" and Offspring's "if its offspring cost was paid" still answer once it has left), attachments, lost abilities and granted triggered abilities — taken by the move off the battlefield and **kept** through later moves until the next departure replaces it, stamped with the `zoneChangeCount` it had there. A spell or ability object carries `lastKnownRefs` (`LastKnownRefs`: the battlefield stint — `zoneChangeCount` — of its source and triggering object when it triggered or was activated (and `triggerSpell`, a triggering spell's stint on the stack: gone from it, "that spell's mana value" is its `lastStackManaValue`, X included), the permanent it sacrificed, and the one it tapped for its cost (station's "the tapped creature"); for a trigger, also what its event was aimed at — `recipient`, the permanent (with its stint) or player dealt damage — and `player`, the player the event names: the one dealt damage or controlling the permanent dealt it, or an attack's defending player; and, for a trigger fired by its object *leaving* the battlefield, `triggerObjectAfterLeaving` — where that object went, which is as far as "return it" follows it: moved on again since, it is a new object (rule 400.7) and `ResolutionContext.triggerObjectLost` makes acting on it a no-op, while reading it stays last-known information); and an enters or attacks trigger's object — on the battlefield as it triggered, `refs.triggerObject` its stint — that has left since, back or not, is lost the same way (Hero's Blade doesn't follow a creature that blinked), which is how a reader tells the departure it means from a later one; a target is known to have been a permanent from `targetZones`. Acting on an object needs it to still be the same one (rule 400.7): a stack object's `targetStints` and `sourceZoneChangeCount` record each target's and its source's `zoneChangeCount` as it went on the stack, and at resolution a target that has changed zones since (`targetsStillMeant`), or is otherwise no longer a legal target, is blanked (`targetLegality`, rule 608.2b): nothing acts on it or finds anything out about it, and the spell or ability doesn't resolve at all only when it chose a target and every one it chose is illegal — an "up to one" that chose none resolves, and each chosen mode of a modal spell applies with its own illegal targets blanked. A changed source is `sourceLost` — while reads of it, and of a target that leaves *during* the resolution (608.2h), go through last-known information. A delayed trigger chose no targets, so only its source's identity is checked (`DelayedTrigger.sourceStint`). `GameState.ceasedTokens` keeps a deleted token's snapshot for the rest of the turn, and past it while a delayed trigger names the token (rule 111.7; `Game.forgetCeasedTokens`); a copy of a spell that left the stack too, as it was there, while a trigger or a stack object names it (707.10a; `Game.ceaseSpellCopy`, every way a copy leaves), read by `lastKnownOfTarget` for any object that has ceased to exist. `enterChoice.transformed` (712.14a): put there transformed, so `Game.nextEnterChoice` asks the back face's choices. `EnteringTokenCopies.created`: plain tokens going the way of token copies because they have an as-enters choice (devour, 702.82a). Also exports pure selectors, `nameOf` among them — the name an object has, a copy exception's `setName` if one renamed it, which the legend rule and name filters read while the registry stays keyed by `printedCardName`. `GameState.phasesAfterThisCombat` (`AddedPhase`: an additional combat phase, its additional main phase when more phases follow it, or an additional beginning phase holding only an upkeep step — rule 500.10 — the most recently created first, 500.8) with `TurnState.addedUpkeep` (the upkeep under way is one of those), `TurnState.addedMain` (that main phase) and `TurnState.endsAfterAddedCombat` (the combat under way was added after the postcombat main phase, so the end step follows it), and `pendingCasualty` (`CasualtyAsk`: a spell's casualty costs still to offer, rule 702.153), and `pendingConspire` (`ConspireAsk`: its conspire costs, rule 702.78), and `pendingGifts` (`GiftAsk`: a spell cast with its gift promised whose opponent is still to be chosen, rule 702.174a — the spell's `giftTo` once chosen, carried onto the battlefield as `enteredGiftTo` and into its last-known information, and copied to a copy of the spell). One-off designations on a `GameObject`: `monstrous` (rule 701.37b — cleared by any zone change), `harnessed` (rule 701.64b — the `harness` effect's, read by an "∞ — " line's `infinity()` static, cleared by any zone change), `renowned` (rule 702.112b — the `renown` effect's, cleared the same way), `saddled` (rule 702.171b — until the cleanup step or a zone change; in the fold key and last-known information) and, on a creature, `saddledThisTurn` (702.171c — each `{ mount, stint }` it was tapped to pay a saddle cost of, cleared at cleanup and by its own zone change, so it outlives the Mount), `exertedBy` / `exertedOnTurn` (rule 701.43 — the exerter whose next untap step it skips, cleared as that untap step happens), and `enterChoice.riot` (rule 702.136, asked before it moves), `enterChoice.tributeFrom` / `tributePaid` (rule 702.104a, likewise — the opponent chosen, then their answer) with `tributeUnpaid`, which the entry sets for the `tribute-not-paid` condition and last-known information keeps. `GameState.pendingExerts` queues the attackers whose "you may exert this creature as it attacks" is still to be asked; `GameState.pendingEnlist` holds a declaration (its `DeclaredAttacker`s) whose attackers' enlist costs (rule 702.154) are still being asked, once per instance, before its tax is paid (`AttackCostDue`, rules 508.1g-j) and it's announced, a `choose-permanents` awaiting carrying `enlist`, and `GameObject.enlistedIn` the combat a creature last enlisted in (kept in last-known information); `GameObject.crewedThisTurn` the Vehicles a creature crewed this turn (rule 702.122b), as `saddledThisTurn`; a `choose-targets` awaiting may carry `divide` (a triggered ability's divided amount, its group's place in the answer), a `choose-from-zone` one `enterUnder` / `enterAs` (a `look-and-choose` over every graveyard), and a `choose-creature-type` one `riot`, or `tribute` (`"opponent"` / `"pay"`) with the `prompt` the client shows. `PlayerEffect` also carries the game-outcome locks — `cantLoseGame`, `cantWinGame`, `cantLoseLife` and `damageLifeFloor` (Angel's Grace, Everybody Lives!), each a list of players fixed as it resolved — and `PlayerState.spellNamesCastThisGame` counts the spells a player has cast this game by name (Approach of the Second Sun), never reset. The `choose-from-zone` awaiting's `order`/`thenOrder` (library ordering) and `priorityTo` (a cost's choice — priority back to the activator, as the `discard` awaiting's `priorityTo` is), its `"exile"` destination and `"bottom-any-order"` leftover. `PtModifier.setToughness` is a layer-7b set of toughness alone (an exchange with a life total, rule 701.12g — Tree of Redemption). the journal's `blocked` entries (2026-11-01; the journal's since 2026-11-07) hold each declared block, both creatures as they were then, recorded off `blocker-declared`, for `CardFilter.blockedThisTurn`; `DelayedTriggerTiming` has `end-of-combat-this-turn`, lapsing with the turn. The journal also counts cards put into a graveyard from hand or library and `left-graveyard` (Welcome the Dead's X, Essence Anchor's condition). `PlayerState.attackedByOnTurn` (the last turn each player declared a creature attacking this player — the player, not a planeswalker) and `lastTurnTaken` are what `attackedYouDuringTheirLastTurn` reads (Weathered Sentinels). `GameObject.exiledWithCount` is how many cards an `exile { linked }` has ever exiled with a permanent's stint (Colfenor's Urn), and the journal's `activated` entries (602.5b's limits) are this turn's, anyone's ("activate only once each turn" — Wall of Roots on an opponent's turn). `GrantedAbilityRef`'s `{ kind: "cycling", cardName }` names a card's cycling ability on the stack. `PlayerEffect.tokenMultiplier` doubles the tokens its owner creates for a while (Kaya, Geist Hunter's −2), multiplied into `tokenCreationMultiplier` with any Doubling Season. `PtModifier.untilEndOfCombat` ends with the combat phase (rule 500.5a); `GameObject.temporaryCopies` are the copy effects with a duration on a permanent (`TemporaryCopy`: its `CopyValues` and `CopyEnds` — end of turn, the next end step, or a player's next turn), oldest first, the last being what it is, with `copyBase` what it is under them all (rule 613.7a); `GameState.modesChosenEver` is the journal's `did` entries never cleared, for `modal`'s `notChosenBefore` (the awaiting `choose-modes`' `chosenEver` says which record). `tokenFoldKey` includes the turn a token entered, so tokens made on different turns never share a stack ("entered this turn" is exact per token), and the awaiting `choose-permanents` carries `priorityTo` when it pays an activation cost. `PlayerState.currentCombat`/`previousCombat` (`CombatId`) and `GameObject.lastAttack` answer "didn't attack during your last combat"; a `must-attack` record's modifier ends it with the combat. A spell's `convokedBy` records the creatures that convoked it, by stint; its `SpellSnapshot` carries them, so a copy's "each creature that convoked this spell" is the original's (rule 707.10). `LastKnownRefs.sets.batch` (`object-sets.ts`, 2026-11-08) holds the objects a batched trigger fired on — the cards of a batched `put-into-graveyard` (Colossal Grave-Reaver), the attackers that counted for an `attacks-batch` or `attack-with` trigger, each with its stint — what an aggregate's `in: "trigger-batch"` reads (Aloy, Vulpine Harvester). `PlayerState.attackedWithCommanderOnTurn` answers "during any turn you attacked with a commander". `GrantedAbilityRef`'s `card-activated` names another card's own printed activated ability (a linked exiled land's, granted by Steward of the Harvest; or one a permanent's exchanged text box came with, from `activatedRefFor`), and `card-triggered` its printed triggered ability (one an exchanged text box came with, set as the trigger is detected), so either resolves as that text however its source changes. "For as long as [this] remains on the battlefield" (rule 611.2b): `PtModifier.whileSource` / `ControlEffect.whileSource` name the source and its stint, and `GameState.whileSourceIds` lists the sources such effects are waiting on, so a permanent leaving is checked against a short list rather than every modifier. `GameState.combatDamagePreventedBy` (turn-scoped source filters for prevented combat damage — Arachnogenesis) and `GameObject.skipsNextUntap` ("doesn't untap during its controller's next untap step", following the permanent, not its controller) with `skipsFurtherUntaps` (how many more after that one — Telekinesis's two), and `skipsUntapOf` (the same tied to named players' untap steps, one `{ player, steps }` a player — Sleep's "that player's", Reveka's "your"); overlapping holds run side by side, `Game.holdThroughUntapSteps` keeping the longer. `GameObject.attachedToPlayer` is the player a Curse is attached to (rule 303.4 — "Enchant player"; never set with `attachedTo`, cleared by any zone change, kept in `LastKnownInfo`), and `enterChoice.enchantPlayer` the player an Aura is entering attached to (its spell's player target, or the 303.4f choice); the `choose-enchant` awaiting's `players` are the players such an Aura could enchant. `GameObject.tally` is what a permanent has done this stint — `lifeTaken`, the life its controller's opponents lost to it (damage it dealt, in combat or by an ability whose source it is, and life one of its abilities made them lose), and cards its controller drew while one of its abilities resolved (`state.resolvingSource`, matched by timestamp) — kept by `Game.tallyDamage`/`loseLifeTallied`/`tallyDraw`, cleared by `moveObject` (rule 400.7), with the current turn's share apart in `thisTurn`; `settledTally(object, turn)` is the part from earlier turns, which is all the bots read, so a search's simulated combat can't add to the evidence it weighs; and while a bot decides, `features.ts`'s `withTrackRecordEvidence` (wrapped around `EvalBotController`'s `act`/`declareAttackers`/`declareBlockers`, and around `scenario-fit.ts`'s recording) makes every track record read off the real board, never off a position a rollout reached in a later turn. **Prepare** (rule 722): a permanent's `prepared` designation names the copy of its prepare spell (`CardDefinition.prepareSpell`) that `Game.becomePrepared` made in exile; that copy's `preparedFor` links it back to the permanent in its stint, and `removeStrayCardCopies` spares it only while the link holds (722.3c, an exception to 704.5e). `legalActions` offers it as a `cast-spell` with `via: "prepared"` to the permanent's controller; casting it unprepares the permanent (601.2i) and marks the spell `preparedSpell`. `moveObject` clears all three, and a prepared token never folds into a stack. The view carries `preparedSpell` on the permanent and `preparedBy` on the copy; the client's menu offers the cast from the permanent (`prepare.test.ts`). **Storied** (rule 702.195): `PlayerState.enduringStory`, set for the rest of the game by `Game.grantEnduringStories` in each state-based check (so before triggers are looked for, 702.195c) once a player controls a permanent with the `storied` keyword and three or more artifacts, Sagas and/or legendary permanents; the `enduring-story` static condition reads it, and the view's `PublicPlayerInfo.enduringStory` shows it. **Speed** (rules 702.178–702.179): `PlayerState.speed`, absent while a player has none (702.179b; read as 0, 702.179f, through `speedOf`, with `MAX_SPEED` = 4), set to 1 by `Game.startEngines` in each state-based check once they control a permanent with the `start-your-engines` keyword (704.5aa) and raised by `Game.setSpeed`; the first time it's set, `setSpeed` also makes `PlayerState.speedAbility`, an object read through `cards/speed.ts`'s `Speed` definition (kind `"emblem"`, in the command zone but in no zone list, like an emblem's) carrying the inherent triggered ability of 702.179d — a `loses-life` trigger for opponents with `whileCondition` your turn, the intervening if `{ kind: "speed", atMost: 3 }` and `oncePerTurn`, whose `increase-speed` effect raises it — which `detectTriggers` scans beside the battlefield: a sourceless ability in the rules, on the stack as "Speed". The `speed` static condition (`atLeast`/`atMost`, `who: "that-player"` at a resolution) gates "Max speed —" statics (`helpers.ts`'s `maxSpeed`), the `{ speed: "you" }` amount, cost reduction and `grantPtPerCount.speed` read it, and the view's `PublicPlayerInfo.speed` (`null` for none) shows it. **Mayhem** (rule 702.187): `GameObject.discardedOnTurn` is the turn a card was discarded, stamped in `emit` as `cards-discarded` is announced and cleared by `moveObject` (400.7); `Game.mayhemCastable` reads it, so `legalActions` offers a card with `CardDefinition.mayhem` from its owner's graveyard that turn — a `cast-spell` with `via: "mayhem"` for its mayhem cost (an alternative cost, timing unchanged), or for a cost-less mayhem land a graveyard land play (`mayPlayFromGraveyard`) (`mayhem.test.ts`). The `choose-from-zone` awaiting's `behold` (`{source, x, then?, enterOf?}`) marks a behold (rule 701.4a): its answer goes to `Game.applyBehold`, `destination` `"behold"` or `"exile"`; `enterChoice.reveal` also holds what a behold land beheld. `GameObject.airbended` marks a card airbended into exile (rule 701.65a): its owner may cast it from there for {2} (`via: "airbend"`) for as long as it stays, cleared by `moveObject` like every exile marker. `PlayerEffect.castOnlyFromHand` lists the players who can't cast spells from anywhere but their hands (Avatar's Wrath). **Phasing (rule 702.26):** a phased-out permanent has `zone: "phased-out"` — a `ZoneType` that isn't a zone (702.26d: it changes no zone or control) — and a `GameObject.phasedOut` status (`PhasedOutStatus`: who controlled it as it phased out, `with` for one phased out indirectly with its host (702.26g), `host` for an Aura/Equipment phased out directly (702.26i), `until` for "until [this] leaves the battlefield" (610.4), `released` once its controller has left and their turn would have begun (702.26n), `priorLastKnown`), and sits in `GameState.phasedOut` (`phasedOutIds`) instead of the battlefield's list — so every battlefield read and every "is it still on the battlefield" check passes it over without being told to (702.26b). `PlayerEffect.lifeCantChange` / `protectionFromEverything` are Teferi's Protection's player half (rules 119.7–8, 702.16j). A flip card (rule 710) has the flipped status as `GameObject.flipped` and shows its alternative half through `flippedName`, which `printedCardName` reads first and `Game.refreshFlippedName` recomputes from its copiable values (own card, copy, merged top) whenever it flips or they change. `PlayerState.untapStepsToSkip` counts a player's untap steps still to be skipped whole (rule 614.10 — the `skip-untap-step` effect; `untapStep` skips phasing and untapping, `beginTurn` the day/night check, and a static `skipsSteps` skips them without using one up, 614.10a; `Game.continueUntapStep` decides which permanents go on to untap (rule 502.3, no priority): after "you may choose not to", each `untapOnlyChosenType` as a `choose-modes` (`awaiting.untapTypes`) and each `untapLimit` broken as a `choose-permanents` of exactly its count (`awaiting.untapLimit`, the rest stay tapped, a partial stack's tokens splitting off), `Game.untapRulesOn` reading them from the battlefield and emblems as they stand), `PlayerState.stepsToSkip` the same for upkeep and draw steps and `TurnState.skipsThisTurn` the active player's this turn (the `skip-step` effect; `Game.enterStep` passes a skipped one with a `step-skipped` event and no `step-began`, rule 500.11), and `GameState.extraTurnsSkippingUntap` marks the queued extra turns whose untap step goes (Savor the Moment), by their index in the `extraTurns` stack. `PtModifier.grantsStatic` (a `grant-static`'s ability, read through `grantedStatics`) and a `restrict`'s `cant-be-blocked` record (a `restrict`'s record, a rule rather than an ability); a rule over the game can carry a `cant-be-blocked` record too (B253). `GameObject.chosenPlayer` is "the chosen player" — chosen as it entered (`enterChoice.chosenPlayer`, from `CardDefinition.choosePlayerOnEnter`, asked through `choose-creature-type` with `choosePlayer`) or kept by `choose-opponent { remember }`; cleared on a zone change, kept in `lastKnown`, shown as `VisibleObject.chosenPlayer` (`GameObject.secretChoice`, `{ by, revealed? }`, is a choice made secretly: `viewFor` leaves `chosen` and `chosenPlayer` out of every view but `by`'s, spectators included, marking the permanent `VisibleObject.secretChoice`; only the `secret-choice-made` and `secret-choice-revealed` events name it, and only `AbilityCost.revealChosen`, payable by `by` alone, reveals it; cleared on a zone change), read by the `"chosen-player"` scope (a resolution's alone: `scopedPlayers` names nobody), trigger subject, `maxHandSize` and `CardFilter.attackingChosenPlayer`. `GameObject.chosenPermanent` is "the chosen creature" — `{ id, stint }`, chosen as it entered (`enterChoice.chosenPermanent`, from `CardDefinition.choosePermanentOnEnter`, asked through `choose-permanents` with `permanentOnEnter`) or kept by `choose-permanents { remember }` as an ability resolves (`rememberChosenPermanent`, which also emits a `chosenOnly` `cards-chosen-from-zone`), kept in `lastKnown`, matched by the trigger subject `who: "chosen"` in its stint, read by the `"chosen"` `ObjectRef` and a static's `{ scope: "chosen" }` only while that object is in the stint it was chosen in (rule 400.7); a chosen token is pinned against refolding. **Outside the game** (rule 400.11, 2026-10-30): `ZoneType`'s `"outside"` isn't a zone — a sideboard card's status (400.11a), the object kept by owner in `GameState.outside`, as `"phased-out"` objects are kept in `phasedOut`; `zoneList` reads both. `GameObject.melded` (rules 701.42, 712.4 — 2026-10-31): a melded permanent, one object for two cards, its own and `with`'s; `faceName` returns its combined back face `into`, so `printedCardName`, the view and the log see that face. `leftWith` names the other cards a merged or melded permanent became as it left the battlefield (712.21, 730.3c), cleared on its next zone change. Face down by an effect (rule 708.2 — 2026-10-31): a fifth `FaceDownKind`, `"effect"` (put or turned face down by an effect that neither manifests nor cloaks; turned face up only by a morph or disguise cost, 702.37e), and `GameObject.faceDown.as`, a `FaceDownAs` — the characteristics the effect listed ("2/2 Cyberman artifact creature", "5/5 artifact creature", "Forest land"), each an internal definition in `cards/face-down.ts` named in `FACE_DOWN_AS_CARDS`; `faceDownCardName` is the one read of a face-down object's registry name (`printedCardName`, `Game.faceDef`). `exiledFaceDown.group` (2026-10-31): the face-down group a card exiled face down is in (what exiled it and when — the event sequence number), and `choose-from-zone`'s `faceDownGroups`: face-down exiled candidates a processor picks by group, each pick carried out on a random card of its group. `choose-from-zone`'s `searchInstead` and `searchRest` (2026-10-31): a search whose finds go card by card picks them first (`destination: "stay"`, no shuffle), and its answer raises a second choice over the finds whose picks go to the `instead` destination while `searchRest` sends the others to the search's own and names the library to shuffle after. `blockedAttackersOf` (moved from `combat/banding.ts`, 2026-10-31) and `LastKnownInfo.blockedAttackers`/`blockers` — what a creature was blocking and blocked by as it left, for `CardFilter.blockingRelation`. An attack requirement narrowed (2026-10-31, now a `must-attack` record): `object` (one creature by stint), `controlledBy`, `attacks` (a player or planeswalker, `attacksStint`), `duration` (`this-turn`, `next-turn-of` with `started`, `next-combat-of` with `after`), read by `attackRequirementActive`; `Game` starts and ends them as turns begin and as combat ends (`removeAllFromCombat`). `EnterAttachHost` is what a card put onto the battlefield "attached to" something enters attached to — a permanent, `null`, `{ player }` or `{ among }` (a host chosen as it enters: `enterChoice.attachAmong`, an Equipment's answer `enterChoice.attachHost`, raised as a `choose-permanents` with `attachOnEnter`). `GameState.resolvingController` (who controls what's resolving, across its decisions), `PlayerEffect.cantSearchLibraries`/`ignoresStatic`, `choose-from-zone`'s `searchForbidden`. `GameState.permissionGroups` (`{ id, left }`): plays shared among the cards one effect gave a permission, named by `graveyardCastPermission.group` and `impulse.group` (Locke, Treasure Hunter; Unlucky Witness).
- **`game.ts`** — `tapCostOffer` offers what the mana plan taps as well, where any `count` of the choices still leaves the mana payable (601.2h; `everySubsetHolds`). A spell payable only with what two of its targets take off says so in `reducingTargetsAtLeast` (`earnersNeeded`); `targetReductionOptions` narrows the first of several earning slots only where the slots are interchangeable (`targetSlotsInterchangeable`). `flashbacksOf(card, face)` gives each flashback's cost for the face cast, and the offer walks a split card's halves (709.3); a grant "equal to its mana cost" is `grantedFlashback.ownManaCost`, read then. `TargetSource.asCastFace` (`cardSource`) and `withBestowed` read a card being cast as the face or bestowed Aura it will be; `oneTokenSpared` is the token an "all other" effect leaves of a stack.
- **`effects.ts` / `duration.ts` / `state.ts`** — a delayed "whenever" about another player (2026-11-11, Nuka-Nuke Launcher): `DelayedWhenever.byPlayer` is resolved into the watch's `player` (which `fireWheneverWatchers` now also holds a `spell-cast` event's caster to, beside `toPlayer`'s damaged player), and `WatchDuration` takes `{ throughTurnOf }`, kept as a `WatchExpiry` word (`"until-end-of-your-next-turn"`) with `DelayedWheneverWatch.turnOf`, the player whose turn `beginTurn`'s `beginningFor` is asked about in place of the controller.
- **`cards/define.ts` / `game.ts`** — the `"read-ahead"` keyword (2026-11-11, Barbara Wright's "Sagas you control have read ahead"): a static's `grantKeywords`, never printed (a Saga's own is `readAhead`). `nextEnterChoice` asks a Saga with no printed choice for its chapter when `enteringKeywordInstances` finds a grant that will reach it (614.12, 702.155b), the entry puts that many lore counters on it, and `triggerChapters` reads the keyword off the permanent the turn it entered (702.155a). The client's three keyword tables show it with storied's Saga mark.
- **`game.ts`** — `modesCostSacrificePlan` (2026-11-11): a `may`'s `costSacrifice` ("you may pay {2} and sacrifice a token", Killer Service) is one cost with its mana (rule 118.3). The `choose-modes` decision keeps it as `costSacrifice` (`ModesCostSacrifice`: filter, count, the "another" object); `beginModesChoice` declines without asking, and `applyModesChoice` takes no payment, unless the mana can be planned with the sacrifice's candidates last (`payMana`'s `last`) and enough of them are left after it (`costSacrificeLeftAfter`). The sacrifice is a `sacrifice` effect put ahead of the `may`'s own, so which permanent is the ordinary `sacrifice` decision.
- **`game.ts`** — `counter`'s `into: "battlefield"` (2026-11-11, Desertion): `counterSpellByEffect` asks the card's "as this enters" choices of the countering player first (614.12; the `counter` step runs again once answered), then `counterObject` puts it onto the battlefield under that player through `putOntoBattlefieldByEffect`, which passes `EnterOptions.notResolving` for any card it takes off the stack. `moveObjectUncached` carries a spell's cast facts (kicked, X, mana spent, `entry.cast`, bestow, a Room's cast half) onto the permanent only while `resolving`; a card put there any other way is a new object that was never cast (400.7, 107.3i). One that can't enter is countered into the graveyard (an Aura with no host by the move itself, 303.4g).
- **`game.ts`** — `xPlanFor` caps X at a card's `maxX` (read through `costReductionAmount` as X is chosen). A card's `minX` ("X can't be 0", Mind Grind) is the floor of the X offered (`castSpellActions`' `leastX`) and a refusal in `whyCannotCastSpell`, which also keeps it from any cast that pays no {X} (rule 107.3b). `beginZoneChoice`'s `{ exiled, looked }` zone (`look-and-choose`'s `"exiled-this-way"`, Scroll Rack): the chooser picks a face-down card itself rather than a group's at random (`exileChoiceCandidates`' `looked`), and one card going back on top isn't asked. A commander picked from exile for a library is asked 903.9b before anything moves, as from a hand; `putPickOnLibrary` carries out the answer without asking again. `announceUnblockedAttackers` also emits `player-attacked-unblocked` for each player with unblocked creatures attacking them directly. `interveningIfMet` and a resolving `conditional` hand conditions an `amounts` reader (`filterAmounts`, `signed`) for the `amounts` kind, and the asking ability's per-turn key (`ConditionOptions.abilityKey` — `abilityTurnKey` on the stack, `triggerTurnKey` as it triggers) for `this-ability-did-this-turn`, which reads the journal's `done` entries — added to by the resolution context's `addMana` for an `add-mana` with `marksAbilityDone` that added at least one mana (rule 107.1b), cleared as a turn begins (Carpet of Flowers' "if you haven't added mana with this ability this turn"). `transformPermanent` applies the new face's `asTransformsInto` (rule 712.20) before it emits the transform, and sets the `chosenPlayer` a face with `choosePlayerOnTransform` was given (the `transform` effect asks it first, through the ctx's `transformChoice`; `effectCanTransform` holds the checks both share). The `player-loses-game` trigger matches `player-lost`, emitted before `leaveGame`. `changeLife` asks `lifeLossReplaced` of every loss (`would-lose-life`, Bloodletter of Aclazotz). `tappedForManaExtras` reads a delayed `tapped-for-mana` trigger too (`abilities.ts`'s `isDelayedManaAbility`, rule 605.1b — High Tide), which `fireWheneverWatchers` leaves off the stack. `computeActivatedGrantSources` and `triggeredGrantSources` read an emblem’s grants as well (each its own ref: no definition holds it), and every `grantsToSpells` reader asks `emblemStatics` too. `leaveStackAfterResolving` reads `intoHandAsItResolves` (Soulfire Grand Master, set by `spendNextSpellGrants` from a `next` grant) ahead of Feather’s `exileAsItResolves`, with the same rule 616.1 choice, answered through `finishExiledAsItResolves`; `returnToHand` takes `now` (Hanabi Blast): a spell returning itself at that point of its instructions rather than as the final part. `announcedModal` reads a chapter's ability too, and `placeTriggerOnStack` picks a `random` announced modal's mode itself (seeded, among the modes it could have, unless `chooseIf` holds), then places it as though chosen.`dealCombatAssignments` records each source dealing combat damage to a player in the journal's `damage` entries (the `combat-damaged-by` word), with its snapshot then (`was`). `refsAtTrigger`: an entry's "that player" is who the permanent entered under, kept for the ability's life whoever controls it by then (Archaeomancer's Map's "if that player controls more lands than you"). A mana ability from another zone (`isManaAbility` no longer asks where, rule 605.1a; `manaSources` leaves those out, so they're activated by hand) and one paid by `exileFromGraveyard` or `collectEvidence`, its choice's `thenManaAbility` adding the mana once answered (605.3b); `ActivatedAbility.exilesFromHand` is a hand ability's "Exile this card from your hand". `tapOthersCandidates` reads its filter with the source, so `{ hostOfSource: true }` is an Aura's "Tap enchanted creature". A cast remembers the one permanent its cost tapped (`LastKnownRefs.tapped`, `SpellSnapshot.tappedCost` for a copy, `tappedLost` once it's gone), the `ObjectRef` `"tapped"`. Offering (rule 702.48) is a keyword kicker: `castSpellActionsAlone` adds `castSpellVariants`' kicked offers once per permanent and reduction under `withOffering`, whose `offeringProbe` `castingCostOf` and `castRouteSacrifice` read; `whyCannotCastSpell` and `castSpell` enter it from the action's `sacrifice` and `costOption`. `costModificationFor` also sums `increaseColored`'s pips, which `castingCostOf` adds before any reduction (rule 601.2f) and `displayCostOf` shows after the printed symbols. `castAlsoCosts` is every non-mana cost a cast adds in `KickerAlsoCost`'s shape: its kicker's, a spliced card's, and its own `additionalCost.also` and the chosen option's, so offers, checks and payment read one list; `spellTapCost` turns them into the one tap offer, and `object.xValue` takes a `tapAnyNumber`'s count. Durations to a point in a later turn: `lasting` reads a `PointDuration` into an `UntilPoint` (`untilPointOf`), kept on a modifier or a control effect (`gainControlByEffect`'s `untilPoint`), and `passUntilPoints` is their one expiry, called from `beginTurn`, `untapStep`, `enterStep("upkeep")` and `endStep`, none of them for a skipped step (614.10a). `permissionWindow`'s `until-begins` is the same moment for a permission over an exiled card. Exiled face down with no permission: `exileFromLibrary`'s `faceDown` sets `exiledFaceDown` with `lookersOnly`, which `shareLinkedLooks` (a hideaway's controller's look) skips, and `turnExiledFaceUp` clears it on the source's linked cards. Permissions over exiled cards: `exilePermissionGrant` turns an `ExilePermission` (`exile`'s and a hand exile's `mayPlay`, `look-and-choose`'s `mayPlay`) into `GameObject.playPermission`, its owner's with `byOwner`, its `tax` read in `castingCostOf` beside a linked `mayPlay`' `reduce`, its `landsEnterTapped` carried on the land play's `PendingEntry`; `spendPlay` spends one a linked `mayPlay` play as a spell is cast or a land played (not an `anyNumber`), and `linkExiledToSource` is every effect's "exile it with this" (rule 607.2a). Changes of control: `control-changed` names its `previousController`, `controlLookedBack` reads a `loses-control` or `control-taken` source as its controller just before (603.10d), `withControlBatch` keeps that for permanents changing hands at once, and `fireLoseControlWatchers` answers a delayed "when you lose control of it" (`DelayedLoseControlWatch`). A "prevent that damage and …" follow-up that finds a decision outstanding parks behind it (two Khârns). `turnFaceUp` takes the X of a morph or disguise cost (`morphCostNow` reads `morphCostIncrease` and `morph.costReduction`, `morphCostShown` what the offer says); `plotCostNow` takes its controller's statics' `plotCostReduction` off a plot cost's generic mana, for the `plot` offer, its check and its payment (Doc Aurlock); `takeLastKnown` records `attachments` (`attachedNow`: each permanent attached, in its stint); the resolution context's `doubleTriggerSpellX` doubles the trigger spell's `xValue` while it's that spell on the stack (`double-x`, Unbound Flourishing), and an `activates-ability` trigger's `xInCost` asks for {X} in the activation cost's mana, a `reveal` part through `beginBehold`, and the `permanent-turned-face-up` event carries the X for `triggerValueOf`. A spell's `for-each-unless` holds choices for its whole run (`heldChoices`, now with `returns`; `holdDiscard` merges one player's discards), asks one `unless` an object with the offer's `aboutObject`, and releases at the end; `beginDiscardChosen` takes an exact count (`discard-chosen`'s `picks` without `reveals`) and one card per filter (`oneEach`: a `one-each` set rule, `largestOneEach` the number to choose); `additionalCost.returnToHandX` (Infernal Harvest) is an X the mana cost needn't have, through the same `xCost` path, queued as an `also`'s `returnToHand` once X is known; `additionalCost.exileFromGraveyard` takes `count: "x"` (offered through the same `xCost` path as "discard X", capped in `xPlanFor` beside a `{X}`), `optional` and `linked` (`payGraveyardExileCost`), an ability's `count: "opponents"`; a copy keeps what its original's cost took (`SpellSnapshot.paidCost`, rule 707.10). Ending the turn or the combat phase (rule 724): `beginEndingTurnOrCombat` empties the waiting triggers and exiles the stack as the effect happens, the resolving object last as it leaves (`leaveStackAfterResolving` reads `GameState.endingPhase`), and `prepareForPriority`, once state-based actions and anything they asked are done, calls `finishEndingTurnOrCombat`: to the cleanup step with the turn's owed phases gone, or on through `endStep` as if end of combat had ended. The attack declaration records its attackers' total power as declared (each attacker's journal entry, written before any `attacker-declared`; pack tactics' `TurnStat`). A graveyard is a list whose end is the top of the pile (rule 404.2): `withGraveyardEnterBatch` queues `pendingGraveyardOrders` for each owner of two or more simultaneous arrivals (rule 404.3), only while some card in the game reads the order (`readsGraveyardOrder`), and `prepareForPriority` asks with a `choose-from-zone` `order` carrying `arrangesGraveyard`. `Game` owns the single mutable `GameState` and is the **only** writer. `dispatch(action)` handles every `Action` variant (see `actions.ts`). `canDispatch(action)` says why one couldn't be dispatched now, or `null` — a cast's targets judged as `castSpell` judges them (`whyCastTargetsInvalid`). After each, it answers any trigger's "you may" or choice of modes a standing "same for all" covers (`GameState.standingModeAnswers`): a `choose-modes` raised as a triggered ability resolves carries `trigger` (its `triggerSignature` — controller, card name and ability index, the granting static, or a resolved effect's granted ability by its text and effect — and the identical triggers still under it, offered as `sameForAll`); answering with `forAll` records the answer, which `standingAnswer` replays for each of those as it resolves while nothing new has gone on the stack (a shortcut, rules 732.2a–b). That's a yes-or-no or a choice among modes (not a vote, an amount of energy, or a question asked once per player or card), whoever is asked — the later players of an "each player may" too — and also a question asked after another decision in the same resolution: a parked `SuspendedResolution` carries `resolvingTrigger` and restores it as the rest runs (2026-11-11). Only the answer is shared — each trigger chose its own targets going on the stack. A 3-4 player table queues attacked defenders in `pendingBlockerDeclarations`, drained one `declare-blockers` at a time.
