`legalActions` offers a `via: "plot"` cast on a later turn, free-only like a free `cast-now` and always at sorcery timing. **Crimes** (rule 700.13): `commitCrimeIfAny`, called beside `announceTargeted` only as a spell is cast, an ability activated or a trigger put on the stack (never for a copy or changed targets), counts `PlayerState.crimesThisTurn` and emits `crime-committed` for the `crime-committed` trigger and `committed-crime-this-turn` condition. `enteringCounterAmount` answers an enters-with amount's `ifCondition` with the entering batch left out (`notYetHere`, rule 614.12), as `tappedUnless` is. **Rad counters** (rule 728): a `PlayerCounterKind`; `changePlayerCounters` makes a player's `radAbility` object (like `speedAbility`, `cards/rad.ts`) on their first one, which the trigger scan adds beside speed's. **The monarch** (rule 725): `setMonarch` makes the one `monarchAbility` object (`cards/monarch.ts` — the end-step draw and the combat-damage steal, 725.2) the first time anyone becomes the monarch and keeps its controller the current monarch (`ensureMonarchAbility`, also run as each step begins for a state that set `monarch` directly); the trigger scan adds it while there is a monarch, so both abilities use the stack. **The initiative and dungeons** (rules 726, 309, 701.49 — `docs/plans/initiative-dungeons.md`): `takeInitiative` sets `GameState.initiative` and keeps the `initiativeAbility` object (`cards/initiative.ts` — its three abilities: venture into Undercity on taking it and at the holder's upkeep, and the combat-damage steal, a `deals-damage-batch` with `to: "you"`) controlled by the holder, emitting `initiative-taken` even on a retake (726.5); it passes like the monarch when its holder leaves (726.4). `ventureInto` enters a dungeon (`beginEnterDungeon` — Undercity for "venture into Undercity", else a choice among the others), moves along an arrow (asking the `venture` decision at a fork), or completes the dungeon from its bottommost room and enters another; `enterDungeon` makes the dungeon's object (kind `"emblem"`, command zone, no zone list — `cards/dungeons.ts`'s `DUNGEON_DEFINITIONS`) and `enterRoom` queues the room's ability as a pending trigger carrying its own record (`reflexive`), sourced from the dungeon and controlled by its owner; `completeFinishedDungeons` is rule 309.6's state-based action. All four dungeons are defined (Lost Mine of Phandelver, Dungeon of the Mad Mage, Tomb of Annihilation — what a plain venture chooses among — and Undercity), each room pinned against `data/oracle/dungeons.json` by `dungeons.test.ts`; the Mad Wizard's Lair is a `draw`, a `reveal-drawn` and a `cast-now` `from: "drawn-this-way"`, the Oubliette a `sacrifice` with `andEach`. **Madness** (702.35): every discard — an effect's, a cost's, cycling's, channel's, the cleanup step's, a held 101.4 discard's release — moves its cards through `discardCards`/`moveDiscarded`, which exiles a card with `madness` instead and queues its trigger (`cards/madness.ts`: a `cast-now` of the card for its madness cost, the offer's `madness`, else `put-into-graveyard`). **Miracle** (702.94): `drawCardRaw`'s first draw of a turn calls `queueMiracle`, which queues the card's trigger (`cards/miracle.ts`: a `cast-now` of the card, the offer's `miracle`, only from hand) unrevealed, with the card's stint in hand (`PendingTrigger.miracle`, set as the stack object's `sourceZoneChangeCount` so a card that left the hand is `sourceLost`); `askMiracleReveal` asks the `miracle-reveal` decision as it would be placed. `PlayerState.dungeon` / `completedDungeons` and the view's `dungeon` (with its `DungeonMap`), `completedDungeons` and `initiative` carry it to the client. **Squad** (702.157): a `multi` kicker with `keyword: "squad"`; `sourceTimesKicked` (the permanent's `enteredTimesKicked`, kept in `LastKnownInfo`) is `create-token-copy`'s `perTimeKicked`. **Explore** (701.44): the `explore` effect reveals through the context's `exploreReveal` (a land to hand, the explorer read as it last was if it left), adds the counter only to a live explorer, asks the may as a `sequence`, and its engine-built `done` step emits `explored` (the `explores` trigger, the explorer its trigger object). **Discover** (701.57): `discover`/`finishDiscover` beside `cascade` — exile until a nonland card of mana value N or less, a free `cast-now` (spell mana value N or less), the card to hand if not cast, the rest to the bottom, then `discovered` (`discovers` triggers; the trigger value is N). **Craft** (702.167): `AbilityCost.craft` beside `exileSelf` — `canCraft` (candidates from the battlefield and the graveyard, token stacks counting each token, `share`/`oneEach` feasibility), `beginCraft` (one `choose-from-zone` over both zones, stacks split, the materials linked through `exileLink` to the card's exile stint), and the `craft-return` effect, which returns the card transformed only from that stint (refs.source + 1) and moves the materials' `exiledWith` to the permanent it became, waiting first for an "as this enters" choice. **Explore each** (701.44d): `explore`'s `each` lists the explorers once (`exploreCandidates`, token stacks split) and walks an engine-built `remaining` list, a one-mode `modal` choosing who goes next. **Grants to cards being cast or in a graveyard**: `grantedCastCost` (via `castGrantReaches`, the card judged where it is) gives `freerunningCostOf`/`webSlingingCostOf`; `mayhemCostOf` and `dredgeOf` read `grantsToGraveyard`'s `mayhem`/`dredge` beside the card's own; `graveyardKeywordsOf` reads its `keywords` (a permanent's or an emblem's) and a card's `grantedGraveyardKeywords` (the `grant-graveyard-keyword` one-shot, until end of turn), for `graveyardGrantedActivated` (unearth, encore, embalm, built by the printed helpers and listed after the card's own, as `handGrantedActivated` does for ninjutsu and granted cycling, which emits `card-cycled` as it's activated), `castKeywordCosts` (harmonize, sneak, each named by `graveyardGrant.source` among several) and `hasRetrace`; a card foretold through `grantsForetellInHand` keeps `foretoldCost`; `costModificationFor` honours `castFrom` (the card's zone, or `castFrom` once on the stack). **Loops** (104.4b, 732.3): `dispatchOne` notes an answer given inside a chain in the loop trail rather than resetting the count; `noteMandatoryResolution`'s first repeating cycle with answers in it sets `GameState.loopBreak` (those players' "you may" answered no by `loopBreakingAnswer`, their standing answers cleared), and a cycle that repeats anyway, or has no answers, is a draw. `canDispatch` judges an activation's chosen targets (`whyTargetsInvalid`). `sacrifice-all-but` is a per-player keep choice (`askKeep`, `keepChoice` on the `choose-permanents` decision, strongest listed first) then `sacrificeAllExcept`; `for-each-player` takes `simultaneous`. **Zone-choice rules** (`zone-choice-together.ts`): beside `share`, `one-each` and `total`, `distinct` (no two chosen share a tag — a search's `distinctNames`) and `variety` (at least `atLeast` tags among them); `return-from-graveyard`'s `from: "hand-or-graveyard"` offers both zones in one choice, its `count` an amount read as it resolves (Worldsoul's Rage's "up to X").
- **`actions.ts` / `decisions/choose-from-zone.ts`** (2026-11-11) — the `choose-from-zone` action's `search`: set when the choice is a library search's (the awaiting decision's `leftover` is `"shuffle"`, or it `shufflesLater`), so a client may sort the finds and show identical cards as one. A look at the top of a library never has it: there the order is information.
- **`actions.ts`** — `CastVia`'s `"more-than-meets-the-eye"` casts a transforming card converted (`face: 1`, rules 702.162a, 712.11a) for its front's `moreThanMeetsTheEye` cost (`Game.moreThanMeetsTheEyeCostOf`), from the hand or the command zone; `whyCannotCastSpell` refuses any other cast of a transforming card's back face but disturb's and an effect's.`TapCostOffer.perExtraMode` is escalate's tap (rule 702.120a — Collective Effort): `count` for each mode chosen beyond the first, counted by `tapCountForModes` (exported through `engine/client`) once the modes are in. `TapCostOffer.anyNumber` is "tap any number of untapped creatures": `count` 0, any picks, none too; `defaultTapPicks` then takes them all. the `Action` union: the priority actions, plus `declare-attackers`/`declare-blockers`/`assign-combat-damage`/`discard`/`choose-from-zone`/`mulligan`/`put-on-bottom`/`commander-replacement`/`choose-copy`/`choose-enchant`/`legend-rule`/`choose-modes`/`choose-targets`/`cast-now`/`sacrifice`/`scry`/`proliferate`/`suspend`/`foretell`/`cycle`/`turn-face-up` (a face-down permanent's special action, rule 701.40b: offered to its controller while its card is a creature card whose mana cost they can pay, beside its abilities; the bots weigh it as a play). `cast-spell` carries optional `xValue`, `via?: CastVia` (`"flashback"|"escape"|"foretell"|"disturb"|"adventure"|"suspend"|"cascade"|"impulse"|"library-top"|"warp"|"blitz"|"harmonize"|"aftermath"|"effect"|"graveyard-permission"` — a fused split spell (rule 702.102, `CardDefinition.fuse`) has none — it is the whole card's `face: 0`, offered only from a hand and resolved half by half; `"aftermath"` is the graveyard cast of a split card's aftermath half, rule 702.127a (`CardDefinition.aftermath`; that half is refused from any other zone, and `aftermathExiles` exiles it whenever it would leave the stack once cast from a graveyard); `"impulse"` is any cast from exile under a permission, one riding on the card (`GameObject.impulse`) or a `playFromExile` static's (Grolnok, the Omnivore); `"library-top"` is a cast off the top of the caster's library under a `castFromLibraryTop` static (Glarb, Calamity's Augur); `"warp"` is the hand cast for a warp cost, rule 702.185, whose permanent is exiled at the next end step by a delayed trigger and castable again from exile through an impulse permission gated by `fromTurn`; `"blitz"` is the hand or command-zone cast for a blitz cost, rule 702.152a, whose permanent gets haste and a dies-draw on a non-copiable modifier and a delayed end-step sacrifice; `"harmonize"` is the graveyard cast for a harmonize cost, rule 702.180a, one offer per creature it could tap — `harmonizeTap: {object, cardName, power}`, echoed as `tap: [object]` — priced with that power off through `Game.harmonizePower`, and exiled as it leaves the stack; `"web-slinging"` is the hand or command-zone cast for a web-slinging cost, rule 702.188a, under normal timing, one offer per tapped creature the caster controls — `webSlingingReturn: {object, cardName}`, echoed as the action's `webSlingingReturn`), `face?`, `modes?`, `kicked?`, `prototype?` (rule 718: cast with the prototype cost, colors and size — a copiable `PtModifier` with `setManaCost`/`setColors`/`setPt`, applied while its legality and cost are worked out (`withPrototype`/`applyPrototype`), put on as it moves to the stack, kept onto the battlefield by `moveObject`, and copied onto a copy of the spell), `overload?`, `evoke?` + `evokeCost?` (rule 702.74: cast for an evoke cost, printed or granted — `grantsEvokeToSpells` — one offer per distinct evoke cost; the permanent gets its sacrifice trigger as it enters, placed ahead of its controller's other triggers of the moment by `TriggeredAbility.stackFirst` so its enters abilities resolve first), `free?` (a `CardDefinition.freeCastIf` permission), `delve?: ObjectId[]` (graveyard cards exiled for delve, each paying {1} of the total cost — rule 702.66; offered as the cast's `delve: { choices, minCards, maxCards, byX?, fromHand? }`, `fromHand` being a March's "exile any number of blue cards from your hand, {2} less for each": `additionalCost.exileFromHandReduces`, its cards first in `choices`, named on the same `delve`), `convoke?: ConvokePayment[]` (the creatures that convoke, a token stack named once per token; what each `pays` is optional and worked out by the engine when omitted; convoke pays for `{X}` too, and `convokeProofFor(offer, x)` fits an offer's proved payment to the X a driver picked), `sacrifice?` (an additional cost), `tap?` (what an alternative cost's "tap N untapped …" taps — Sephara), `escapeExile?` (which other graveyard cards an escape cast exiles, from the offer's `escapeExile: {count, choices}`; omitted, the oldest); `cast-spell` and `play-land` carry `graveyardGrant?: {source, asType?}` — which graveyard permission a card played from a graveyard spends (a grantor permanent, or the card itself for a one-shot "you may cast that card this turn"; Muldrotha's per-type `asType`), one `LegalAction` variant per permission that applies; `activate-ability` carries optional `sacrifice`, `xValue`, `tap` (an `AbilityCost.tapOthers` choice) and `manaColors` (a mana ability tapped by hand: one pick per unit whose type is the player's — the ability's own, then each triggered mana ability's extra with a choice in it, Mirari's Wake off a Karoo; `Game.standaloneManaVariants` offers one variant per outcome, labelled with all it makes, a `tap-for-mana` multiplier included). `LegalAction` mirrors this — a castable/activatable option carries whatever context the client needs to build the action (`xCost: {maxX, minX?, maxXByTargetCount?, minXByTargetCount?}`, `targetCount: {min, max, copies?}` — the distinct-target counts (a token stack named in several slots counting once per slot, up to its size) a "for each target" cost (Hinata, or the spell's own `costPerExtraTarget` — Fireball) is affordable at, which drivers fit their picks into with `fitTargetCount`/`fitCastTargets`; with an `{X}` the two trade off, `maxXByTargetCount` holding the largest X at each count (`maxXForTargets` for a driver that picks targets first, `targetCountAtX` for one that picks X first); a spell's `any-number` group tied to X (`max: "x"` / `min: "x"` — Pest Infestation, Curse of the Swine) gets the same `targetCount` with `minXByTargetCount` beside it (`Game.xGroupOffer`, `minXForTargets`), and a spell whose target filter reads X (Stolen by the Fae) is offered once per X with `minX` = `maxX` and that X's `targetOptions`, as an activated ability is; a multikicker variant carries `kickCount` (`kicker.multi`, rule 702.33c), and escalate (`castModal.costPerExtraMode`, rule 702.120a) caps `castModal.maxModes` at what's payable, and spree (each mode's `spreeCost`, rule 702.172a) lists the sets of modes the variant can pay for, each mode with a legal target, as `castModal.modeSets` (`spreeModeSets`; a variant with none isn't offered), its modes' `cost` for labelling — `castCostString` takes the chosen modes and adds escalate's and spree's costs on top of any alternative or free cost —, `sacrifice: {choices}`, `tapCost: {count, choices, copies?, totalPower?, power?, creatureTypes?}` — "Tap X untapped artifacts" (`tapOthers.count: "x"`) offered once per X with that X's `count`, `creatureTypes` for "that share a creature type" (`shareACreatureType`, `tapShareChoices`); crew's form taps any number whose `power` adds up to `totalPower` — each creature's power, or what a `crewsAs` static makes it for a crew or saddle cost; `tapPicksPower` sums a set of picks and `defaultTapPicks` is the engine's own pick for a driver that names none, summoning-sick first, `castModal {...}`, `mustBlock`, etc.), and a card playable multiple ways (kicked/unkicked, multiple faces, via different alt-zones) is enumerated once per variant. Kept out of `game.ts` to avoid a cycle with `controller.ts`. `activate-ability` and `choose-targets` carry `division` (an ability's divided amount, as `cast-spell` does), and their offers `divide`. A spree spell's `castModal` offer (rule 702.172a) carries `modeSets`, the sets of modes it can be cast with, and each mode's `cost`; `fitModeSet(modeSets, wanted)` is how a driver picks the largest of them made of the modes it wants, and every driver (the random controller, `randomCast`, v1's `usableModes`, v2's `castCandidates`, the client's mode chooser) casts only one of those sets. A `castModal` offer with `repeatModes` (rule 700.2d, the Confluences) takes a mode more than once: `fillModes(usable, min, max, repeat)` is the drivers' "as many as allowed", taking the usable modes again in turn. `kickerKeyword` is `"offspring"` or `"gift"` (the promised variant, rule 702.174), or another kicker-shaped keyword's name — `"bargain"` (702.166, `kickerCost` empty) among them. A `choose-from-zone` offer's `forPlayer` names whose the chosen cards are when the chooser picks for someone else (Tasigur, the Golden Fang). `choose-from-zone`'s `destination` may be `"exile"` (a cost's). A `choose-from-zone` offer's `behold: true` / `destination: "behold"` is a behold over hand and battlefield at once (rule 701.4a), and `kickerKeyword: "behold"` labels the beholding cast with `kickerCost` its `text` ("a Dragon"). `CastVia` `"airbend"` casts an airbended card from exile for {2} (rule 701.65a); the client's exile viewer labels it "Cast (airbend)" from the `via` alone. `waterbend?: ObjectId[]` on `cast-spell` and `activate-ability` is what taps to pay a waterbend cost (rule 701.67a — a stack named once per token; `[]` pays it all with mana, omitted the engine taps the offer's `proof`), offered as the variant's `waterbend: WaterbendOffer` (`choices`, `copies?`, `max`, `proof`, `byX?` — `waterbendAt(offer, x)` reads one X's); `kickerKeyword` may be `"waterbend"`.- **`view.ts`** — `viewFor(state, registry, viewer, opts)` / `game.viewFor(player)` → a `PlayerView`: your hand in full, other hands as counts, libraries as counts (except what a pending `choose-from-zone`/`scry`, or a `cast-now`'s `looked`, reveals to that player — and a pending `cast-now`'s `cards`/`offers` are emptied for every other viewer, since they can name a hand or library), every exiled id's owner in `zones.exileOwners` (a face-down card has no object for most seats, but whose it is stays public, rule 406.3), each player's top library card in `revealedLibraryTop` when a permanent of theirs reveals it to all (`revealsOwnLibraryTop` — Oracle of Mul Daya) and, in the viewer's own view only, when one of theirs lets them look at it any time (`looksAtOwnLibraryTop` — Glarb, Calamity's Augur; rule 401.5; not while it has lost its abilities), battlefield/stack/graveyards public with **computed** characteristics baked in (power/toughness/keywords/types/subtypes/colors/restrictions/loyalty/`isToken`/`attachedTo`/`isCommander`/`xValue`/`copyOf`/`suspended`/`foretold`/`faceName`+`faces`/`stackCount`/`art`/`regenerationShields`/`toxic` (the total toxic value, rule 702.164b, granted ones included), and `spellFace`: an adventurer's Adventure or an omen card's Omen, cost, types and text, while the card shows its creature face off the battlefield; and `splitCosts`: a split card's two halves' costs while it's the whole card off the battlefield), so a client needs neither the registry nor the layer system. `effectiveManaCost` is what a card costs whoever could cast it, when that isn't the printed cost (tax left to the client's own badge: `commanderTax`, through `ViewOptions.commanderTax`, the tax still owed now — a reduction past the printed generic part pays the tax down too, rule 601.2f, so a commander with affinity owes less than 2 per cast): for the viewer's own hand, and for every commander in the command zone priced for its owner — public, as the zone, the tax and the reducers all are — through `ViewOptions.effectiveCost(cardId, caster)`, which `Game.viewFor` supplies; never an opponent's hand. `subtypes` is the type line's: without `subtypes.ts`'s every-type markers. `SPECTATOR` is a viewer no seat is: every hand as a count, nothing a decision reveals (a server's spectators, `spectate-room`). A foretold card in exile is redacted for everyone but its owner, and a card exiled face down by an effect (`GameObject.exiledFaceDown`, rule 406.3) for everyone but the players it names — for a hideaway card (rule 702.75a), its exiler and everyone who has controlled the permanent it's linked to since (`Game.shareLinkedLooks`, on each `control-changed`). The view also carries `publicStints` (`GameState.publicStints`, kept by `Game.moveObject`'s `trackPublicity`): when each object's identity was known to every player, from entering a public zone or being revealed — a token or a copy of a spell from the event that announces it (`Game.emit`), so a line still names it once it has ceased to exist — until it moves on from a hidden zone, its library is shuffled or it's turned face down (foretold, or exiled face down — a stint the move itself opened is dropped, not closed, so the card is never named). The history reads it through `publicNameAt` to name old lines, never the current view's hidden objects. **`state.revealedThisTurn` is the one path that puts a card in someone else's hidden zone into your view** — a reveal is to *all* players (rule 701.16), so those ids go into every seat's `objects`; a library is still never listed as a zone, so a revealed card's identity is public while its position stays hidden. A `VisibleObject` also carries its designations: `goadedBy` (`goad.ts`'s `goadersOf`), `suspected` and `saddled`; and a permanent carries `holding`, the exile ids linked to it (`exileHoldings`: an O-Ring's `exiledBy`, or an impulse permission's source while it's the same object — Theater of Horrors, Maralen), face-down ones included by id only, and `chosen`, what was chosen as it entered (`chosenOnEnter` or `chosenCreatureType` — Frostcliff Siege's "Temur", Heraldic Banner's `"U"`). `PlayerView` also carries `startingPlayer`, `dayNight`, `monarch`, `emblems` (each with its owner, text and `source` — the name of the card that created it, `EmblemState.sourceName`), and `decisionSource` (the `{object, cardName}` behind `awaiting`, or `null` — a decision variant's own `source` wins over `state.decisionSource`, and `cardName` stands alone because the object can be somewhere the deciding player can't see — a spell that exiled itself, a face-down foretold card — though a spell asking as it resolves is still on the stack, rule 608.2n); `PublicPlayerInfo` carries `maxLandsThisTurn` (extra land drops included — `ViewOptions.maxLands`, supplied by `Game.viewFor`, since only `Game` can evaluate the statics), `energy`, `counters`, `commanderCastCounts`, and `commanderDamageTaken` as a list of `CommanderDamage` (`commander` id, `name`, `owner`, `art`, `amount`) — named even when that commander isn't visible to the viewer. `COMMANDER_DAMAGE_LETHAL` (21) is exported alongside. `art` is where a deck's **chosen printing** surfaces: `DeckList.printings` (card name → Scryfall card id) is stored at setup on `PlayerState.printings` and substituted here for the card's *owner*'s objects, so every device at the table draws the card its owner brought. Player-level, not per-`GameObject`, because it's a fact about the physical cards someone owns rather than any game state — so it survives zone changes and control changes for free. Keyed by the card's **front face** (`def.faces[0]`), which is the name a decklist uses, so a transformed permanent keeps wearing it. `faceIsBack` rides alongside: a card id names the whole card and serves its front image, so a client has to ask Scryfall for `face=back`, and only the registry can tell a real second image from an adventure's spell half (same two-entry `faces` shape, one printing). A `VisibleObject` carries `supertypes` as they apply now (`filter.ts`'s `supertypesOf` — a copy made "not legendary" isn't), for the client's "Legendary" type line and gold name banner. `publicStints`' open from where a move began (`moveObject`'s `startSeq`), so a card's own arrival event — a lone card's `cards-put-into-exile`, which a cascade emits for each card — names it. `VisibleObject.attachedToPlayer` is the player a Curse is attached to (`null` otherwise). A phased-out permanent is in `zones.phasedOut` (never `zones.battlefield`) with `phasedOut: true` and `zone: "battlefield"`, drawn as it was as it phased out (its `lastKnown` P/T, types, colours and keywords); face-down stays face-down. The view carries each player's `sideboardSize` and the viewer's own `zones.sideboard` (their cards outside the game, visible to them alone — rule 400.11a; none beside a commander, `sideboardOf`), and each player's public `companion`, the revealed card while it's still outside the game (103.2b), its object in every seat's `objects`. The `cast-spell` action's `assist` and its offer's `assist`/`assistMax` (rule 702.132a): an assist variant names the other player asked, listed after the caster's own. `cast-spell`'s and `activate-ability`'s `otherChooser` (and their offers'): the opponent named to choose some of the targets or the modes; the action's `targets` are then only the caster's own slots. A `choose-from-zone` offer's `prompt`. A `choose-from-zone`'s face-down exiled candidates (`faceDownGroups`) stay hidden from a chooser who can't look at them (2026-10-31).
 A Room on the battlefield (rule 709.5) is drawn as its whole card (`faceName`, art) with `VisibleObject.doors` giving each door's name, cost, text and lock state, while the rest of the object is what it has. A face-down permanent (`faceDown`, rule 708) is the face-down 2/2 in every view — its internal `cardName`, no art, faces or copy — with `VisibleObject.faceDown.card` naming the real card only in its controller's view (708.5) and in everyone's once the game is over (708.9); a face-down commander isn't flagged as one to anyone else. A pending behold's `choose-from-zone` (`awaiting.behold`) shows other seats only the permanents among its candidates: which hand cards could be revealed would say what they are (rule 701.4a). `PublicPlayerInfo.speed` is a player's speed (rule 702.179), `null` while they have none.
- **`turn.ts`** — `Phase`/`Step` string-literal unions (no `enum`), `TURN_SEQUENCE`, `nextStep`, `stepUsesPriority` (false only for untap/cleanup), `isMainPhase`.
- **`delayed-at.ts`** (2026-11-09, need-key themes theme 15) — what a delayed triggered ability waits for: `DelayedAt` (`{ step, whose?, which? }`, what a card says), `DelayedStepWatch` (the state's, `whose` a `PlayerId` or `"any"`), `readDelayedAt`, `delayedStep` (the engine's own), `isStepWatch`, `delayedStepFires` (the one answer to "does this step beginning fire it", with the end-step and upkeep guards), `watchOutlastsItsTurn`. `until: WatchDuration` on `DelayedWheneverWatch` and `DelayedTrigger.if` (`Game.delayedIfHolds`) are read in `game.ts`.
- **`duration.ts`** (2026-11-07, need-key themes step 2) — how long an effect lasts, in one vocabulary: `Duration`, what a card says (strings for the controller's own forms, `{ untilTurnOf | throughTurnOf | duringTurnOf: DurationPlayer }` for any other player's, each word one meaning everywhere), and `Expiry`, what the state keeps once it began (moved here from `events.ts` with `UntilPoint`, `WhileSource`, `UntilCast`). Each store takes an `Extract` subset declared here — `PermanentDuration` (modifiers and control, via `Game.lasting`/`durationFields`), `PlayerDuration` (`PlayerEffect` and the timed static, via `resolvePlayerDuration`; its `"while-source"` / `"while-you-control-source"` read the source through `EffectContext.whileSourceHere` and end in `Game.endPlayerWhileSource`, called where a permanent's `whileSource` effects end — The Great Synthesis), `CopyDuration`, `PermissionDuration` (impulse), `DamageRuleDuration`, `WatchDuration`, `AttackRequirementDuration`, `PointDuration` (`untilPointOf`) — never a field-local union. `PlayerEffect.expires` and `EmblemState.expires` (a timed static: the `timed-static` effect, an emblem the view and the bots ignore) end in one pass, `Game.expirePlayerEffects` (cleanup, end of combat, a turn's beginning) and `mapPlayerExpiries`, their `UntilPoint`s with `passUntilPoints`; a `during` effect waits in `GameState.laterPlayerEffects` until that player's next turn. Not moved: the impulse permission's stored `expiry`, `TemporaryCopy.ends` (`CopyEnds`), prevention shields and the turn-wide lists. Pure, imports types only. `test/timed-statics.test.ts`.
- **`effects.ts`** — `ChooserRef` (2026-11-08, need-key themes step 6) and `chooserPlayer` say who makes a choice, the one type on every effect that lets another player choose (`docs/plans/need-key-themes.md`, theme 5).
- **`cost-acts.ts`** (2026-11-08, need-key themes step 6) — effects as costs: `COST_ACTS`, one row per `EffectSpec` kind a cost may be (`AbilityCost.do`, `KickerAlsoCost.do`): whether all of it can be paid now (118.3, built on `mayBeDone`), the most an X could be, whether it moves a card from a library (605.1a) or is random (601.2h). `costActPayable`, `payableCostAct`, `costActMaxX`, `costActReadsX`, `costActMovesLibrary`, `costActPaidLast`, `costActMayAsk`, `costActLosesSource`. A new cost is a row here, not an `AbilityCost` field; `Game.payCostAct` applies it, a spell's `additionalCost.also.do` that reads X makes it an X spell (`Game.additionalCostActX` — Soul Immolation's "blight X", capped by the card's `maxX`), and `SpellCastClause` (`when`, `cards/define.ts`) and `Game.costAmount` are the rest of the pipeline (`docs/plans/need-key-themes.md`, theme 4).
- **`journal.ts`** (2026-11-07, need-key themes step 3) — what has happened, for every "this turn", "last turn", "during their last turn", "since your last turn" and "this game" read (docs/plans/need-key-themes.md, theme 6's design note). `JournalEntry` is one event in one normalised shape (`kind`, `turn`, `player` whose record it is, `object` + `stint`, `arrived` after a move, `was` the object as it was — rules 603.10a, 608.2h, the event's own snapshot shared — `count`, `amount`, `from`/`to`, `target`/`targetStint`/`targetWas`, `combat`, `counter`, `how`); `Journal` (`GameState.journal`) holds this round's entries, the turns they belong to (`JournalTurn`, whose turn each was) and a whole-game count per player and kind, plus `combatDamagedBy`, each player's whole-game list of the names of creatures that dealt them combat damage (`noteCombatDamagedBy`, read by the `combat-damaged-this-game` condition — Gollum, Obsessed Stalker). `appendJournal` stamps the turn; `beginJournalTurn` notes a turn's player and drops what no window can name (before the last turn and each player's own last turn). `JournalWindow`: `this-turn`, `last-turn`, `your-last-turn`, `their-last-turn`, `since-your-last-turn`, `this-game` (`inJournalWindow`). `JournalWhat` is what a card says, each word a `JOURNAL_VIEWS` row over one kind ("died" is a `left` entry to a graveyard of a creature, "descended" a `to-graveyard` entry of a permanent card, "attackers" each `attacked` object once); `journalEntries` reads a view for players in a window, `entriesThisTurn` and `journalTotal` are the engine's fast paths, `journalGameCount` the game summary. Pure: `Game.journal` (game.ts) appends and invalidates the computed cache, `Game.recordJournal` records off `emit`, and `characteristics.ts`'s `journalCount`, `turnStatOf` (`TURN_STAT_READS`), `countersPutThisTurn` and `damageDealtThisTurn` and `filter.ts`'s `journaled` clause read it. Since step 4 (B278) it also holds the per-ability usage limits, each entry keyed by its `ability` (an opaque key) — `activated` (602.5b and Dragon Whelp), `triggered` (603.2), `resolved`, `did` (a mode chosen, `mode`), `done` — read by `abilityCountThisTurn` and `modesDoneThisTurn`, and `targeted`, `saddled` and `bent` entries for the trigger timing wrapper and Avatar Aang. A `land-played` entry keeps the zone the land was played from (`from`, 2026-11-11), so a `lands-played` read takes a `JournalOrigin` (Spider-Man 2099's "from anywhere other than your hand"). Not `eventLog`: bot simulations empty that.
- **`random-results.ts`** (2026-11-08, need-key themes step 7, theme 12) — what a coin flip or a die roll came to (rules 705, 706): `FlipLanding` (`won` absent on a flip that only cares about the side, 705.2) and `RollRecord` (natural results kept, modifier, total), carried by the `coin-flipped` and `dice-rolled` events and kept in the journal's `flipped` and `rolled` entries with the event's `seq`. Pure reads over them: `rollRead` (`{ roll }`), `matchingDice` (the `rolls-die` trigger's `die` test), `keepHighest` (706.6), `flipsSince` (`thisWay: "flips-won"` and its fellows), `lastRollIn` / `lastRollEntry`, `hasFlippedThisTurn`. `Game.flipCoinOutcomes` applies `would-flip-coin` and `rollDice` applies `would-roll-dice`; a trigger on a roll carries it in `lastKnownRefs.roll`. `rollsSince(state, since)` is every roll one resolution made, oldest first: what the `"opponents-who-rolled-highest"` scope compares (Chaos Dragon, 2026-11-12).
- **`trigger-events.ts`** (2026-11-07, need-key themes step 4; a row's `journaled` names the journal kind the timing wrapper counts it by, `becomes-crewed`'s "crewed") — the trigger events a card names by a word of its own, for the generic `{ on: "event", event, who?, filter?, by?, batched? }` `TriggerSpec` member: `TRIGGER_EVENTS` (`satisfies Record<string, TriggerEventRow>`, one row a word: its `GameEvent` type, an optional `when`, and how the event names its `player`, `object` / `objects`, `by` and `amount`), inverted into `TRIGGER_EVENTS_BY_TYPE` so `Game.refsAtTrigger` and `Game.eventTriggerObject` find "that player" / "that creature" by event type (`triggerEventPlayer`, `triggerEventObject`); `triggerEventSubjects` is what a row's `who` and `filter` are matched against (the objects where it names some, else the player; several objects are one occurrence each, 603.2c). Also `timingCountable`, the one list of kinds the timing wrapper (`TriggerTiming`: `firstTimeEachTurn`, `nthEachTurn` on every trigger) can count for — `Game.timingCount` counts each from the journal, `test/trigger-timing.test.ts` keeps the pool on the list. Pure; a new trigger event is a row here, not a `TriggerSpec` kind (theme 2's design note in `docs/plans/need-key-themes.md`).
- **`refs.ts`** (2026-11-08, need-key themes step 7) — one `ObjectRef` and one `PlayerRef`: base refs and accessors (`hostOf`, `pairedWith`, `controllerOf`, `ownerOf`, `defendingPlayerOf`), types only; resolved by `objectRefAct` / `objectRefRead` / `resolvePlayerRef` in `effects.ts` (read vs act: 608.2h, 400.7). `PlayerScope` is its plural words or any ref. Filter clauses take them, bound in `bindDynamicCompares`.
- **`play-permission.ts`** (2026-11-08, need-key themes step 7) — "you may play this card from there" (rule 601.3), one vocabulary however it arises: `PlayTerms` (what it reaches, what it costs, how often, when, what happens afterwards), and where one is kept: `StaticPlay` on a static (its `anyGraveyard` reaches every graveyard and `landAndSpell` splits its allowance into a land and a spell, Coram, the Undertaker, 2026-11-13; its `eachPlayer` makes every player its holder, Share the Spoils, and a play under a permanent's permission is announced with it, `PlayedUnder` on `spell-cast` / `land-played`, for `plays-card`'s `underThis`; `PlayTerms.affinity` gives a spell cast under a card's permission affinity, rule 702.41a, Chiss-Goria, and a card permission's `spell` is read where the play is offered), `EffectPlay` on a `PlayerEffect` (from `"library-top"` with `owners` it reaches those players' library tops too, `Game.playZoneOf` / `libraryTopsFor`, Xanathar, Guild Kingpin, and `PlayerEffect.looksAtLibraryTopOf` shows them in its owner's view, `view.ts`) (a rule that reaches later arrivals, 611.2c), `CardPlayPermission` on the one card (`GameObject.playPermission`, gone with any move, 400.7), `SelfPlay` on a definition (113.6f). `Game.playOptions` is the one reader, per card and face, and a permission never shows a hidden card by itself (406.3, 601.3f); the statics come from `playStaticsOf`, kept for the computed-cache region, so a read costs the permissions that player has, not the size of the battlefield. `chosenPlayOption` picks what a cast nothing names is under, `spendPlay` spends it. `cast-now` takes the same cost words (`CastNowTerms`) but is a decision (608.2g). Keyword casts, `grantsToGraveyard` and `via` are deliberately outside it (theme 10's design note). `PlayTerms.flash` (2026-11-11, Elsha of the Infinite) is the one timing word: `Game.whyCannotCast`'s timing check asks `permissionGivesFlash` (a permission that fits how the cast is paid — `chosenPlayOption`'s `flashOnly` — or the graveyard permission the cast names), and a cast that leans on it (`castNeedsPermissionFlash`) is put under such a permission as it's cast.
- **`object-sets.ts`** (2026-11-08, need-key themes step 5; `{ paid: "exiled-self" }` is a graveyard ability's own exile cost, as the card last was there) — one way to name a set of objects a card refers to, and how each was then: `ObjectSetRef` (`{ thisWay, who?, byOwner? }`, `{ paid: PaidKind }` — a cost's sacrificed, discarded, exiled, revealed, tapped or unattached objects —, `"exiled-with-source"` (607.2a), `"trigger-batch"`, `{ attachmentsOf: "source" }`, `{ targets }`, `{ remembered }`), its members `SetMember` (`{ object, stint, count?, was?, shown? }`: the stint after the act, how it was in the act, a token stack's size, shown in a hidden zone), and `ObjectSets`, the one field a stack object keeps them in (`LastKnownRefs.sets`: `paid`, `batch`, `remembered`), written only through `addToSet`. `staticSetMembers` answers the source's own sets for a static (a CDA's "cards exiled with it"); the rest are a resolution's, resolved by `ResolutionContext.objectSet` in `Game.makeResolutionContext` (and its `setMatches` for the aggregate). Every consumer takes the one ref: `AggregateSpec.in` (`filter.ts`, beside its zones; `"min"` too), `CardFilter.inSet` (bound to `amongObjects`), `ShareReference`, an `AmountRef`'s "the one member", `create-token-copy.of`, and `remember` on `delayed-trigger` / `reflexive-trigger` (theme 15's captured context, with `DelayedTrigger.x` and `.player`). `RememberSpec`, `isObjectSetRef`, `isCardSet`, `onlyMember`, `memberCount`, `setMembersAll`. `test/object-sets.test.ts` walks the pool for the per-set shapes it replaced.
- **`events.ts`** — **`cards-returned-to-library` (2026-11-11):** cards exiled from a library going back under it at random (`Game.exiledToBottomRandomly`: a cascade's, discover's or an exiling reveal-until's misses), per owner, for the client's animation; not `cards-put-on-bottom`, which "put on the bottom this way" counts. **For the log (2026-11-11):** `spell-countered` and `crime-committed` carry `source` when the object is an ability (it is gone by the time the line is read); `cards-chosen-from-zone` carries `to` (`battlefield`, `graveyard`, `exile`) and isn't emitted for a look that offered nothing (`max` 0); `prohibition-imposed` has `lands` (the `play-land` act); `Game.emit` opens a public stint for a `spell-cast` object that has none (a copy that is cast). `spell-control-changed` (a spell on the stack, not the `control-changed` of what watches permanents). `spell-countered` says who countered it (`by`), and who cast it (`castBy`); `player-counters-changed` and `energy-changed` who put them (`by`); `permanents-left`, like `permanents-died` never logged, closes a simultaneous leaving for `leaves-battlefield`'s `batched`. A discriminated `GameEvent` union (the audit log) covering every state change the engine makes — zone moves, damage, triggers, decisions, alt-cast events, combat, replacements, etc. Both log formatters (`scripts/format.mjs`, `client/src/format.ts`) render them; check there when you need the exact shape of a specific event. `permanents-died` is one simultaneous event's deaths, tokens included (`withLeaveBatch` emits it when done, `moveObject` at once for a lone death), with everything that left in it for look-back (rule 603.10a): the hook for a `batched` `dies` trigger, never written to the log (each death has its own lines there). `became-monstrous` (the `monstrosity` effect), `became-renowned` (the `renown` effect), `became-saddled` (the `become-saddled` effect; `first` when it wasn't saddled already) and `permanent-exerted` (with `asItAttacks`, what the linked "when you do" reads) are the trigger events for `becomes-monstrous` and `exerted`; `creature-tapped-for` (a creature tapped for another's keyword cost — `as: "enlist" | "crew" | "saddle"`, rules 702.154c, 702.122b, 702.171c) is `enlists`' and `crews-or-saddles`'. `permanent-untapped`'s `asItEntered` marks a shock land turned upright once its life was paid: it entered untapped (rule 614.1c), so no `becomes-untapped` trigger sees it. `gift-promised` (rule 702.174a) names the spell, its caster and the opponent its gift was promised to. `ability-triggered` is logged as the ability goes on the stack and names it there (`object`), which the client flies it to from its `source`. `damage-dealt`'s `by` is who controlled the source as it dealt the damage (as it last existed, for one that has left — what lifelink already reads), which the end-of-game stats credit damage by. `random-player-chosen` logs an at-random opponent (Territorial Hellkite), and `chosen-at-random` every other random pick — a `RandomPick` player, graveyard card or mode, with the `source` whose effect made it; `attacker-declared`'s `firstThisTurn` marks a creature's first attack of the turn. `Expiry`'s `{ whileSource }` (null when the source had already left, so the effect doesn't begin); `restrictions-imposed` carries a `duration` when it isn't until end of turn. `combat-damage-prevention-set`'s `partialBy`. `cards-put-into-exile` (one per simultaneous move — `withGraveyardEnterBatch`) gives each arrival its `from` zone and its `owner`: exile is one shared zone, so the owner is what says whose library a card left, public even for a face-down card (rule 406.3) and still known after it has gone back (cascade's misses). An `exile-from-library` effect is one move however many cards and players it takes ("exile the top three", Pako's "the top card of each player's library" — `ctx.simultaneously`, rule 603.2c); cascade, discover and an exiling `reveal-until` exile one card per move, as "until" does. `speed-changed` (`player`, `speed`, `from` — absent when start your engines! set it from none) logs each change of a player's speed. `permanents-phased-out` / `permanents-phased-in` (rule 702.26): no zone change, so no leaves or enters watcher sees either. `vote-cast` (an open vote, as cast) and `votes-finished` (every vote, a secret vote's revealed) carry `Vote`s; `sameVoteChoice` compares two. `permanent-turned-face-down` (708.2a) and `card-manifested`'s `library`, the other player whose library it came from (2026-10-31). `permanent-tapped-for-mana` (rule 106.12a, from a hand activation and from `useManaSource`, 2026-11-01) is what a `tapped-for-mana` trigger that isn't a mana ability hears: `detectTriggersUncached` leaves out the ones `isTriggeredManaAbility` passes, which `tappedForManaExtras` still makes with the mana (for a trigger of `anyAbility` too on an ability with no {T}, and on a land's stack ability through `ResolutionContext.withLandManaExtras`), reading the trigger's `condition` and `producing` (`"chosen"` too), and whose `add-mana`'s `also` runs right after the source's own rider (`manaExtraRiders`, read before a sacrifice moves it; `applyManaExtraRiders`, the tapped permanent the trigger object and its tapper "that player"; rule 605.4a). `noteTappedForMana` journals a `tapped-for-mana` entry (`TurnStat` `"lands-tapped-for-mana"`, Desolation). An extra with `mayAdd` is two `standaloneManaVariants`, its pick its type to take it and any other to decline (`declineManaPick`); paying a cost, `manaPlanningView` keeps an extra with its own restriction only where this payment can spend it. `permanent-tapped` says `by` (who tapped it) and `asAttacker`, read by `becomes-tapped`'s `by` and `notAttacking`; its `batched` fires once per tapping at the same time, consecutive events or an attack declaration (`Game.tapBatchFired`).
- **`cards.ts`** — a thin re-export barrel; the real content lives in `cards/`: `cards/define.ts` (`CardDefinition`/`CardDraft` + `defineCard`), `cards/helpers.ts` (`basicLand`, `manaTapAbility`, `addManaAbility`, `thisOrAnother` ("this creature or another X" as a self trigger and an `otherOnly` one), land-toolkit builders — `entersTappedStatic`, `checkLandStatic`, `painLand`, `fetchLand`, `sacrificeFetchLand`, `shockLand`), `cards/registry.ts` (`CardRegistry`, `createDefaultRegistry`), one file per card under `cards/pool/` (real cards + basics) and `cards/tokens/`. `CardDefinition` is **declarative** (`effect`, `keywords`, `activated`, `triggered`, `static: StaticAbility[]`, `supertypes`, `loyalty`, `copyOnEnter` (a `CopyOnEnter`: a Clone's "you may have this enter as a copy of …" — a `CardFilter` over what's already on the battlefield, read from the side of the player it enters under, or over cards in graveyards or exile (`from`), behind an optional `condition`, its `CopyExceptions`, and what copying adds to how it enters: tapped, extra counters judged by what it became, an until-end-of-turn duration, a linked "when you do" — rule 707.9; asked by `Game.askEnterChoice`, answered by the `choose-copy` decision, or for cards by a `choose-from-zone` with `copyOnEnterOf`, so the client's zone popup shows them), `becomesOnEnter` (a `BecomesOnEnter`: "it becomes your choice of …" or a coin's, each form a copy exception pushed as a copiable modifier as it enters — rule 707.2 — and asked as a menu), `chapters` for a Saga (each a `SagaChapter`, shaped like a targeted triggered ability and taking its `divided` — rule 603.3d, The Grand Evolution's seven counters; `readAhead` — rule 702.155: `defineCard` turns it into `chooseOnEnter` options "1" to the final chapter; `Game` enters the Saga with the chosen number of lore counters and, the turn it entered, `triggerChapters` lets only the chapter equal to its lore count trigger), `faces` for a multi-face card, `transform`/`disturb`/`adventure`/`suspend`/`foretell`/`flashback`/`escape`/`cycling`/`castModal`/`kicker`/`overload`/`freeCastIf`/`convoke`/`delve`/`selfCostReduction`/`targetCostReduction` (priced by `Game.castingCostOf` from the chosen targets, the offer narrowed by `targetReductionOptions`; a target-keyed `costModification` goes through the same offers, `spellPricedByTargets`, and `targetCostIncrease` through `targetTaxOffer`, as a `targetedBySpellsCost` does; rule 205.4e and `prohibits.castOnlyAsSorcery` are in `whyProhibitedFromCasting`, `activateAsThoughHaste` in `tapAbilityBlockedBySickness`, Oath of Teferi's second activation in `loyaltyActivationsAllowed`, Birgi's second boast in `boastsAllowed`)/`flashIf` (a spell's own conditional flash — a `condition` read as the cast begins, rule 601.3d, or `targets` judged on those chosen, 601.3b/601.6, by `Game.castsByConditionalFlash`; an effect's "as though it had flash", rule 601.3b, is `Game.castsAsThoughFlash` — a `castAsThoughFlash` static, every player's with `castAsThoughFlashAnyPlayer`, the first matching spell each turn with `castAsThoughFlashFirstEachTurn`, or a `player-effect`'s, and suspend reads it too; the offer's one fitting slot narrowed by `flashIfTargetOptions` when only it allows the cast now)/`additionalCost`/`cantBeCountered`/`art`) with an imperative `resolve(ctx)` hatch. `cards/generated.ts` is the codegen'd barrel (`npm run gen:cards -w engine`, also a `prebuild`/`pretypecheck` step, checked in; it reads the card files 64 at a time, ~3 s against 9 s one by one on Windows, 2026-10-31) — **to add a card, drop a new file in `cards/pool/` and run `gen:cards`**, which also refreshes the gitignored pool index the card tools read (`scripts/pool-index.mjs`, see the commands below). It emits `POOL_CARDS` and `TOKEN_CARDS` as well as the combined `BUILTIN_CARDS`, because the directory is the only record that a definition is a *token* — nothing on `CardDefinition` says so. `cards/classify.ts` reads them back as `isTokenCard` (rule 111.1 — a token isn't a card), `isCardFront` (rule 712.3 — a DFC is one card, deck-listed under its front face; a meld pair's combined back face, a definition with `meldOf`, is never a card, 712.4b) and `isDeckableCard` for the two together; `deck-validation.ts`, `card-replacer.ts`, the library page and the deck builder's pool all filter on these rather than guessing from the name. `cards/pool.test.ts` fails if a card isn't wired into `generated.ts`, or if the pool/tokens split drifts. The same run writes the pool a second way, for the web client: 32 shard modules under `cards/shards/`, each card in the shard its name hashes to (`cards/card-shards.ts`: `cardShardOf`, and `loadCardShard`, whose literal `import()` per shard is what gives each shard its own file in a bundle), plus `cards/generated-index.ts`, what a page can know without loading any card: `TOKEN_NAMES` (what `isTokenCard` reads, so classifying a definition never imports every token) and `PINNED_ART` (a card's pinned printing, for drawing a commander from its name alone). The codegen reads each card's name and art from its source — `defineCard({ name, … })`, or a helper whose first argument is the name — and `pool.test.ts` checks both, and every card's shard, against the real definitions. The engine's `package.json` declares **`"sideEffects": false`**, which is what lets a bundler leave out every card module a page never reaches: each card file's `defineCard(…)` call would otherwise count as an import-time side effect, and the whole pool would ship with any page that touched the engine's barrel. It holds because no engine module does anything at import time but define things; keep it so. **`cards/AUTHORING.md` is the hand-authoring guide** — every field, the effect/target/ability/static vocab, worked examples, current limitations; read it before authoring a new card. `Game.debugSpawn(name, player, zone)` is the sanctioned white-box "put a card straight into a zone" helper, going through the real `moveObject`. `Game.debugMove(id, zone)` moves an object that already exists the same way, announcing nothing (the scenario builder laying out a board, `server/src/builder.ts`). `CardDefinition.toxic` is Toxic N as a number, not a `Keyword` (it's cumulative), and `casualty` is Casualty N. `CardDefinition.omen` marks an omen card (rule 720, `faces: [creature, Omen]`), resolved as an Omen into its owner's library. Keywords `infect` and `wither` (rules 702.90, 702.80 — damage as -1/-1 counters and poison, in `Game.dealDamage`), `skulk`, `flanking` (with the `flanking()` helper for its trigger) and `riot` are in the union; `StaticAbility.exertAsItAttacks` is "you may exert this creature as it attacks", `StaticAbility.enlist` enlist (rule 702.154a, the `enlist()` helper), and `castFromGraveyard.exileOthers` a permission's "by exiling N other cards from your graveyard". `helpers.ts`'s `GIFT_KICKER` (`{ cost: "", keyword: "gift" }`) and `giftTrigger(gift)` ("when this permanent enters, if its gift cost was paid, …") are gift, rule 702.174. `CardDefinition.castFromGraveyardIf` is "you may cast this card from your graveyard as long as …" (Gravecrawler — rule 113.6f). `AdditionalCostOption.blight` (Bogslither's Embrace's "blight 1 or pay {3}") and `kicker.keyword: "blight"` with `kicker.blight` (Burning Curiosity's "you may blight 1") are blight as a spell's costs (rule 701.68a). `BeholdSpec` (`effects.ts`) is behold as one: `AdditionalCostOption.behold` ("behold an Elf or pay {2}"), `additionalCost.behold` (mandatory — with `exile`, the Champions' "behold an Elemental and exile it"), `kicker.keyword: "behold"` with `kicker.behold` ("you may behold a Dragon"; "if a Dragon was beheld" is the kicked cast, 701.4b), `kicker.flash` (Molten Exhale) and `flashback.behold` (Kindle the Inner Flame's "behold three Elementals"). `additionalCost.waterbend` (`number | "x"`) is "as an additional cost to cast this spell, waterbend {5}" / "{X}" (rule 701.67a — Water Whip, Waterbender's Restoration; `"x"` an X spell without an `{X}` in its mana cost, like `payLifeX`), and `kicker.keyword: "waterbend"` with `kicker.cost: "{N}"` is "you may waterbend {N}" (Katara, Seeking Revenge). `helpers.ts`'s `crew(n)`/`crewText(n)` (rule 702.122a: a `tapOthers.totalPower` cost, `crew: true`, an `become` of the source) write crew, and its `saddle(n)`/`saddleText(n)` (rule 702.171a: crew's `tapOthers.totalPower` cost, `sorcerySpeed`, `saddle: true`, a `become-saddled` of the source) and `ATTACKS_WHILE_SADDLED` (an `attacks` trigger whose `whileCondition` is the source's `saddled` clause) write saddle. `helpers.ts`'s `VOID_CONDITION` (void, Edge of Eternities: an `any` StaticCondition — "at least one of these" — over a `turn-history` `left` of every player's nonland permanents and a `cast-this-turn` `{ castVia: "warp" }` of everyone's spells, rule 702.185c) and `REVOLT_CONDITION` (revolt and disappear: `{ kind: "turn-history", what: "left" }`) are the two ability words over the `left` turn history, and `CELEBRATION_CONDITION` (celebration, Wilds of Eldraine: `{ kind: "turn-history", what: "entered", filter: { notTypes: ["land"] }, atLeast: 2 }` — read as each entered, though it has left or changed control since) the one over the `entered` list; the scaffolder writes them as literals. `godDevotion(colors, lessThan, text)` is a Theros God's "as long as your devotion to … is less than N, ~ isn't a creature" (a `"self"` static: a `devotion` condition and `removeTypes: ["creature"]`), which the scaffolder reads, with a Demigod's "~'s power is equal to your devotion to …", Altar of the Pantheon's bonus, a devotion intervening-if, Arixmethes' "as long as ~ has a [kind] counter on it, it's a [type]" and Titan of Littjara's chosen type. `GraveyardCastCosts` (define.ts, 2026-10-31) is the cost half of a graveyard permission — `payLife`, `sacrifice`, `exileOthers`/`exileFilter`, `discard`, alternative `mana`, `costIncreasePerOther` — shared by `StaticAbility.castFromGraveyard` and the card's own `castSelfFromGraveyard` (`if?`, `fromExile?`), which the scaffolder reads.
- **`cards/edhrec-rank.ts`** — `EDHREC_RANK` / `edhrecRankOf(name)`: how popular each pool card is in Commander (1 = most played). **Generated** by `npm run gen:edhrec-rank -w engine` from the Oracle snapshot's `edhrec_rank` (`-- --online` asks Scryfall instead, batched 75 names to a request), and checked in so nothing needs the network at runtime; rerun it as the pool grows (it ranked 11,224 cards against a 29,435-card pool before its 2026-11-06 rerun). A card absent from the table is *unranked* rather than unpopular — basics always are — and consumers sort those last. Two consumers: the library's `Popularity` sort, and the authoring backlog's ordering (`top-commander-cards.txt` is a frozen snapshot of the same data).
- **`cards/release-date.ts`** — `FIRST_PRINTED` / `releaseDateOf(name)`: the day each card was first printed (`YYYY-MM-DD`), grouped by date so a name is written once; `null` for a name it doesn't list. **Generated** by `npm run gen:release-dates -w engine` (`scripts/gen-release-dates.mjs`: Scryfall's `is:firstprint`, cached three days in `.cache/scryfall/firstprints.json`), for the pool's cards and those in `client/public/missing-cards.json`. Only the card library's "Release date" sort reads it, through `engine/client`.
- **`cards/salt-score.ts`** — `SALT_SCORE` / `saltScoreOf(name)`: how much Commander players dislike facing each pool card, as EDHREC's salt score (the yearly survey's average, 0–4; Stasis tops it near 3). **Generated** by `npm run gen:salt-score -w engine` from `data/edhrec-salt.json`, a checked-in snapshot of every card EDHREC scores above 0 (not just the pool's), so a card joining the pool gets its score offline; `-- --online` refreshes the snapshot from json.edhrec.com's `top/salt` pages, an undocumented endpoint (the same risk `top-commanders.mjs` takes), stopping at the first card scored 0. A card absent from the table reads as 0, whether nobody minds it or it's too new to be surveyed. No consumer yet: it's an input for aiming bot removal (`BACKLOG.md`, Bots).
- **`cards/flavor-names.ts`** — `FLAVOR_NAMES` / `nameForFlavorName(name)` / `flavorNamesOf(cardName)`: the alternate names cards are printed under (Scryfall's `flavor_name` — "Princess Sarah" for Azusa, Lost but Seeking), each mapped to the card's pool name (a split card's full name, another multi-face card's front face); never a name some real card has. **Generated** by `npm run gen:flavor-names -w engine` from Scryfall's `has:flavorname` search and the Oracle snapshot. Read by the decklist importer and the library and deck-builder searches; exported from both `index.ts` and `client.ts`.
- **`local.ts`** (`engine/local`), **`local-plugin.ts`** (`engine/local-plugin`), **`cards/default-registry.ts`** / **`default-registry.browser.ts`**, **`cards/registry-from.ts`** — the engine as a browser runs a game with it, for solo games in the player's browser (`docs/plans/local-rooms.md`): `engine/client`'s values plus `Game`, the controllers, `auto-settle`, the v2 bot and its evaluation (not `bot/index.ts`, whose training scenarios build the whole pool), what the server's room code needs besides (`actionPlayer`, `computeCharacteristics`, `autoAnswerFor` — the room code imports only `engine/local`, so a browser can run it — and `defaultRegistry`, the whole pool in Node and the refusing stub in a browser bundle), and `registryFrom(cards)` — a registry of the given definitions plus the synthetic ones every game needs (face-down, the locked Room door, emblems, speed), which `createDefaultRegistry` is over the whole pool. `Game.create`/`fromSnapshot` and both bots fall back, when handed no registry, on `defaultRegistry()` from `cards/default-registry.ts` — the one route from them to the pool — and a browser bundle resolves that file to `default-registry.browser.ts`, which throws ("no card registry was passed"): `engineLocalPlugin()` (`local-plugin.ts`, typed structurally so the engine depends on no bundler) does the swap for Vite, Rollup or Rolldown. `test/local-entry.test.ts` walks `local.ts`'s static imports with the same swap and fails if any reach a card file (as `client-entry.test.ts` does for `engine/client`); `test/local-bundle.test.ts` bundles the built `dist/local.js` with Rolldown and the plugin, checks the entry and its static chunks hold no card definition (the 32 shards each a chunk loaded on demand), then imports the bundle, builds a registry from the shards and plays a bot game to turn 5 — skipped when `dist/` is older than the sources.
- **`companion.ts`** — companion's deck conditions (rule 702.139): `CompanionCondition`, the data on `CardDefinition.companion` (`each-card` over a scope and a test, `different-names`, `shared-card-type`, `deck-size-over-minimum`), and `companionConditionProblem`/`companionProblem`, why a starting deck by name doesn't fulfil one, commanders counted (702.139b); `companionProblem` also holds the card to the commanders' colour identity. Pure over the registry, for `Game`'s reveal and the deck builder. `hasActivatedAbility(def)` is Zirda's read: `activated`, `cycling`, or a basic land type (305.6).
- **`identity.ts`** — `colorIdentityOf(def)` (rule 903.4 — a lexical `{…}`-symbol scan of the mana cost + all rules/ability text) + `withinIdentity`/`identityString`. Consumed by `deck-validation.ts`. A card's `colors` count too, unless it sets `colorsOutsideIdentity` (Fallaji Wayfarer's "this ability doesn't affect its color identity").
- **`deck-validation.ts`** — `validateCommanderDeck({commanders, cards, size}, registry)` → `{legal, violations, identity}`: singleton (basics exempt, and a card's own `deckCopies` through `copiesAllowed`: "any number of cards named ~", "up to seven"; a commander counts as a copy), colour identity, deck size (each bent by a commander's declarative `rulebreaker`, Mystery Booster Commander's Rulebreaker, 2026-11-12: `anyIdentity` card kinds let in off-identity, `extraColorFor` one extra colour the whole deck's instants and sorceries share — legal when some one colour covers them all — and `noMaximumSize` makes the size a minimum; `fitsDeckIdentity` is the per-card half the deck builder's identity filter reads, `rulebreakersOf` a deck's), a commander that may command (`canCommandAlone`, rule 903.3: a legendary creature, Vehicle, or Spacecraft with P/T, or a card whose declarative `canBeCommander` says it can be your commander — a planeswalker without it can't), a legal pair for two commanders (`pairingProblem`/`canPairCommanders` over each card's declarative `pairing` — never its rules text — and also what the deck builder's commander toggle asks: two plain Partners, two "Partner with"s naming each other, two same-text "Partner—[text]"s, "Choose a Background" + a legendary Background enchantment, "Doctor's companion" + a lone Time Lord Doctor; a Background is a commander *only* as that second half, and `canCommandAlone`/`isBackground` are exported for callers that pick commanders), and "is actually a card" (`cards/classify.ts` — a token or a DFC back face is a registered definition and would otherwise pass every other check). Only checks *implemented* cards. Lives in the engine (it originated in `server/`) because it's pure synchronous logic over `CardRegistry` data — the client's deck builder needs the same rules with no network round-trip. **Banned cards** (`banned`): the deck's cards banned in Commander, from `COMMANDER_BANNED` (`cards/generated-index.ts`, which `gen:cards` writes from the Oracle snapshot's legality). They're in the pool for casual play (the user's call, 2026-10-11), so they aren't violations: `legal` stays true, and a room set to Commander-legal decks only refuses them (server `deckProblemOf`).
- **`sample-decks.ts`** — `SAMPLE_DECKS`: fourteen real Commander precons, each a legal 100-card deck — first the five Tarkir: Dragonstorm decks (`TDC`, the bots' defaults since 2026-09-30, so the server's seats take the first four), then the five 2022 Starter Commander Decks and Tramplesaurus Rex, Reign of Dragons (`FDC`), Family Matters (`BLC`) and World Shaper (`EOC`). Only decks flagged `bench` (six: Sultai Arisen, Abzan Armor, Jeskai Striker, Token Triumph, Reign of Dragons and, since 2026-10-07, World Shaper) are in `BENCH_DECKS`, which is all the bot tooling seats (`scripts/bot-seating.mjs`), so benchmarks stay on one set of decks; flag another once a deck-against-deck run shows it holds its own. Each keeps its `printed` list verbatim plus a `substitutions` table (`{original, substitute, reason}`) swapping in reviewed stand-ins for cards the engine doesn't implement yet; `cards` is the result. To revert a substitution once its card is authored, delete its entry — `test/sample-decks.test.ts` fails for any substitution whose original is now implemented, and checks legality. The record of every swap and why is `docs/plans/precon-decks.md`. **`UPGRADED_DECKS`** is every precon upgraded by hand, what a blitz deals (2026-11-11; `upgraded(base, {cards, lands})`: `[cut, add]` swaps, land for land and spell for spell, named "<base> (Upgraded)", with `upgradeOf` and `swaps` kept: one strong mana base of duals, shocks, fetches and other untapped lands in every deck, and 21-30 spells a deck, counterspells in the blue ones; a deck with `bots: false` is kept out of what a bot is dealt, read through `botPool`: Jeskai Striker (Upgraded), which the bots pilot too badly; `docs/plans/upgraded-decks.md`), and **`STARTER_DECKS`** both lists, the upgrades appended so a saved starter index still names the same deck. **`STRONG_DECKS`** is eight real bracket 4 decks from Archidekt, each its creator's list exactly, named "<commander> by <author>" with a `credit` (`DeckCredit`: author, title, Archidekt id, date taken): a pool a room can deal its bots and nothing else, so not in `STARTER_DECKS`. Its data is **`strong-decks.ts`**, generated by `scripts/gen-strong-decks.mjs` (`npm run decks:strong`) from the lists the user copied by hand into `data/archidekt-decks/` (no script may fetch from Archidekt; `docs/plans/strong-bot-decks.md`). `scripts/deck-winrates.mjs --pool upgraded | blitz | strong` (`scripts/deck-pool.mjs`) benches the precons against their upgrades, the upgrades against each other, or the strong decks against the upgrades; `--budget MS` gives each decision the live rooms' clock, and the run ends with the cards the bots could most often cast and didn't. Each precon also carries `printings` (card name → the Scryfall id of its printing in that product), from **`starter-printings.ts`**'s `PRECON_PRINTINGS` — generated from MTGJSON's deck files by `scripts/gen-starter-printings.mjs` (default source the git-ignored `.scratch/precons/decks/`; rerun when a starter deck changes) — narrowed to the cards it plays, so a stand-in and an upgrade's added card keep the pool's default art; an upgraded deck inherits its base's for the cards it keeps (2026-10-11, the user's ask). One source of truth for two consumers: `server/src/decks.ts`'s `SEATS` (a seat's fallback deck — the first four, one per seat) and the client deck builder's "starter decks" (all five).
- **`card-replacer.ts`** — `suggestReplacements(target, context)` → up to three implemented `BUILTIN_CARDS` stand-ins for an unimplemented card, best first, each with a `confidence` (`high`/`medium`/`low`) and the `sharedTags` behind it. `assignReplacements` does it for a whole deck at once, and is what the import uses: the Hungarian algorithm over every missing card's candidates, so a stand-in two cards want goes to the one with the most to lose. Pure and synchronous. `target` is what Scryfall says about the missing card (name, mana cost, type line, power/toughness, keywords); `context` is the destination deck — `identity` (only cards inside it), `exclude` (never a card the deck already has, so singleton holds), `forCommander` (only legendary creatures/planeswalkers) — plus an optional `OracleTagIndex`. **Role matching runs on Scryfall Tagger oracle tags**: `createOracleTagIndex(data)` wraps the server's generated index, and similarity is an IDF-weighted cosine over the tags two cards share (a rare shared tag says more than a common one). A candidate must share a primary type with the target (instant/sorcery count as one) unless the tags say they do the same job; a land only replaces a land. Score = tags, then type, a steep mana-value closeness (so a finisher isn't replaced by a two-drop that shares a mechanic) and, for creatures, body (P/T and combat keywords). Commander popularity then scales it (`popularityOf`: EDHREC rank on a log scale, ×0.85 for an unranked card to ×1.15 for the most played), so of two comparable stand-ins the more played one comes first, while a clearly better match still wins. Without tags only the latter count and confidence is always `low`. The engine does no I/O; the index lives server-side. Design record and measured results: `docs/plans/card-replacer.md`. Also exports `parseTypeLine`.
- **`view.ts`** — A face-down permanent's `faceDown.card` also goes to the chooser of an open `look-at-face-down` look (`awaiting.looksAtFaceDown`), for as long as it's open and to no one else. Ahand looked at for an "as this enters" choice (`looked` on a `choose-card-name` or a `choose-creature-type`) is in the chooser's view alone: the objects, and the field stripped from everyone else's `awaiting`. `viewFor(state, registry, viewer)`, the redacted per-seat snapshot (hidden information stays hidden; every hand's ids are public, its cards' identities only to whoever may see them). `sightOf` folds the battlefield's `StaticAbility.visibility` statics (a card's own or granted, with their conditions) into what this viewer sees past that: hands played revealed, libraries' top cards played revealed, the viewer's own top card, and `VisibleObject.faceDown.card` on face-down creatures they don't control (never a face-down spell). `state.awaiting`'s looks (a `choose-from-zone`, a `scry`, a `separate-piles`) go through the same `visibleIds`. A pure read, inside `withComputedCache`. `view.ts` holds a NUL byte like `game.ts`: `grep -a`. `sightOf` also adds the hands a `PlayerEffect.handsRevealed` shows (Sen Triplets). A stack object's targets leave out a card exiled face down the viewer isn't shown (`isHiddenExile`): a delayed ability's "those cards" (Memory Jar).
- **`zone-choice-together.ts`** — A sum still to reach fits so far (`fitsSoFar` reads only a cap), and `completeTogether` adds nothing that adds nothing to a sum, naming a token stack once per token where `eligible` does (`choose-permanents`' `totalPowerAtLeast`, whose `stackSizes` counts them). `"one-of-each"` (`choose-one-of-each`): every slot with a candidate covered, each chosen the one for a slot of its own; `fitsSoFar` is the second half alone, for a set still being built, and `completeTogether` always gets from a pick to an answer. `ZoneChoiceTogether` (`rule: "share" | "one-each" | "total" | "distinct" | "variety"`, per-card `tags`, a client `text`) and the pure `fitsTogether` / `togetherViolation` / `completeTogether` over it: a rule a `choose-from-zone`'s picks must obey as a set, raised by a `search-library`'s or a `look-and-choose`'s `together`, and a `choose-permanents`' picks too (`distinctPowers`, `distinctNames`: Sigarda's Vanguard, Battle for Bretagard) (`SearchTogether` in `effects.ts` — Myriad Landscape's "that share a land type", Krosan Verge's "a Forest card and a Plains card", Atraxa, Grand Unifier's nine card-type slots, whose `oneEach` matching is plain backtracking; a look's card that fits no slot isn't eligible, its `max` is the slot count, and its `min` is clamped to `largestOneEach`, the largest set a card a slot can make; `oneEachTypeAmong` reads its slots from the spells cast this turn and `totalManaValueAtMost` raises the `total` rule). A `look-and-choose`'s `split` (`ZoneChoiceSplitCase`, carried on the decision as `split`) sends each chosen card by what it is, read in `Game.chosenFromZoneTo` as they move. "An opponent chooses" (`look-and-choose`'s `chooser: "an-opponent"`, `SearchInstead`'s `chooser`) goes through `Game.offerToAnOpponent`: the ready decision, for the one opponent, or wrapped in an engine-built `offer-zone-choice` effect under each mode of the controller's choice of opponent, so the opponent is chosen with the cards in view. One instruction over several objects is one event: `enterHeldEntries` wraps its entries in one graveyard leave and one enter batch (Exhume), a `search-library`'s `alongExiledThisWay` enters what the resolution exiled with the find (`enterAlong`, Sword of Hearth and Home), and `castModal.simultaneous` runs a spell's chosen modes in one `simultaneously` (Call Damage Control). The turn's `damageDealt` record says what each source was and what it hit (`name`, `types`, `to`, `toStint`), read by `{ damageDealtThisTurn }`'s `to` and `by` and the condition's `toSelf`; `choose-value`'s `"spell-damage"` picks among a player's spells by what each dealt (Backdraft, with `choose-opponent`'s `whoIf`). `additional-combat`'s `forcedAttackers` queues the bound objects on the `AddedPhase` and binds them (a `must-attack` modifier until end of combat, with a `cant-attack` record for `avoid`) only as that phase begins; `remove-from-combat`'s `unblockSoleBlocked` reads `lostBlockerIn`, and its `thenMayBlock` raises a `choose-permanents` whose `block-attacker` (`blockAttackerByEffect`) blocks by effect, its triggers included (rules 509.3a-d). A `for-each-player`'s `choicesHeld` holds a look-and-choose's picks (`GameState.heldChoices`, each entry with its `under`) until the last player has chosen; `beginZoneChoice` leaves out what's already held. `Game.searchTogether` works the tags out as the search is raised (each card's land types, or the slots whose filter it matches), so checking a set needs no registry — the validator, the bots and the client (`engine/client` exports `fitsTogether`) share it. `view.ts` empties `tags` for every viewer but the searcher: they say what kind of card each library card is.
- **`filter.ts`** — `sameNameWith`'s reference objects skip a card exiled face down or foretold, which has no name to share (rule 406.3a). `manaValueParity` and `powerParity` (`ParityClause`; the latter never inside the fold, as `power`): `"odd"`/`"even"`, `$chosen` substituted into a resolving effect, or `"chosen"`, the `FilterContext.source`'s `chosenOnEnter`, its `lastKnown` once it has left (608.2h).`attackedThisCombat`, `attackedYouThisTurn`, `attackedDuringControllersLastTurn`: a creature's record of its attacks, a left one read through `LastKnownInfo`.`DistinctOf`'s `"card-type"` (printed, delirium's count at any threshold) and `"counter-kind"`; `nameDiffersFromEach` compares every name either object has (rule 201.2c). An aggregate's `of: { manaSymbols }` (chroma) and `aggregate: "greatest-shared-creature-type"` (a changeling in every group), which every reader of `AggregateSpec` takes: amounts, costs, conditions. `hasSuspend` reads a card's printed suspend (The Face of Boe), beside `hasCycling`. `greatestInZone: { of, among }` is one comparison over everything in the object's zone that matches `among`, ties counted unless `strictly`: every permanent on the battlefield whoever controls it, or that graveyard (Topple, Getaway Glamer, Desecrator Hag), an object that left read as it last was (608.2h); in the layer fold only mana value and counters compare (Favor of the Mighty). `controlledBy: "monarch"` (rule 725, Fall from Favor through `attachedTo`). `hasFlashback` reads a card's printed flashback or a resolved `grantedFlashback` (rule 702.34; a static's graveyard-only grant isn't read). `creaturesBlocking` counts an attacker's blockers for `{ blockersOf }` and `CardFilter.blockerCount`. `blockingRelation.of` is the source, its host, or `"trigger-object"` (an effect's filter alone: `bindDynamicCompares` is what can read a trigger object). A `shares…TypeWith` clause no effect bound (a trigger's filter) is answered from `liveReferenceObjects` where the context can name the reference; `hasAbilities` is answered inside the fold through `layered` (`keywords`, `hasProtection`). `attachments` asks what's attached to a permanent (`otherThanSource` for "another Aura"), live; `sharesColorWithHost` and `sharesCreatureTypeWithHost` are a static's live read of the source's host, not bound by `bindDynamicCompares` as `sharesCreatureTypeWith` is. `sameNameTokensOnly`, `doubleFaced` (rule 712.1, reading `faces`, `melded` and a meld front's `meldCard`) beside `sameNameWith`: the namesakes must be tokens, the target needn't be (Legions to Ashes). `controlledBy: "enchanted-player"` is read off the filter's source (`attachedToPlayer`), the same player a `would-gain-life`/`would-mill` `who`, a `would-deal-damage` `to` and a `deals-damage-batch` `to` can name. `CardFilter` + `matchesFilter(state, registry, id, filter, { you })`: a pure predicate over an object + its *computed* characteristics (`targets` — a `SpellTargetsFilter` on what a spell on the stack targets: one target, or with `only` every target, being a player (`player`) or a battlefield permanent matching `permanent` and being or not being the filter's `source`, `single` for all one object; a target that has changed zones since it was targeted no longer counts, rule 400.7 — used by cast triggers, a `deals-damage` source filter and `spell` target specs; `type`/`types`/`notTypes`/`subtype`/`subtypes`/`supertype`/`name`/`colors`/`notColors`/`colorless`/`manaValue`/`power`/`toughness`/`controlledBy`/`ownedBy`/`keyword`/`tapped`/`token`/`enteredThisTurn`/`controlledContinuously` (rule 302.6's test on any permanent, read off `summoningSick`)/`attackedThisTurn`/`attackedBattleThisTurn`/`damagedThisTurnBy`/`excessDamageThisTurn`/`dealtDamageToCreatureThisTurn`/`dealtDamageThisTurn` (the last the other side, `GameObject.dealtDamage`: a `DealtDamageRecord` of what the permanent has dealt damage to this stint, this turn's players cleared as a turn begins, which the condition `self-hasnt-dealt-damage` reads too and which keeps tokens with different records from folding) (a `DamageHistory` `dealDamage` keeps on `GameObject.damageThisTurn` as it damages a permanent: each source as it was then, and whether any of it was excess, rule 120.4a)/`goaded`/`suspected`/`saddled`/`enlistedThisCombat` (rule 702.154c, `GameObject.enlistedIn` in the combat under way, or the last-known one)/`crewedSource` and `crewedBySource` ("a creature that crewed it this turn" and "a Vehicle crewed by ~ this turn", rule 702.122c — `crewedVehicle` reads `GameObject.crewedThisTurn`, sharing `saddledMount`'s stint lookup; an effect's binds to `crewedBy`)/`saddledSource` ("a creature that saddled it this turn", rule 702.171c — `saddledMount` reads `GameObject.saddledThisTurn` against `FilterContext.source`, or the `saddledBy` an effect's filter is bound to, as the Mount is on the battlefield or last was there)/`unblocked` (an unblocked attacker, rule 509.1h: attacking and not blocked, from the declare blockers step on — 508.4d)/`putIntoGraveyardFromLibraryThisTurn`/`putIntoGraveyardThisTurn` (`{ from? }`: put there this turn from one of these zones, or from anywhere — Grim Return, Night Nurse)/`blockedThisTurn` (`{ relation, partner?, of?, with?, thisCombat? }`: read off the journal's `blocked` entries, the other creature as it was at the block, `of` bound to objects and stints by `bindDynamicCompares` or as a `delayed-trigger` is set up — You Cannot Pass!, Venomous Breath) — the last two read off `GameObject.putIntoGraveyardOn` (`{ turn, from }`), which `moveObject` sets on every move into a graveyard and clears on any other; `discardedThisTurn` off `GameObject.discardedOnTurn`, set as a discard is announced and cleared on any move but a declined madness card's exile-to-graveyard; `milledThisTurn` off `GameObject.milledOnTurn`, set as a mill is announced (`cards-milled`) and cleared on any move — a surveilled card wasn't milled, ANDed). Off the battlefield it degrades to printed values — unless the caller asks for last-known information: `FilterContext.lastKnown` (a trigger filter matched as a permanent leaves — its `GameObject.lastKnown`, or `GameState.ceasedTokens` for a deleted token) or `FilterContext.snapshot` (a `LastKnownInfo` the caller picked out), and then every clause reads the snapshot through the same code path. `FilterContext.castX` judges a card not on the stack as the spell it would be with `{X}` announced at that value (rules 601.3e, 202.3e): its mana value counts it — a cast permission's filter, Glarb, Calamity's Augur's "mana value 4 or greater". `sharesCardTypeWith` / `sharesPermanentTypeWith` / `sharesCreatureTypeWith` / `sharesLandTypeWith` each take a `ShareReference` (`"sacrificed"`, `"trigger-object"`, `"source"`, `"attached"`, `"chosen"`, a target slot, or `{ controlled: filter }` — every permanent you control matching it) and are bound by `bindDynamicCompares` (its `SHARE_CLAUSES` table and `shareReferenceObjects`) to a `sharesTypeWith` entry — the reference objects, which always match ("target creature and each other creature that shares a creature type with it"), and their types of that kind as they are or last existed (`cardTypesOf`/`subtypesOf`), changeling marker included, matched through `hasSubtype`; unbound they match nothing (Braids, Baral and Kari Zev, Heirloom Blade, kinship, the Crowns, Endemic Plague, Pyre of Heroes, Killer, Counterlash, Reality Scramble, Hiveheart Shaman). `sameNameWith: n` ("target X and all other Xs with the same name as that X", rule 201.2a — Maelstrom Pulse, the Echoing cycle) is bound by `bindDynamicCompares` to `sameNameAs: { object, name }` — the target itself and its name, `null` for a face-down one (`ResolutionContext.nameForMatching`) — and matches nothing unbound;
