The option list for any "choose a creature type" decision: the `choose-creature-type` effect (Crippling Fear) and `chooseCreatureTypeOnEnter` (Urza's Incubator) both raise an `awaiting` `choose-creature-type` with `catalog: true`, and its `LegalAction` adds `suggested` — the chooser's 8 most common creature types across their own hand/library/graveyard/command zone and the whole battlefield, computed in `legalActions` because only the engine may read that library. The client renders a catalog choice as `ui/CreatureTypePicker.tsx` (suggestion chips over a search box); a short fixed list (Heraldic Banner's colours) stays a row of buttons.
- **`attachments.ts`** — What an `attach` or `unattach` moves and where to (theme 14): `AttachmentsRef` (an `ObjectRef`, `AttachAll`, `AttachChoice`, the engine-built `AttachResolved`) and `AttachTarget` (an `ObjectRef`, a `{ player }`, `AttachHostChoice`, the engine-built `AttachHostResolved`), with their guards. Applied by `applyAttach` and `applyUnattach` in `effects.ts`: what's attached is settled first, then the host, and a group's host must take every member. Legality is `Game.attachmentKind` and `canAttachTo`, reading the permanent as it is (rules 301.5, 301.6, 303.4j).
- **`prohibitions.ts`** — A static's `who` can be `PlayersWhoDid` (`{ who, thisTurn: TurnDid }`): the players `who` names whose own `TurnStat` has reached the count, each asked for themselves in `whoBinds` (Angelic Arbiter's "each opponent who attacked with a creature this turn can't cast spells"); its creature-side twin is `CardFilter.controllerThisTurn`. `gain-life` is a resolved effect's "can't gain life" bound to its players (Stigma Lasher); a static's is still a `would-gain-life` `prevent`, and both are read before any replacement by `Game.changeLife`. One record for "can't" (theme 3c, rule 101.2): `Prohibition`, a union on `act`, on `StaticAbility.prohibits` and in the `prohibit` effect, which lowers to a timed static with its players and objects bound (rule 611.2c). Player acts take `who` (`StaticWho`, an effect's `PlayerScope`, or stored `{ players }`); object acts take `object` (a filter from the source's side), an effect's `target`, or stored `objects: [{ object, stint }]`. `liveProhibitions` is the memoized read; `prohibitionsOnPlayer`, `objectProhibited`, `reachesObject`, `whoBinds`, `targetProhibited`, `causeProhibited`, `costExempt`, `playerCantLoseGame`, `playerCantWinGame`, `playerCantLoseLife`, `playerLosesNoLifeToDamage` (a `lose-life` prohibition `from: "damage"`, asked only where damage dealt to a player would cost life, rule 120.3a), `playerLifeCantChange`, `playerCantSearchLibrary` and `targetsIgnoreHexproof` are the gates' helpers. `Game.canPayLife` takes a third argument: `"cost"`, `"mana-ability"` or `"not-a-cost"`, because only a cost to cast or activate is stopped.
- **`keyword-values.ts`** — Keywords with costs a static can give a card or a spell (theme 16): `KeywordCost` (a mana string, `"mana-cost"`, `{ manaCostLess }`), `KeywordValue` (one keyword with its cost), `KeywordCostsPaid` (the cast action's `offspring` and `replicate` counts, one per instance), `keywordCostString`. The printed field stays on the definition; `Game.handKeywordValues` and `Game.spellKeywordCosts` fold the grants (`grantsToHand`, `grantsToSpells.keywordValues`) beside it. The cast action's picks (`keywordCostsPick`, `emergeCostPick`) live only for the judging and paying of one cast, as `altCostPick` does.
- **`characteristics-patch.ts`** — What an effect can make a permanent into (theme 8): `TypePatch` and `ColorPatch` (layers 4 and 5, shared with `StaticAbility`), `CharacteristicsPatch<Pt>` (name, types, colours, abilities, base P/T; `Pt` an `EffectAmount` on a one-shot, a number on a copy exception), `ResolvedPatch`, `CopyExceptions<Pt>` (the patch plus `noManaCost`, `loyalty` — a copy's starting loyalty, the copiable `PtModifier.startingLoyalty` that `Game.moveObject` reads through `startingLoyaltyOverride` in place of the printed loyalty —, `thisAbility`, `legendRuleOff` and `ifCopied`), `StaticPt` (a static's base P/T value), `patchModifier` (a patch's `PtModifier` fields, the one path), `changesOnlyColors`, `changesTypes`.
- **`characteristics.ts`** — Conditions: `self-counters` takes `parity: "odd" | "even"` in place of `compare` (Sab-Sunen, Luxa Embodied: every kind together with no `counter`, zero even); `controls` reads its filter with the source, for a clause read against it (`blockingRelation`); `exiled-with-source`'s `cardTypes` (the card types among the cards still exiled with it, none from a face-down one); `attacked-you-last-turn`, `turn-history`'s `selfOnly`, `cards-in-graveyard`'s `excludeSelf`, `aggregate`'s `countsSelf` and `"prime"`, `controls`' `sharingAName`; `distinctNames` reads names through `namesOf` (a Room has one per unlocked door). `journalCount` reads an entry's `asDealt` as it reads `asEntered`. `commandersOnBattlefieldOrInCommandZone`, and the `self-devoured` condition. `ConditionOptions.outsideFold` is a triggered ability's or a resolving effect's condition, which takes no re-entrancy guard, so the source's own statics still apply to what it reads (Historian's Wisdom's host with the Aura's +2/+1). `Characteristics.landwalk` (rule 702.14, a static's `landwalk` as land filters, adding the `landwalk` keyword while any survives; `staticLandwalk` turns a land type alone with a keyword of its own into that keyword, so a chosen Island is islandwalk). `setBasePt` (layer 7b, each affected permanent by its own count of a counter — Spark Rupture) and `grantPtPerCount.anyCountersOnAffected`. `selfOutsideBattlefield` (Grist): a card's own types and base P/T off the battlefield, read by `outsideLayerFour` and the fold's off-battlefield P/T. `basePtCount` is the one read of a `setBasePtFromCount`, printed or granted, and the only one that knows `{ countersOnThis }`. The static fold's passes: unscoped, keyword-scoped, then `scopedByAbilities` ("creatures with no abilities", seeing what the others granted). A combat restriction scoped by power or toughness (`isPtScopedRestriction`) stays out of the fold: `restrictionsOf` applies it after, from `ptScopedRestrictionSources` (so a reader of `Characteristics.restrictions` directly, as the bots' estimates do, doesn't see it). `of-player`'s `"an-opponent"` holds if it does for any opponent still in the game. `setBasePtFromCount`'s `"chosen-number"` count (`chosenOnEnter`, 0 off the battlefield) and `toughnessMinusCount` (Shapeshifter's "*/7-*", a CDA, 2026-11-01). `emblemStatics(state, owner?)` (rules 113.6p, 114.4; 2026-11-01): an emblem's statics with its object as their source, so "you" and every scope read as a permanent's; the layer fold (`contributingStaticSources`), `playerStaticHolds`, `Game.activeStaticsOf` and, through `Game.staticSourceIds`/`staticsOfSource`, the hand size, prohibition, cost, play, graveyard-cast and damage-replacement scans read them, and `test/emblem-statics.test.ts` pins the fields against the pool's emblems. `most-common-color` (rule 105.2) is a `StaticCondition` answered here at layer 5 (`effectiveColors`), so a P/T static can ask it without folding. `computeCharacteristics(state, registry, id)` folds printed → layer 3 (text substitution) → layer 4 (type/subtype changes) → layer 5 (colour) → layer 6 (keyword grants/ability loss, and keyword counters — rule 122.1b, `KEYWORD_COUNTERS` — in timestamp order, rule 613.7: losing all abilities takes what was granted *before* it and none of what's granted after — `abilitiesLostAt`/`grantOutlastsLoss`/`modifierGrantApplies` — a static's grant dated by its source, an Aura's or Equipment's by when it attached, a keyword counter's by the latest counter of its kind, `GameObject.counterTimestamps`, 613.7c, and every modifier that grants something by its own `timestamp`) → layer 7b (a `setBasePtFromCount` CDA — a `CountSpec` counting a `CardFilter` over the battlefield or all graveyards, or the greatest mana value among matching permanents (`greatestManaValueOf`, Karn's — mana value being no layered characteristic), counted for the chosen player with `forChosenPlayer` (`countSide`; as `grantPtPerCount`'s is), applied in every zone per rule 604.3 — then `become`'s `setPt` (or, from an `become` given only one of the two, `PtModifier.setPower` / `setToughness` — base power or toughness alone, 2026-10-31) and statics' `setBasePt`, by timestamp) → layer 7c (counters) → layer 7d (P/T grants/modifiers — a `grantPtPerCount` scaling with a live count, or with `greatestCommanderManaValue`, the greatest mana value among the controller's commanders wherever they are: Tangleweave Armor; a hand size, `cardsInHandFor` — the counting player's own, their opponents' together or the affected creature's controller's — or a life total, unclamped per rule 107.1b, 2026-10-31), timestamp-ordered. `countValue`'s `"your-life-total"` is the life-total CDA (Serra Avatar). `Characteristics.restrictions`/`.protectionFrom` collect `StaticAbility.restrictions`/`.protection` (the combat ones, and `"doesnt-untap"`, which `Game`'s untap step reads through `restrictionsOf` — rule 502.3, Tractor Beam's enchanted permanent). **Layer 4 is its own fold, `layerFour`** (off the battlefield `outsideLayerFour`: the card's own, plus each `outsideBattlefield` static reaching a card its controller owns or a spell they control), because statics grant types to other permanents (`StaticAbility.addTypes`/`addSubtypes`/`setSubtypes` — Kudo's Bears, Ragost's Foods, Bello's creatures, Goddric's "is a Dragon"; such a static outlives its source losing its abilities, rule 613.6): the object's own type-changing modifiers (ordered by `PtModifier.timestamp`) and every type-granting static on the battlefield, in timestamp order, each static's scope matched against the types folded so far plus everything the previous pass added, repeated until the set of statics that apply settles — dependency order (rule 613.8) for effects that only add types, so Kudo still reaches a land animated after it arrived; a dependency loop settles in timestamp order. `effectiveTypes`/`effectiveSubtypes` read it (so every filter, target check and scope sees granted types); with no type-granting static on the battlefield it is the old self-contained read of the object's own modifiers. A re-entrant fold for the same object (a grant's condition or attachment clause reading back into it) answers with its own types only (`layer4InProgress`, a third re-entrancy guard beside the condition and CDA ones), which is what keeps two grants whose scopes read each other's grants from looping. `LayerFour.applied` records which statics reached the object there, and **`staticReaches`** — what the fold and `Game`'s granted-ability lookups ask — sends a static with a layer-4 part to exactly those for its later layers (rule 613.6). `staticAffects` resolves an `AffectSpec` scope (`"self"`/`"creatures-you-control"`/`"all-creatures"`/`"lands-you-control"`/`"attached"`, lord `subtype` clauses, and `"filter"` — any `CardFilter`, from the source's controller's perspective), given a `TargetView` by callers partway through the fold. Changeling (rule 702.73a) is a CDA, so `withChangeling` adds `EVERY_CREATURE_TYPE` to a creature's subtypes before any other layer-4 step, in every zone, from its copiable values — kept through a loss of abilities (layer 6 comes later), replaced by a later `setSubtypes`. `Characteristics` also carries `basePower`/`baseToughness`, the P/T after layer 7b. `hasManaAbility` (rule 605.1 — activated, `isManaAbility`, or triggered, `isTriggeredManaAbility`) and `hasAnyAbility` (rule 113 — `cards/define.ts`'s `printedHasAbility`, a table over every `CardDefinition` field, plus every kind of grant) read granted abilities through the memoized `abilityGrantSources`; a scope asking about the permanent it is granting to re-enters, which the `grantedAbilitiesInProgress` guard answers conservatively (printed abilities only), never cached. Layer 1 (copy) is handled upstream via `printedCardName`; layer 3 (an exchange of text boxes) via `rulesTextName`, which every keyword, static and CDA read goes through, while name, types, colors and P/T stay `printedCardName`'s. `spellHasSplitSecond`/`spellCantBeCountered` read a spell's printed split second / "can't be countered" and the `grantsToSpells` statics that give it (`spellGrantReaches` — "creature spells you control can't be countered", read by `Game.counterObject`). `staticConditionMet(state, registry, source, condition, opts?)` evaluates a `StaticAbility.condition` gate, checked *after* `staticAffects` confirms reachability, with a re-entrancy guard for mutually-conditional permanents; `not`, `all` and `any` compose the other kinds (`Game`'s resolution-time `conditionMet` recurses through the same three, so a `target` or `x` kind under them still answers). `journalCount` reads a `left` entry through the snapshot of the stint it names (603.10a). Dependency ordering between static effects is implemented for layer 4's additive grants and for **a static loss of all abilities** only (2026-10-28, B249): `StaticAbility.loseAllAbilities` is folded board-wide by `staticAbilityLosses` — an effect's loss first, then round by round each static no other remaining one reaches, a loop by timestamp (rule 613.8) — into each permanent's loss timestamp (half a step before the static's, so what the same static grants comes after it; kept per mutation epoch, and the cache check recomputes each epoch's first hit and one in 16 after, since every ability check asks it); `hasLostAbilities`/`abilitiesLostAt` take `(state, registry, object)` and read it beside the modifiers, and `staticOutlivesLoss` keeps an applied loss static's other parts in the later layers (613.6 — Humility made a creature). `entersWithoutAbilities` asks whether such a loss would reach a permanent about to enter, as it would exist there (614.12); `Game`'s `nextEnterChoice` and `enteringReplacementOf` then skip its own entering abilities (no "enters tapped", counters of its own or "as this enters" choice — an Aura's object and a battle's protector are still asked). "Is there any such static on the battlefield" is memoized on a mutation epoch that `invalidateComputedCache` bumps region or not (`anyLoseAllSource`, re-derived under `MTG_CACHE_CHECK`) — the scan inside `Game`'s battlefield loops made a 500-token board 150 times slower. The three whole-board lists of statics (`typeGrantSources`, `contributingStaticSources`, `abilityGrantSources`) are kept the same way through one `boardScan` (2026-11-10, the bot audit's S6: most reads come outside a region, and each walked the battlefield again — 17-28% of a bot's simulation on a wide board): on (state, registry, epoch), never inside a re-entrancy guard, sampled under the cache check. Because those outlive a region, the epoch also moves through `noteStateMutation()` (the epoch alone, an open region untouched) at the end of every effect instruction (`applyEffectSpec`) and every dispatched action — a write between regions that announces nothing (`grant-static`'s modifier) used to be covered by a region clearing as it opens. `Characteristics.extraBlocks` folds `blockAdditional` (a static's or a `block-additional` modifier's, an ability lost with the rest), read by `blockLimitOf` (509.1a). Layer 5 also folds a static's `addColors` and `setsChosenColor`, layer 4 its `loseLandTypes`. **Also owns the scoped computed-value cache**: `withComputedCache(fn)` opens a region (one per `legalActions` call / SBA sweep / `detectTriggers` event / `viewFor` / bot feature scan; and the pure combat and targeting reads open their own when none is active — `legalDefenders`, `whyCannotAttack`, `whyCannotBlock`, `mustAttack`, `legalTargets`, `isLegalTarget`, v1's `declareAttackers`/`declareBlockers` — which read every creature many times and were half a million uncached computes a four-player bot game, 2026-10-14) inside which characteristics, layer-4 results, the contributing- and type-granting-statics pre-scans (each card's relevant statics worked out once per name, `StaticsInfo`) and `Game.manaSources` memoize; `invalidateComputedCache()` must be called by any mutation reachable from inside a region (`emit` is the broad net; `moveObject` suspends the cache outright via `suspendComputedCache`), values computed during a condition evaluation, a CDA count or a layer-4 fold (each has a re-entrancy guard whose answers are conservative) are never cached, and `MTG_CACHE_CHECK=1` on the fuzzer turns every characteristics and layer-4 hit into a recompute-and-compare that throws on divergence. **Wrap new read-only hot paths in a region; never let a region span a mutation without an invalidate.** `Characteristics.toxic` is the total toxic value (rule 702.164b): the printed `CardDefinition.toxic` unless the permanent has lost its abilities, plus each `grantToxic` static reaching it. `spellGrantReaches` honours `grantsToSpells.firstEachTurn` (the first matching spell its controller cast this turn, read from `spellsCastThisTurnAs`). Beside `playerHasHexproof`, the facts about players read off the battlefield and `GameState.playerEffects`: `playerCantLoseGame` (a `cantLoseGame` static of theirs — Platinum Angel — or a `player-effect` naming them), `playerCantWinGame` (an opponent's `opponentsCantWinGame` static, or a `player-effect`) and `playerCantLoseLife` (rule 119.8). `staticConditionMet` reads `spells-cast-this-game` and `library-size`'s `compare`; a `chosen-on-enter` asked again as a triggered ability resolves (`sourceTimestamp` given) holds once its source has left the battlefield or come back as a new object — it says which abilities the permanent had, and one that triggered exists apart from it (rule 113.7a: Mirrodin Besieged destroyed in response); asked as it triggers for a source that has just left, it reads `LastKnownInfo.chosenOnEnter` (603.10a: Outpost Siege's "Dragons", destroyed along with the creatures). Any `+X/+Y` or `-X/-Y` counter changes P/T in layer 7c (`counterPtBonus`, rule 122.1a — Wall of Roots' -0/-1). `canAttackAttackersDespiteDefender` is a `canAttackAsThoughNoDefender: "players-who-attacked-you"` static's fold, read by `canAttackDespiteDefender(state, registry, id, defender?)` against the defender; the `exiled-with-source` condition reads `GameObject.exiledWithCount`; `turnStatOf` answers `"cards-to-graveyard-from-hand-or-library"` and `"cards-left-graveyard"`. `contributingStaticSources` also takes a `fromGraveyard` static from each living player's graveyard ("as long as this card is in your graveyard" — Wonder, Anger), and skips one on the battlefield. The `attacked-with-commander-this-turn` condition. The `speed` condition (rule 702.179 — `speedWithin`, 0 for none) and `grantPtPerCount.speed` read `speedOf`; a `"that-player"` one is answered only by a resolution (`Game`'s `conditionMet`). **Devotion** (rule 700.5, 2026-10-12): `devotionOf(state, registry, player, colors)` counts the coloured symbols among the mana costs (copy effects applied — `manaCostOverride`, the up face's own) of permanents a player controls that are any of `colors`, each symbol once (a `{W/B}` once for "white and black"), plus every `devotionBonus` static their permanents' text has (Altar of the Pantheon, kept through a loss of abilities — 700.5a). The `devotion` condition, the `{ devotionTo }` CDA count, the `{ devotionTo }` effect amount and the `{ devotionTo }` cost-reduction amount (`abilities.ts`'s `CostReductionAmount` — Drag to the Underworld) all read it; the condition counts its own source. **Domain** (an ability word, rule 207.2c, 2026-10-19): `basicLandTypesAmong(state, registry, player)` — the basic land types (205.3i) among lands a player controls, each once, read through `matchesFilter` as a `countOf` is so a CDA may use it; the `{ basicLandTypes: "you" }` effect amount, cost-reduction amount (`abilities.ts`'s `CostReductionAmount`) and CDA count all read it. `withChangeling` also adds a card's `alsoSubtypes` ("is also a Cleric, Rogue, Warrior, and Wizard" — the same kind of CDA, every zone). The `cards-in-graveyard` condition's `whose` reads an opponent's (`"an-opponent"`, each graveyard on its own) or any player's graveyard (`"any-player"`). **Merged permanents** (mutate, rule 702.140 / 730, 2026-10-20): `GameObject.merged` lists the components topmost first; `printedCardName` is the topmost's (730.2a), `rulesTextName` a merged key (`state.ts`'s `mergedTextName`, `MERGED_PREFIX`) that `CardRegistry.get` resolves to a combined definition — the topmost's with every component's keywords, activated, triggered and static abilities — so every ability read already sees all of them, and `textNameOf` names the merged key for trigger/activation records and the last-known `textName`. `Game.mergeMutatingSpell` merges (the `mutate-merge` effect, asked as a mode choice by `resolveMutatingSpell`); `moveObjectUncached` scatters the other components into the zone it left for (`scatterMergedComponents`). **Party** (rule 700.8, 2026-10-20): `partySizeOf(state, registry, player)` — up to one each of Cleric, Rogue, Warrior and Wizard among the creatures a player controls, a creature of several of those types filling the one role that makes the party largest (700.8b): a maximum matching kept as the set of reachable role masks, each token of a stack its own creature (capped at four); the `{ partySize: "you" }` effect amount, cost-reduction amount and CDA count, `grantPtPerCount.partySize` and the `full-party` condition (700.8c) read it. **Bestow** (rule 702.103, 2026-10-25): an object whose `GameObject.bestowed` is set starts layer 4 (and the off-battlefield read) from `cards/define.ts`'s `bestowedDefinition` — the card as an Aura enchantment, no creature types — rather than its printed definition (`printedDefinition`); `Game` sets the flag at the cast, keeps it from the stack onto the battlefield only, clears it as the spell begins resolving with its target illegal (702.103e, `unbestowIfTargetIllegal`) and in the state-based check that finds it unattached or on something it can't enchant (702.103f — a creature again, where it is). **Licids** (2026-10-26): a `GameObject.licid` adds a layer-4 modifier at its timestamp (`licidModifier`, beside reconfigure's in `typeModifiers`) — an enchantment, subtype Aura — and `Game` reads the Licid through `cards/define.ts`'s `licidDefinition` wherever it asks an Aura question of a printed definition (`attachPermanent`, the 704.5m and 704.5p checks); its `licid: true` ability is refused meanwhile (`whyCannotActivateAbility`), and the `end-licid-effect` action (`endLicidEffect`, 116.2c) clears it and unattaches. **An enchant ability an effect gives** (2026-11-05): `GameObject.enchantSwap` (the `swap-enchant` effect — Animate Dead, Necromancy, Cloudform) is "enchant creature put onto the battlefield with this" (`put`: one object in one stint) or any target spec, and with `becameAura` a third layer-4 modifier in `typeModifiers` (`becameAuraModifier`); `Game.attachmentDefinition` picks the bestowed, Licid or `becameAuraDefinition` definition for every Aura question, and `canEnchant` reads the swap before the target slot. An Aura whose slot is `card-in-graveyard` (`enchantsGraveyardCard`) is legally attached to that card in a graveyard (`enchantableObjects` offers graveyard cards to 303.4f's choice); `moveObject` unattaches what's on a card leaving a graveyard (`detachFrom`), `attachedHost` reads it there, and `staticAffects`' `attached` scope skips it. `withDevotionUnseen(ids, fn)` leaves objects out while `Game` judges an entering permanent's replacements (rule 614.12 — a God's own pips don't count as it enters), dropping the computed cache on the way in and out. Layer 4 also folds a static's `setTypes` / `removeTypes` (card types replaced or taken away, with the subtypes that went only with them — rule 205.1a: a God that isn't a creature, Arixmethes' "it's a land", Swift Reconfiguration's Vehicle artifact) and `addsChosenCreatureType` (its source's `chosenCreatureType` — Titan of Littjara); `hasLayerFourPart` counts all three. `KEYWORD_COUNTERS` has `shadow` (rule 122.1b). `playerLifeCantChange` and `playerHasProtectionFromEverything` read `GameState.playerEffects`; `playerCantLoseLife` is true for a player whose life can't change too. **Layer 5** is `effectiveColors(state, registry, object)`: the object's own colour modifiers and, on the battlefield, every static with a `setColors` (`colorSetSources`, memoized per region — a Zendikon's "is a 3/3 black Ooze creature"), in timestamp order; a re-entrant read for the same object answers without statics (`layer5InProgress`), and `state: null` applies the modifiers alone. Protection in layer 6 folds statics' `protection` (a `chosenColor` read off the source's `chosenOnEnter`, `staticProtection`) and modifiers' `protection` (lost to a later loss of all abilities like a granted keyword); a `doesntRemoveSource` instance (rule 702.16n) is in `protectionFrom.colors` and also in `protectionFrom.sparing`, with every other instance's colours, which only an Aura's attachment reads. Rule 305.7 (B253): `landTextLost` — a land whose land types an effect set to basic ones has lost its rules-text abilities, folded into `abilitiesLostAt` as a loss before every grant (`layerSixLossAt` is the layer-6 part, which alone takes the intrinsic mana abilities); `withoutTextLostSources` drops a land's own layer-4 statics under a land-type setter (613.8a); `entersWithoutAbilities` counts a setter too (614.12). `grantedStatics` feeds `contributingStaticSources` (each with the grant's 613.7a timestamp), `abilityGrantSources`, `playerStaticHolds` and the 7b sets (a granted `setBasePtFromCount`, not a CDA — 604.3a). Layer 7d (rule 613.4d) is `PtModifier.switchPt`, swapped after every counter and modification (the `switch-pt` effect, 2026-10-29). `ConditionOptions.thatPlayer` (the trigger's or resolution's "that player", passed by `interveningIfMet` as it triggers and resolves and by a resolution's fallback) and `asPlayer` (an `of-player`'s seat: `evalStaticCondition`'s `you`); `conditionPlayerOf` resolves a `ConditionPlayer` (that player, active player, a target slot or its controller, the defending player of the source's attack — rule 802.2a), `lifeWithinBounds` a `life-total`'s bounds with `{ startingPlus }`; `player-compare` counts its source on its controller's side (2026-10-30). `meld-ready` and `meldPartners` (2026-10-31): a meld card's "if you both own and control ~ and a [type] named X" — the source on the battlefield and a matching permanent, both owned and controlled by `you`. `chosenProtectionQualities` (2026-10-31): a protection's colours and card types with "the chosen color" / "the chosen card type" (`chosenCardType`) read off its source's `chosenOnEnter` — a permanent's `protection` and a player's `playerProtection` alike. `castGrantReaches` honours `firstEachTurn` as a spell is cast (2026-10-31). Statics granted by statics (`StaticAbility.grantsStatic`, layer 6): `grantedStatics` returns them beside a `grant-static`'s (`grantedStaticsTimed` carries each one's timestamp, the later of the object's and the grant's — 613.7a), matched against `staticGrantSources` (gated by the epoch-memoized `anyStaticGrantSource`, guarded by `staticGrantsInProgress`); `contributingStaticSources` folds them in and doesn't cache a list built while that guard is up. `turnSpellGrantReaches` / `turnCastGrantReaches` answer for a `grant-spells-this-turn`'s record (`GameState.spellGrantsThisTurn`, a `SpellGrantThisTurn`): on the stack a `next` grant is only its `usedBy` spell's, before the cast an unused one reaches the card that would use it up; `spellCantBeCountered` reads its `cantBeCountered` (Overmaster), and a `grantsToSpells` `firstEachTurn` takes a `castFrom` or `manaFrom` filter, read on each earlier spell as it was cast (Wild-Magic Sorcerer, Rain of Riches) (2026-10-31). A `hand-size` condition's `filter` counts only matching cards (Land Grant) (2026-10-31). `cantHaveKeywords` (a static's or a modifier's) is applied last in the keyword fold and in the keyword-scoped pass's `targetKeywords`, over every timestamp, and `grantableKeywords` is what a resolving grant asks so that one made meanwhile isn't created; a loss of hexproof takes "hexproof from" through `AppliedEffect.hexproofFromAt`, `-Infinity` for a permanent's own (702.11e), except a printed "can't be the target of …" marked `notHexproof` (Shanna, Sisay's Legacy), which `isQualifiedHexproof` also judges alone (2026-11-02). `LayerFour.addedSupertypes` holds what `addSupertypes` statics add over `supertypesOf`, and `effectiveSupertypes` is the read everything uses: after its first pass the fold shows each scope the supertypes the last pass ended with, not those so far, because a "nonbasic lands" scope depends on what adds basic (613.8a), and `withoutTextLostSources` asks the same of a land's own view (2026-11-02). `playerCantSearchLibrary` (a `cantSearchLibraries` static or `player-effect`, per searcher and per what caused the search), `playerIgnoresStatic`/`staticIgnoreCost` (rule 116.2d) and `playerDamageLifeFloor` (the effect's and the static's, the highest); `playerStaticHolds`' test also gets the source. `staticProtection` also reads `colorsAmong` (`colorSetAmongPermanents`, layer 5's colours, so no cycle with layer 6) and `doesntRemoveAuras` (`sparing`'s `aura: "all"`, which `protectionBlocks` never counts against an attached Aura).
- **`target.ts`/`targeting.ts`** — a `permanent` slot's `playerWith: { turnStat }` is its `orPlayer` part's own condition (that player's turn stat above 0, through `turnStatOf` — Needle Drop's "any target that was dealt damage this turn"); `defendingPlayerOf` (the `"creature-defending-player-controls"` and `whose: "defending-player"` specs) reads the source's live `attacking`, and, once it has left combat, this combat's `attacked` journal entry for it while the combat phase lasts (rule 508.5 — Armix, Filigree Thrasher, 2026-11-11). The `{ kind: "ability" }` target's `loyalty: true` takes only a loyalty ability (rule 606.2), read from `GameObject.loyaltyAbility`, which `Game` stamps on an activated ability's stack object when its cost has a loyalty symbol (Verdant Command, 2026-11-11). `groupReadsCastAmount`: a spell's `any-number` bound other than `"x"` is an amount `Game.effectiveTargetSpecs` reads for the cast (`spellSpecsWithAmounts`, its kick count passed in, since the spell isn't on the stack yet), so every reader of a cast's specs sees numbers. `one-of` is one slot over several kinds of object, each part a plain spec with its own filter and zone (a spell or creature, abilities and a filtered spell, a graveyard or exile); a target is legal when any part accepts it, and an effect on one says what happens in each zone. `card-in-exile`'s `thisWay` on a reflexive ability is fixed to `among` by `Game.bindExiledThisWay` as it triggers (rule 603.12: it chooses targets after the resolution). `permanent`'s `orPlayer` with `typesAnyOf` or `filter.anyOf` is one slot over players and permanents, each part its own filter ("any target that isn't a Dragon", rule 115.4); `describeTargetSpec` says the player part after a `whose` and names `anyOf`'s parts. A group's label is plural past one (`pluralTargetLabel`: "3 creatures", "any number of creatures you control", "instant or sorcery spells"; 2026-11-11). `card-in-exile` is a card in exile (`whose` its owner, `faceUp`, `linked` to the source's battlefield stint through `exiledWith`/`exiledUntil` — `exiledWithTargetSource`, its last stint once it has left — and a filter a face-down card never matches, rule 406.3a); `legalTargets` scans exile only for it. An `ability` slot's `whose` takes `"opponent"` ("you don't control"), and a `stack` slot's `whose` and `targeting` read its controller and what it aims at (`stackObjectTargetsMatch`, a filter's `targets` for an ability as well). An `other` slot's `sameObjectAllowed` lets a `sameController` slot name the object its relation names (two instances of "target", rule 115.3). `TargetRef` (player or object) and `TargetSpec` (`"any-target"`/`"creature"`/`"nonblack-creature"`/`"creature-you-control"`/`"creature-an-opponent-controls"`/`"player"`/`"opponent"`/`"opponent-whose-turn-it-is"`/`"creature-or-player"`/`"permanent"`/`"nonland-permanent"`/`"nonland-permanent-an-opponent-controls"`/`"land"`/`"artifact"`/`"artifact-an-opponent-controls"`/`"artifact-or-enchantment"`/`"artifact-enchantment-or-nonbasic-land-an-opponent-controls"`/`"creature-or-enchantment"`/`"attacking-or-blocking-creature"`/`"spell"`/`"creature-spell"`/`"noncreature-spell"`/`"instant-or-sorcery-in-your-graveyard"`/`"instant-or-sorcery-spell"`/`"instant-or-sorcery-spell-or-triggered-ability"` (one slot over both kinds of stack object — Spider-Sense) (the spell literals read the face on the stack, `printedCardName` — an Adventure cast as its instant half is an instant spell, rule 715.3b), plus the structured `permanent`/`spell`/`card-in-graveyard` forms, each with a filter and a `whose`, and `ability {whose?, abilityKind?, sourceTypes?, sourceFilter?, otherSource?}` — an activated or triggered ability on the stack, "you control", only "triggered" (a chapter ability counts, rule 714.2b), "from an enchantment source" (its source's types as it is, or as it last existed on the battlefield — `abilitySourceTypes`), "from a colorless source" / "from another legendary source that's not a commander" (`sourceFilter`, a `CardFilter` over the same read of the source, `abilitySourceAs`, its snapshot handed to `matchesFilter`; `otherSource`: not the choosing ability's own source — Abstruse Archaic, The Peregrine Dynamo) — a `permanent`'s `"trigger-player"` is "that player" of a trigger, `TargetSource.triggerPlayer`, and its `attacking: "trigger-player"` a creature attacking that player — Echoing Assault; its `orPlayer: "opponent" | "any"` lets a player fill the slot too — Price of Betrayal's "…, or opponent"). `{ kind: "any-number", of, min?, max? }` is "any number of target …": a group, usually the last slot (one anywhere else — two in a list, Filigree Vector, or one with a slot after it, Kamahl's Will — has its members ended by an empty slot in the flat list, `targetSpans`/`nextTargetSlot`/`groupMembers`, and `for-each-target`'s `group` reaches a later one), filled with as many distinct targets as the player picks (between `min` and `max` — `groupBounds`; `"x"` for either is the spell's X, fixed by `specsAtX` at the X cast, rule 601.2b–c; a whole multiple of X, `{ product: ["x", 2] }`, is "up to twice X", read through `xMultiple` — Erebos's Intervention), and judged — at the cast and again on resolution — against `concreteTargetSpecs(specs, chosen.length)`, so its shape comes from the choice, never from the board; effects reach its members with `as-player` (`effect` with another player as the controller, `asPlayer` on the context), `for-each-permanent` (`effect` done to each permanent a filter matches, nobody choosing, a stack woken first  rule 700.3b; `for-permanent` its one step), `for-each-target` or a flicker's `{ from }`. `isLegalTarget`/`legalTargets` take a `forPlayer` and an optional `TargetSource` (`{colors, types}`), refuse any object a player who has left the game owns or controls (rule 800.4a — the engine leaves those where they are), and enforce protection (`protectionBlocks`, also used by `dealDamage`/`whyCannotBlock`/`attachPermanent`), hexproof from a quality (`hexproofFromBlocks` — `Characteristics.hexproofFrom`, matched as protection is, against an opponent's spell or ability only; rule 702.11d — and each printed "can't be the target of …" that isn't the keyword, its `qualified` instances judged alone: spells only or abilities only, told apart by `TargetSource.spell`, which `cardSource` sets; any player's; or every spell or ability when it names no quality — rules 601.2c, 602.2b), a card in a graveyard under a `graveyardCardsUntargetable` static (`graveyardCardShielded`) and a player's hexproof (`playerHasHexproof` in `characteristics.ts`: a one-shot grant, or a `playerHexproof` static — Shalai). Checked on cast and again at resolution (all-illegal ⇒ fizzle); the slots found illegal beside a legal one ride along as `ResolutionContext.illegalTargets` (rule 608.2b), honoured so far only by the control-changing effects. `target-count.ts` is the pure distinct-target arithmetic behind "for each target" costs: `distinctTargetCount`, `targetCountBounds` (fewest/most distinct targets a legal choice can have), `fitTargetCount` (an `any-number` group trimmed or grown from its end), and `maxXForTargets`/`minXForTargets`/`targetCountAtX` for X against the count. A range may count from a slot on (`TargetCountRange.from` — an X-tied group's members after another slot, Spinning Wheel Kick, its fixed slot free to name a member too, rule 115.3); `rangeTargetCount` is the count every reader takes. `divisionOf(divided, targets, chosen)` is the one division check for spells and abilities; `specsCappedByDivision(specs, divide)` caps a divided group at its total (at least 1 each). Relations among targets that need the board — an `other` slot's `differentController` ("controlled by different players") and an `any-number` group's `maxTotalPower` ("total power N or less") — read `TargetFacts` (who controls a target, its power): `stackSize` (a compacted stack's tokens, so "another target" may name it once per token, `targetUnits` counting a group's candidates that way) and the rest: `slotOptions` / `targetsFillable` / `otherSlotConflict` / `groupPowerExceeded` take them optionally (a chooser without them narrows nothing; the engine still refuses a breach), `stateTargetFacts(state, registry)` builds them for the engine, and the client builds its own off the view. A `permanent` or `card-in-graveyard` spec's `whose: { seat }` binds a slot to one player — `playerAtSeat(turnOrder, you, seat)`, 0 being you — for "for each opponent, up to one target … that player controls" (Dismantling Wave, Windgrace's Judgment, The Balrog of Moria: seats 1–3) and "for each player … in that player's graveyard" (Afterlife from the Loam: seats 0–3); a target under anyone else's control by resolution is illegal, as the printed card's would be. `specSide` reads seat 0 as yours and the rest as opponents'. An X-tied `any-number` group may sit on an activated ability too (Shigeki's channel). On a triggered or activated ability a group's `max`/`min` (and `maxTotalManaValue`) may be any `EffectAmount`: `groupReadsAmount` says so, and `specsWithAmounts(specs, value)` reads them into numbers once, as the targets are chosen — `Game.triggerSpecsWithAmounts` as a trigger goes on the stack (603.3d; again on resolution for the totals), `Game.activatedSpecsAt` as an ability is activated, before its costs (602.2b), feeding the offer, the check and the activation alike. `maxTotalManaValue` and `differentManaValues` are checked through `TargetFacts.manaValueOf` (`groupManaValueBroken`, inside `groupPowerExceeded`). A `castModal` mode may hold a group where no later mode chosen with it targets; `fitModalXGroup` (target-count.ts) fits a driver's picks and X together for it. `targetsFillable` holds a group to its floor: one needing more distinct targets than there are isn't fillable. A target filter is matched with the targeting permanent as its `FilterContext.source` (Giant Beaver's "target creature that saddled it this turn"). A player with protection from everything (a `player-effect`) can't be targeted by anyone, their own spells included (rule 702.16j). A `{ kind: "stack", spells, abilities, singleTarget? }` spec takes a spell or an ability on the stack in one slot — "target spell or ability **with a single target**" counts the targets chosen as it went on the stack (rule 115.9a). The `change-targets` effect changes a stack object's targets as it resolves (rule 115.7 — `Game.changeStackTargets`): every slot's spec, current target and other legal targets come from `Game.stackTargetSlots` (shared with a copy's new targets; only the filled, chosen slots, so each one's "other than slot n" is renumbered to them with `target.ts`'s `renumberOtherSlots`, and an automatically filled slot it must differ from is kept out of its options), judged for that object's controller; it asks a `choose-targets` decision with `current`, a `prompt`, `changeOne` for "change a target" and `targetsFor` (`GameState.pendingRetarget`), or applies a forced answer at once, and `retargetStackObject` announces each new target. Protection's quality reads from `qualityMatches`: a colour, a card type, a filter (`Characteristics.protectionFrom.filters`, and `boundFilters` for a static's, read with its own source: Riders of Gavony's "of the chosen type"), an empty filter (everything, 702.16j, needing no object) and players (`players`, 702.16k: `by`, the one targeting or the Aura's controller, else the source's controller, as it last existed (`TargetSource.lastKnown`) or, off the battlefield and stack, its owner). `CardFilter.isSpell` with `TargetSource.spell` (`FilterContext.asSpell`) is "from spells": `Game.enteringSource` is an Aura or Equipment put onto the battlefield, not a spell. `protectionBlocks`'s `enchanting` is the Aura whose attachment is the question: an instance that "doesn't remove" that Aura is left out, by its colours and card types (702.16n). `protectionQualitiesMatch` asks whether a set of qualities would match a source (the bots' colour choice). `sameController` on an `other` slot or an `any-number` group (`true`, or `{ slot }` for a group tied to an earlier slot) is "that player controls" / "from a single graveyard" / "from their graveyard": `concreteTargetSpecs` makes each member an `other` with it, `slotOptions` and `otherSlotConflict` judge it through `playerOfTarget` (a player target itself, a permanent's controller, a card's owner), and `Game.targetLegality` rechecks it as the spell resolves (608.2b). `shares` (a `TargetShare`, 2026-10-31) on an `other` slot is "…that share a card type / a creature type / no creature types" (`among` the card types that count, `none` the negation), and an `any-number` group's `equalToughness` / `differentNames` "with equal toughness" / "with different names": `slotOptions` narrows by them and `otherSlotConflict` / `groupRelationBroken` judge them through `TargetFacts`' optional `shareTypes`, `toughnessOf` and `nameOf` (`stateTargetFacts` has all three, over `targeting.ts`'s `typesShare`; without them, as on the client, they narrow nothing), and `Game.targetLegality` — which now takes the stack object's `targetZones` — rechecks them as it resolves: a pair no longer sharing are both illegal, one already illegal read through `lastKnownOfTarget`; a broken group, over its still-legal members, makes every member illegal. `attachedToThan` on an `other` slot ("…target Equipment attached to that creature", 2026-11-01) is judged the same way through `TargetFacts.attachedTo`; as it resolves, only that slot goes illegal — no longer attached, or its host slot illegal. `playerProtectionBlocks` (2026-10-31): a player's protection from a quality — every `StaticAbility.playerProtection` of a permanent they control, matched as a permanent's protection is (`qualityMatches`, whose filters now read a `filterSource` — Runed Halo's `chosenName`), the chosen colour or card type off its permanent (`chosenProtectionQualities`); asked by a player target here, and by `Game.canEnchantPlayer` and player damage (702.16b, c, e). `opponent-compared`'s `comparedTo: "active-player"` measures from the active player rather than the targeting one and stays a requirement as it resolves (`resolvingTargetSpec` leaves it whole): the Oaths.
- **`mana.ts`** — `SpentMana`, `ManaSpentQuery` and `spentManaCount` are the one record and read of what paid for a spell or activated ability (theme 13: `GameObject.manaSpent`, `CardFilter.manaSpent`, the `{ manaSpentOf }` amount, the `ability-mana-spent` condition). `ManaProductionRule` is what a tapped permanent makes instead (`StaticAbility.manaProduction`, `PlayerEffect.manaProduction`, `Game.manaProductionRules`). `ManaProductionRule` (what a tapped land makes instead, `Game.landManaInsteadFor`) and `differentManaSplits`: the ways to add "two mana of different colors" (`add-mana`'s `oneOf` with `different`, the units of each colour — 2 and `amount: 4` for Open the Omenpaths), each pairing once. `colorSymbols`: a cost's symbols of some colours, a hybrid or Phyrexian one once, for devotion and chroma. `manaCostReductions` and `reducedBy`: a cost reduced by a mana cost's symbols, its hybrid halves chosen (rules 702.48c, 118.7e). `repeatedManaCost` (`may.costTimes`). `ManaRestriction.purposes` (`ManaSpendPurpose`) names what a filter can't: the special actions, a disturb cost, equip and power-up abilities, a level bar, a cost with `{X}`. `SpendAs` is `WideSpendAs` or a `SpendUnitAs` (one type of mana as a colour — Sunglasses of Urza). `ManaOrigin` carries the permanent and its stint (`manaFrom.fromSource`, "using mana produced by [this]"). `Color`/`ManaType`/`ManaPool`/`ManaUnit`, `parseManaCost` (generic, `{X}`, `{C}` colorless, hybrid `{W/U}`, twobrid `{2/W}`, Phyrexian `{W/P}`, snow `{S}` as `ManaCost.snow` — rule 107.4h: paid only with mana a snow source made, the `ManaUnit.from` supertype; `mana-payment.ts`'s `planSnowPayment` pays those pips first, from floating snow mana or one activation per pip of a snow `ManaSource` (`snow: true`), every combination tried until the rest plans, and `spendFromPool` gives typed pips the non-snow units first; the `manaSpentOf` amount's `from` counts the snow mana spent), `manaValue`, `poolTotal`, `poolCounts`. **A player's mana pool is a list of `ManaUnit`s, not a count per colour** (`poolCounts` rebuilds the count view for the player view and the payment planner): a unit carries where it came from — a `ManaRestriction` on what it may be spent on (rule 106.6b — Cavern of Souls; or, with `notSpell`, a deny-list — "can't be spent to cast nonartifact spells", which pays for anything but such a spell, abilities and ward costs included), a `ManaSpendRider` that fires when it's spent (Path of Ancestry — its trigger object the spell it paid for, "that spell" as a `"trigger-spell"` fixed as the rider is placed: Primal Wellspring copies it even once countered; with `orAbility` an activated ability it paid for too, bound as its trigger object once the ability is on the stack — `PendingTrigger.awaitsActivation`, Sunken Palace), `persists` for mana that survives the end of a step (Savage Ventmaw), `uncounterable` for "and that spell can't be countered", `from` for what made it (a `ManaOrigin` — the source's types, subtypes and supertypes as it made the mana, which a spell keeps as `GameObject.manaSpentFrom` and the `manaFrom` filter clause reads: "if mana from an artifact was spent to cast it"; and how much of each colour, `GameObject.manaSpentByColor`, which the `manaSpentOfColors`/`manaSpentOfOneColor` clauses read — adamant) — and a count can carry none of it. The pool is empty almost all the time, since the engine auto-pays; it fills only when someone activates a mana ability by hand, which is exactly where an untagged pool would drop the restriction. The restriction is enforced twice on purpose: `planManaPayment` won't *tap* a source whose mana couldn't pay for the thing being paid for, and `spendFromPool` won't *spend* such a unit — and the latter spends restricted units first, because an unrestricted one can pay for anything later in the same cost. `ManaRestriction.castFrom` (`ManaCastFrom`) is where the spell is cast from ("from your graveyard", "from exile", "other than your hand", "with flashback"), `notCastFrom` the deny-list; `ManaSpendPurpose` has `"kicked-spell"`, `"foretell"`, `"cumulative-upkeep"` and `"cost-with-c"`. `ManaUnit.spellGains` carries `add-mana`'s spell-gains rider to the spend, and `spellEntersWith` its counters (`GameObject.entersWithCounters`' `amountAsItEnters`), those units spent ahead of plain mana when they reach the spell.
- **`controller.ts`** — `PlayerController.act(view)` is the single entry point: when `view.state.awaiting` is set it must return that declaration, otherwise any legal priority action. `AutomaticController` always passes/keeps its hand. `ScriptedController` plays a queue plus assignable `*Fn` callbacks (what tests use). `RandomController` picks uniformly from `legalActions()` — the engine's fuzzer (see `random-demo.mjs`); it builds the six priority actions itself and gets every *decision* answer from that kind's `decisions/` module via `randomAnswerFor`, handing it a `RandomSource` (`random`/`pickIndex`/`pickTargets`). One fuzzing guard, not a rule (`idleRepeat`): an `{X}` ability that can only be activated for X = 0 isn't activated again while one is on the stack — Helix Pinnacle's is free and legal any number of times, and uniform picks stacked it without end — and no other non-mana ability past three of it on the stack (Hyalopterous Lemure's {0} stacked 150 deep, fuzzer seed 40). **The exact sequence of draws against that source is what makes a seed replay identically**, so changing the order or count of calls in one of those arms silently re-points every fuzzer seed. `HeuristicBotController` extends `AutomaticController` with a v1 "plays a sensible game, not a good one" policy (land first, else the highest-mana-value affordable spell, else a **non-mana** ability, else pass; attack with every eligible creature, each at the lowest-life defender *it* may legally attack that can't profitably block it (`defendersFor` — goad and "can't attack you" differ per creature — and `wouldDieAttacking`, a crude "can anyone there kill it" check; a creature every defender could eat stays home — unless everything at one player is lethal through their blockers, `wideLethal`, when it all goes there); block favourable trades, take a free block (a blocker that survives the attacker stops its damage for nothing; not a deathtouch or menace attacker), and chump-block lethal), owning its own `CardRegistry` so it can read mana values and computed P/T. It **aims** by `target-polarity.ts` ("v1.5"): each slot's target is the best on the side its effect belongs on — removal at the opponents' most valuable permanent, a pump at its own best creature, an "up to one" help with only opponents to point at left empty — for casts, activations, trigger targets (`chooseTargets`), an Aura entering without being cast (`chooseEnchant`) and a Clone's copy (`chooseCopy`: the most valuable, never a legend it already controls); and a harmful slot passes over a permanent our own destroy or exile on the stack is already taking while something else on the opponents' side is left (`doomedByOurStack`: two Summon: Bahamut chapter triggers no longer both take one Serra Angel), a permanent the damage already on the stack kills among them; a damage slot — a trigger's too, its amount read by `target-polarity.ts`'s `pendingTargetDamage` (a fixed number, X, Terror of the Peaks' snapshotted power, Scourge of Valkas's count of Dragons) — ranks what it kills first with the damage already on the stack at each permanent added (`damageOnStack`), so a second trigger finishes what the first started — and the first plans for its siblings still waiting to go on the stack (`waitingTriggerDamage`, `planDamage`: most valuable first, each opposing permanent the chunks can kill takes the cheapest set of them that does, so two Terror of the Peaks triggers of 2 both go at a 4/4); and it leaves alone a spell or ability whose only legal targets are on the wrong side (a counterspell with only its own spell on the stack), unless the spec names that side. It used to take the first legal target — players in turn order, then the battlefield oldest-first — and hit its own side 22% of the time; aimed, it benches 30.0% against three unaimed copies at four players (even 25%). Its other choices read `effect-worth.ts`: a "you may" or punisher is taken when what it does (less its cost) beats what declining lets happen, a modal choice takes its best modes, a sacrifice or an edict gives up the cheapest permanent (`targetValue`, a token below a card), a discard throws surplus lands and what the board can't cast soon (`chooseBottomOfHand` with the lands in play), and of the abilities it could activate it takes the one worth most, never one worth less than nothing — Viscera Seer's scry for a creature, a life payment down to nothing. Mana abilities are never activated on their own (`isManaOnlyAbility`): casting auto-pays, so floating mana gains nothing, strands the source, and — once bot moves are paced out one at a time — shows up as a land flipping sideways for no reason between a spell being cast and that spell resolving. Already-attached Equipment is re-equipped only onto a creature ranking clearly above its host (`targetValue` beyond `REATTACH_MARGIN` — Greaves onto the six-drop just cast), so a move can never undo itself, and one ability of one permanent is activated at most `MAX_ACTIVATIONS_PER_TURN` (4) times a turn — without both, Lightning Greaves' Equip {0} kept a bot in one main phase forever. It's the base class of both searching bots (`bot/` below) and the fallback under them, so every decision their search doesn't improve on is still this — including, now, the opening hand: `mulligan`/`chooseBottomOfLibrary` are overridden here and delegate to `bot/mulligan.ts`. Its land drop (`bestLand`) looks at each land once per decision (`castableAfterPlay` remembers its answers for the board it's on, by land card and face, until the next `act`, so v2's land search reuses v1's looks) and plays the land after which the spell it would cast first (the dearest, as `act` picks) leaves another castable once resolved (`castTogether` — with Hinterland Harbor its only green source, a Mountain's Temur Battlecrier left nothing for Sakura-Tribe Elder where a Forest's did), then the one that lets it cast the most different spells this turn (the two together a `LandReach`, compared by `compareReach`; looking past a colour the land asks for as it enters), then — with a landfall permanent of ours out — a fetch it would crack at once with a land left to find (`crackedOnPlay`), two landfall triggers to any other land's one, then one that enters tapped as things stand (`entersTapped`: always, or a check land whose condition fails now — a tapped land costs nothing on a turn the untapped one casts nothing more); "cast" counts only what it would cast (`wouldCast`, the filter `act` casts from — Sticky Fingers with only an opponent's creature to enchant doesn't count), then the one unlocking the most coloured pips in hand no land in play pays; it asks the engine through `ControllerView.legalActionsAfter` (optional — what would be legal after an action, or several in order, on a throwaway copy; `Game.controllerView`, the live rooms' view, offers it, and the bot tools use that view too; `viewAfter` is the same copy's whole view, for a look that must answer what the copy asks, and `continueAfter`, asked of such a copy, steps it on while keeping the look's own events — a resolution under way reads them), so a view without it falls back to pips alone. v2's land search takes v1's land first, so it wins every tie, and searches only the lands that reach furthest this turn (`castableAfterPlay`'s `LandReach`: two spells cast together, then the most one at a time; looking past a shock land's "pay 2 life?" as paid, and past a free fetch's crack (`fetchCrack`), then past whatever the land put on the stack, resolved with every player passing and our own choices answered as the bot will (`settledFrom`) — so Wooded Foothills counts the untapped Forest it finds and Evolving Wilds the tapped one, and a scry land or Bojuka Bog no longer reads as casting nothing while its own trigger waits), since its rollouts pass our seat and never see that cast; looked at and searched once per land *name*, two Forests in hand reaching and scoring the same (the look runs outside the search budget). The look steps one copy of the game forward (`viewAfter` once, then `carryOn` — `continueAfter` — from each copy) instead of replaying its whole path from the real game at each step: 7.5 → 4.3 ms a look, the same decisions to the event (2026-10-13). The Ring tempting it (a `choose-permanents` whose `then` is `become-ring-bearer`) picks a Ring-bearer that can attack and deal damage, an evasive one first, then the most combat damage (`bestRingBearer`; it took the first creature on the battlefield) — v2 inherits it, its search offering no other candidate for a choice of exactly one. A scry or surveil (`chooseScry`) sends away what `scry-pick.ts` says to — v2's search rarely reaches the next draw, so it ties to this too. A creature that sacrifices itself to fetch a land (Sakura-Tribe Elder) is due at the end step of the player whose turn comes right before ours, with fewer than seven lands (`isCreatureFetchDue`); v2 takes it then without weighing it, as it does a fetch land, since the evaluation kept the 0/2 over the land; and at any other moment with nothing on the stack, outside a combat it may be blocking in, both bots hold it (`holdsCreatureFetch`; in our own turn, unless a landfall permanent of ours is out), since its land enters tapped all the same and cracking it sooner only gives up blocking — once `earlyMana` counted the land, the search cracked it in its own main phase and in another player's upkeep. Both bots also sequence a turn's casts by rules ahead of the search, whose rollouts pass our seat and never see a later cast: a payoff goes first (`payoffFirst`) — a "whenever you cast" one when another spell still fits after it, one that counts the turn's spells (`spellCountPayoff`, Flurry's `nthEachTurn`) before any other, and while one of ours waits for its spell, not a payoff that targets nothing when a spell it would copy is castable; v2 tries each sacrifice (up to five) for an offer whose effect reads the sacrificed creature (`withSacrificeChoices`: Felothar, the Fling family), and an offer whose effect doesn't read it pays with the cheapest, as v1 does (`cheapestPermanents`, now `protected`; 2026-10-10, capture AN5SU t13, where the last eligible was the commander); v2 scores a land drop it would crack at once as the drop and the crack together (`scoreLandDrop`, through `simulatePlan`), since its rollouts never crack it; v2 records each such cast (`acted`) so the window after it passes while it resolves, and `castPayoff` counts a payoff of ours on the stack (the `"acting"` rollout it turns on is for our own turn only, with a spell castable now that the payoff's trigger would see — the bot audit's S5, 2026-11-10); and an enters payoff (`entersPayoffFilters`: Dragon Tempest's flyers and Dragons) when a creature it would see is still castable once it has resolved; a cost reducer goes first (`reducerFirst`) when, resolved and its creature type named, a spell castable now still is, where that spell first would leave the reducer uncastable (Urza's Incubator, then Miirym); a hideaway land's "play the exiled card" goes first (`hideawayFirst`) when its condition holds and `chooseCastNow` takes the card, and a spell castable now whose payment would tap the land or its mana is still castable once the activation has resolved (Mosswort Bridge, then Utvara Hellkite); and a landfall permanent goes before the land drop and before a free fetch (`isLandfallPermanent`). The evaluation's `counters` term counts a station card's charge counters only up to its highest station threshold (`features.ts`'s `chargeCap`), unless the card reads its charge counters itself (The Eternity Elevator), so stationing past it reads as only the tapped creature. And a tapped creature is a blocker gone until our next untap step, as an attacker is: on our own turn, when the opponents could kill us with nothing left home but not with what we hold now, v2 plays each cast or activation out to an empty stack and ranks one that leaves the crackback lethal below every safe move (`tapsIntoCrackback`, the attack declaration's `crackbackLethal` with its `grown` rule — station, tap costs, convoke, a mana creature its payment taps, a sacrifice), and v1 skips an activation whose cost taps or sacrifices the blockers that make it lethal (`activationTapsIntoCrackback`). `combat-math.ts`'s `crackback` takes the blockers to count as gone (`lost`, or `"all"`). More rules around the search, all 2026-10-07: the priority search sets its rollout policy (`decisionRollout`, `"acting"` with a cast payoff out) before scoring anything, and under it a candidate that only ties with passing wins the tie only if it's worth something scored alone, with nobody acting after it (`scoreAlone`; 2026-10-10: a spell mustn't lose half a card's weight on its own, an activation must gain; captures KEYZZ t6, Magmaquake at X=0, and WAMFR t54, Lightning Greaves' equip {0} looping; the replay reads `lastRefusedTies`), so the pass baseline, the land search and the batches are rolled out as the candidates are; both bots hold a cast or activation that sets up a copy of the next instant or sorcery with none to copy (`holdsForNextSpell`: Adaptive Training Post), skip an `{X}` ability affordable only at X = 0 whose effect is all X (`inertAtZeroX`: Necropolis Fiend), and cast a "dies"/"leaves" payoff whose filter takes what a chosen wipe kills before the wipe, when the wipe is still on offer after it (`payoffBeforeWipe`: Colfenor's Urn, then Citywide Bust); v2, while a play is held for after combat (`heldPlay`: a held wipe, or a planned line's move after combat), drops a combat-step candidate that leaves the held play off offer in main 2 unless it kills the attacked player (`spendsHeldMana`, asked of the engine on that board read as our second main phase). **Untap plans** (`bot/untap-plans.ts`, scored by `simulate.ts`'s `simulatePlan`): when an ability we could activate untaps a permanent (Kiora, Behemoth Beckoner's −1), the search also tries tapping one of our untapped lands for mana, untapping it, letting that resolve with the mana still in the pool (rule 106.4) and casting a spell the extra mana pays for — a three-step line the one-ply search never saw (a live capture: Temur Ascendancy cast over Ganax). A winning plan is played out step by step by `continuePlan`, dropped the moment anything else happens. A land that taps for mana and also fetches (Bountiful Landscape, `isManaLandFetch`) is held until that same end step and cracked there, both bots: cracked in its own turn, it cost that turn's mana and its fetched land sat tapped (a live capture). `engine/src/test/heuristic-bot.test.ts` runs bot-vs-bot games to completion as a second fuzz target. `ControllerView.targetFacts` (built by `Game.controllerView`) is what the choosers narrow relations among targets by; `RandomController.pickTargets` keeps a group within its `min` (topped up in order, no extra draws) and its `maxTotalPower`. A spree offer's modes are one of its `castModal.modeSets` (`fitModeSet` over the aimable modes, then over every mode with a target, for the heuristic bot; a random set for the random one). v1's `chooseFromZone` gives up the least useful for a Champion's behold-and-exile, and from its own search takes the cards it would most want in hand (`mostUsefulTo`: the dear spells the board can nearly cast), lands alone by the colour they add (`newColorsFirst`); it took library order, so Demonic Tutor found the next card, and v2 kept that pick, its rollouts unable to tell one card in hand from another (2026-11-09, `test/bot-tutor.test.ts`). `commanderReplacement` leaves an airbended commander in exile, where it costs {2} to cast. A sacrifice *cost* is paid through `cheapestFodder`: the cheapest choice that isn't the ability's own source or a permanent with a trigger on something else dying, being sacrificed or leaving (`watchesSacrifices`), while another choice exists (the bot audit's Q3, 2026-11-10: Blood Artist fed to Viscera Seer beside two Grizzly Bears); an edict still gives up the cheapest by stats. `payoffFirst`'s cast branch asks that the spell castable after the payoff match its trigger's filter (Opt doesn't trigger Beast Whisperer).
- **`bot/`** — the searching bot, **v2** (`EvalBotController`), which `Room.addBot` seats; design records `docs/plans/smarter-bots.md` (the search) and `docs/plans/bot-effect-knowledge.md` (the plan of record — read it before changing how a bot picks actions). A v3 that planned whole turns over sampled worlds was built, benched and retired (`docs/plans/bot-v3-search.md` records why); what survives of it is `determinize.ts` (sampling plausible completions of the hidden information — v2 still reads the real hands) and `simulate.ts`'s `"playing"` policy (`simulateTurns`), which `bot:audit --rollout` prices a card with. One-ply search: enumerate the concrete actions available now (`candidates.ts`), play each against a throwaway `Game.fromSnapshot` copy (`simulate.ts`), score the result and take the best. Candidates are capped (8 target combinations an action, a dozen rollouts a decision; X at its maximum, except a "−X" loyalty ability's, offered at each X from 1 up so the search weighs what it leaves the planeswalker, and an X paid in life alone, offered on a ladder from 1 to 20 (`lifeXLadder`, the offer's `xCost.paidInLife`: at its maximum, the life total, v2 never cast Toxic Deluge; now it pays the X that sweeps the board, 2026-11-09)), so each slot's options are first ranked by `target-polarity.ts` (`aimOffer` in `eval-bot.ts` — the right side first, most valuable first, the wrong side last rather than dropped): unranked, the cap cut to the battlefield's oldest permanents, and on a four-player board v2 never saw a threat newer than the turn-one lands. A targeted modal spell is tried in each choice of modes (as many as allowed, each mode alone, then the other combinations, capped) with its X at the maximum; it used to be tried only as every mode at once, and without an X — which the engine reads as 0. `features.ts` computes one player's raw unweighted feature vector (for the bot's own seat, `untappedCreatures` counts only creatures that would hold an attack off — able to block one an opponent could attack it with and kill it or survive it (`deterringBlockers`), so a lone 2/2 isn't kept home against nothing or against 3/3s it could only chump — and blockers too small to hold one off alone count in gangs that kill an attacker together (two 1/1s against a 2/2), as many as the one opponent with the most attackers would take, since opponents attack on separate turns and blocking taps nothing; and a subtracted `threat`: opponents' creatures by the combat damage they could turn on it — only those the attack rules would let attack it at their controller's next combat (`combat/eligibility.ts`'s `playersAttackableNextTurn`: tapped and summoning sickness ignored, a Vow of Duty, a goad by us or Pacifism honoured) — in full from whoever attacked it within the last round — `PlayerState.lastAttackedBy`, recorded as attacks are declared — and as a share of the life, or commander damage, it has left, with this turn's damage credited back as far as the life above 20; the worst commander damage taken itself counts squared over 21, so 21 is still 21 and a first hit is cheap), `drawEngines`, priced by the cards each engine draws a round of the table (`drawRate`: Phyrexian Arena 1, Rhystic Study half a card per opponent, capped at 3), `tokenEngines`, the creature tokens a round its permanents keep making, rated the same way (`tokenRate`: Hero of Bladehold 2, Elspeth, Sun's Champion's +1 3; not a one-shot like Beetleback Chief, nor an ability that costs loyalty), `valueEngines`, what an engine that makes neither keeps paying, in cards a round (`valueRate`, since 2026-11-10, the audit's Q4: resource tokens at four a card, Smothering Tithe 0.375 at four players, and life taken from opponents at four a card, Zulaport Cutthroat 0.75, capped at 1.5; a static cost reduction is mana in `nonlandMana`, `reductionPerTurn`; a printed drain comes off `trackRecord`), `standingEffects`, the floor under those rates, in points (`standingWorth`, since 2026-11-11: a nontoken noncreature permanent with a static ability, a trigger that can fire again or a Saga's chapters is worth its mana value plus 1.5 less what the rates already score it, so a four-mana enchantment no rate prices scores just over a vanilla 4/4 where it used to score under half of one and sat in hand behind every creature; not an Aura, an Equipment, a planeswalker, a rock or a permanent whose only trigger is its own entering; for the bot's own permanents it shrinks from whole, while what could attack it is under a third of its life, to nothing when that would be lethal, so a blocker still comes first under pressure), `earlyMana`, acceleration in the opening rounds — mana a turn from rocks and creatures plus each land past one per land drop the player has had (`landDropsHad`: Rampant Growth's land counts, a natural drop never), in full through round 2 and fading by round 6 — and `answers`, the counterspells in its own hand that its untapped mana could cast: a reserve casting one (or tapping out) gives up, so it counters a Craw Wurm or a Rhystic Study and lets a Signet through and `evaluate.ts` is `EvalWeights` plus a dot product over it (the bot's own features scored with its deck's `ownWeights` laid over them — `outcomeOf` reads them from `deck-bias.ts`; one-shot tokens that sacrifice themselves, Treasure, Clue, Food, are `resourceTokens` at 0.5 rather than full `otherPermanents`, and a mana rock's ongoing mana counts at `nonlandMana` 0.5, since 2026-10-02's behaviour sweep found Sol Ring held rather than paid for with a Treasure; each opponent eliminated adds `eliminations` (20) scaled by the round, a quarter of it in round 1 rising to all of it at round 10 — before it a kill scored nothing, or less when the weakest player went, since the rest are averaged over those still in) — split apart so the weights can be **fitted** by logistic regression over harvested self-play positions rather than hand-picked. Combat is built one attacker/defender pair at a time — a token stack in parts: one more token, the fewest that finish a planeswalker, or all it has left, smallest first — or re-aimed (an attacker already sent, sent at another opponent), never enumerated; each opponent past the first an attack reaches adds `SPREAD_BONUS` (0.5) to its score, enough to split a near tie between open opponents (the user's preference, 2026-10-07) and not a real difference (`combat-math.ts`, `eval-bot.ts`), its moves ranked before simulation by arithmetic that prices life with the evaluation's bend (`features.ts`'s `lifeCost`); before the climb, `alphaStrike` looks for a kill — as many opponents as the attackers can kill at once, trying each order of the killable ones and giving each the fewest attackers its kill needs, a token stack counted token by token and split between defenders where that kills more — when the attackers' total damage covers every killable opponent's life, the subset of up to 10 attackers that kills spending the least damage, so 3+2 and 3+2 kill two opponents at 5 where biggest-first 3+3 left 2+2 short — then the most kills that survives the crackback; blocks are climbed one blocker at a time from v1's, plus two kinds of paired move — two blockers at once against menace, and a double block where neither blocker kills the attacker alone but the two together do (the estimate letting the attacker kill only what its damage reaches, best first); `decisions.ts` searches the decisions the engine raises mid-resolution. `scenarios.ts` is the opponent-independent gate (hand-built positions with a known right answer, parameterised by a weight vector) — `gate` scenarios every shipped vector passes, and `training` ones it may still get wrong, kept to tune against; most are written as a `position` (the board and a judge of *any* answer) rather than a script. `benchmarks.ts` is the third kind, neither gate nor training: hard positions whose winning line takes more lookahead than v2's search has (several moves that only pay off together, a combat planned around, the opponents' next turn, our own after it), each played out over `turns` whole turns with the bot under test as Alice and v2 in the other seats, and judged on the board it ends on rather than on the first action; each carries its winning line and its natural mistake as a scripted `BenchmarkLine` (`LineController`), and `test/bot-benchmarks.test.ts` checks the first wins and the second loses, never that a bot solves one. **Deep horizons** (an experiment, 2026-10-18, never seated): `EvalBotOptions.horizon` `"round"` rolls each candidate out through every opponent's turn until ours begins again, and `"next-turn"` through our next turn too (`simulate.ts`'s `horizonTest`, one end-of-rollout test for every priority rollout); the policy defaults to `"playing"` (opponents cast and attack as v1), and our own seat is a `DeepRolloutController` — passing for the rest of the candidate's turn, so a candidate means "this, and nothing more this turn", and a small v2 search (8 simulations, the turn as its horizon) on every later turn, so the Fog or Counterspell we passed to keep is cast where it matters. Under one, blocks are scored to the horizon rather than to the end of combat, ties with passing break toward acting (an instant passed on is otherwise cast in the opponent's turn and ties), and `"next-turn"` searches the 903.9a choice (`commanderToGraveyard`, which needs the graveyard to win by `COMMANDER_STAYS_MARGIN`). A finished game scores `WIN` less its turn number, so a sooner win wins. **Turn planning** (`turn-plan.ts`, `EvalBotOptions.plan`, `"turn"` by default since 2026-10-18; `"off"`, or `followUps: false`, searches as before): in place of `followUps`, a beam search (`PLAN_DEPTH` 5, `PLAN_BEAM` 3, `planSimulations` 48) over our own moves, each settled (`simulateSettled`), plus a step through combat to our second main phase (our attack as v1's) when a creature of ours could attack (`TurnPlanHooks.mayAttack`), every partial line scored by passing the turn out with the opponents only blocking; a move that ends the game is a finished line. Past the first moves each move is passed out as soon as it has resolved, the best line's moves first, so a plan the simulations or the clock cut keeps every line it resolved, and only a line with a step left takes a place in the beam (the bot audit's S4, 2026-11-10). A first move the one-move search rolled out the same way (`plansAsScored`: the `"turn"` horizon, the `"combat"` rollout, no `rolloutDecisions`; `lastEnds`, `planTurn`'s `scored`, a `ScoredFirst`) is a line at no cost, and when all are, they're resolved best first only until `PLAN_BEAM` can go on; under `"acting"` the plan scores its own, keeps the one-move answer's line in the beam (`lead`), and a line must beat the plan's own line for that answer rather than its `"acting"` score. It scores nothing unless some first move, once resolved, leaves another (`onlyIfChained`), so a window with nothing to sequence costs what the follow-ups' settling did; it runs on the decision's clock (`TurnPlanLimits.clock`, a `PlanClock`: the search's own predictive stop, and its simulations charged to the decision), and traces each line for `scenario-fit.ts`. The chosen line is replayed window by window (`turnPlan`, `continueTurnPlan`) while each step's board matches (`boardKey`), a move it has for later passed on in our combat steps (`deferToPlan`) and its mana held through combat (`heldPlay`). `"next-turn"` also plays our next turn on (`nextTurnValue`: opponents pass their turns without attacking, then `planTurn` again) from the best line of each of the best `PLAN_REFINE_LINES` first moves, the one-move answer first and keeping ties, when a line leaves a card in hand. Neither plans with a card that was in our library. `scenario-fit.ts` records one: every answer v2 simulated there (`EvalBotOptions.trace`) and the state its rollout reached, read into features by `evaluate.ts`'s `outcomeOf`. A priority window's rollouts never read the weights (v1 plays them out), so its choice under any vector is replayed exactly by `scoreOutcome` — which is all `evaluateState` is — and `leversFor`/`handTune` ask what single weight would fix a wrong scenario and what that breaks. `champions/` holds the frozen gauntlet opponents, each spelling out its whole `EvalWeights` literally so it can't drift when the defaults change. `bot/mulligan.ts` is the opening-hand policy both share (hooked onto v1, since a mulligan has no board to roll forward): a hand is scored on mana sources plus its cheapest few castable spells, ramp counting for both, and the bar it has to clear *falls* as the hand it would keep gets smaller — with a free Commander mulligan raising it instead, and a floor at four cards. `EvalBotOptions.timeBudgetMs` is a wall-clock ceiling on one decision, **off by default** because a clock breaks the engine's same-seed-replays-identically guarantee that tests, the fuzzer and the tuner all rely on; only `Room.addBot` turns it on. When it expires the search plays the best candidate found so far, `pass` and v1's own pick having been scored first. Since 2026-11-10 (the bot audit's step 2) the deadline also reaches inside a rollout: `onClock` sets it for the decision (`withRolloutDeadline` in `simulate.ts`, module state, so a stand-in's nested rollouts inherit it), every rollout's stop test reads it between ticks (`untilDeadline`) and a stopped rollout returns `null` with `rolloutAborted()` true (`rollout`, the one wrapper every `simulate*` and `turn-plan.ts`'s three run in); a priority search stopped with no candidate scored plays v1's cast unscored, matched by card, `via` and face, or passes (`unscoredCast`; `lastDecision.via` `"v1's cast"` / `"no time"`); and a search whose first simulation was stopped marks the turn (`tooSlow`, `noteCost`), after which that turn's priority windows and awaited decisions are answered without a simulation. An aborted crackback check reads as unsafe, an aborted `nextTurnValue` keeps the one-move answer, and an attack declaration whose first simulation was stopped is v1's swing. With no clock none of it is read. The clock starts when the decision is asked (`onClock`), so v1's own pick counts against it, and stops a search *predictively* — once the longest simulation so far no longer fits (`outOfTime`), but never before its baselines (two for a priority search or an attack, one — v1's — for a land search, blocks or a decision) — and one deadline covers a whole decision, an attack declaration reached through `act` included. Twin targets — tokens nothing tells apart (`state.ts`'s `tokenFoldKey`, shared with the token fold, plus combat, damage and anything pending that names one) — are cut to as many as the offer has slots before the cap (`bot/twins.ts`), so seven identical Treasures are one simulation and the cap goes to real alternatives. An until-end-of-turn pump, keyword grant or animation is not a candidate where it can't matter — outside combat, outside its own first main phase, with nothing on the stack (`HeuristicBotController.wastedNow`, over `effect-worth.ts`'s `onlyUntilEndOfTurn`/`temporaryEffectCanMatter`; v1 obeys it too): the live bot was casting Unleash Fury on an opponent's creature in its own upkeep. Nor is anything that costs mana (cycling included) in its own upkeep or draw step with nothing on the stack, or in its own main phase with only its own abilities on the stack (`holdsManaForMain`): the rollouts pass at every window, so the main phase's spells never appear in them and upkeep mana looked free.
