Nor is a creature that counters a spell as it enters (`effect-worth.ts`' `entersToCounter` — Transcendent Dragon, Mystic Snake) with no opponent's spell on the stack (`holdsForASpell`; such a card also counts toward `answers`), nor, in our own first main phase, a board wipe that would take a creature of ours able to attack this turn (`holdsWipeForCombat`: it waits for the second main phase; a one-sided wipe, or one our attackers survive, is left alone, and "each *other* creature" dealt by a creature we target (Chandra's Ignition) spares our most powerful one and deals its power) — though v2 still scores it, and if it scores best passes to combat with its mana intact (`lastHeldForCombat`) rather than letting a cheaper play spend it. A counter-creature is held only until the end of the turn before ours, then cast for its body. v1's blocks, which v2's attack planner predicts every defender with, take worthwhile trades and gang blocks (`blockWorth`, `gangLoss`), skip deathtouch attackers and block with one token of a stack — in the planner's simulation any trade at all (`simulate.ts`'s `CombatRolloutController` sets v1's `tradeMargin` from 2 to −∞), since the v2 defenders in live rooms take them; and at three or more players an attack also pays `bystanderShare` (half at three, two-thirds at four) of the creature value it costs us, since a trade sets both its players back against the rest (the user, 2026-10-10) — kept out of `scoreOutcome`, where removal needs the leader counted in full; v1's attack check sees evasion and gang blocks. v1 obeys both. Help aimed at an **opponent's** creature is a candidate only while that creature attacks someone else, and then only if it ends at end of turn, the creature is goaded by us, or — for lasting help, a counter or an Aura — the player attacked dies in the rollout (`opponentPump`, the user's rule). In its own main phase with nothing on the stack, a move is also worth what it lets it do next this turn (`followUps`, on unless `EvalBotOptions.followUps` is false): every move is played until it has resolved and we have priority again (`simulate.ts`'s `simulateSettled`), the moves `listCandidates` finds on that board are each rolled out from there (`scoreFrom`), and the first move wins if one of those beats every move scored alone — the reducer before what it reduces, the rock before what it pays for, which a rollout that passes our seat never sees. Moves are expanded by what they open up (offers that weren't castable before first), never shortlisted by their own score, which is what undersells a setup move; only the first move is played, the next window searching again; `lastFollowUp` records a win. They're weighed last, after every single move, batch and untap plan, so under a clock they spend only what's left, and out of their own ceiling (`FOLLOW_UP_SIMULATIONS`, 24, half of it at most on settling, the cheapest first moves first): uncapped, every move times every move after it ran one 79-permanent search to 381 simulations and 126 s. A follow-up never uses a card that was in our library (the rollout knows what a draw finds). Each pair is traced as an answer for its first move, so `scenario-fit.ts`'s replay weighs it as the search did. An ability it could activate again at once is also tried as a **batch** — as many times as the mana allows, one candidate (`simulateRepeated`; a targeted one twice, every activation at the same target and each at a new one, `nextInBatch`) — and once chosen, the rest go on the stack without searching again (`continueBatch`). Nor an empty-stack window, outside its own main phases, like one whose whole search passed earlier in the turn (`passMemo`, keyed by `passMemoKey` on the candidates and the whole state but the step, the priority holder and the event counter; `EvalBotOptions.passMemo` `"off"` or `"check"`; the bot audit's S3, 2026-11-10). It doesn't search a window it has already decided either (`holdPass`): after a batch, after its own spell or ability, or after it passed, while the stack only resolves — the candidate it chose was scored as exactly that, passed through to resolution; anything new on the stack is searched — except our own board wipe about to take a creature of ours (`ownWipeOnStack`), searched again since the cast was scored with no answer of ours to it (Yahenni sacrificing into its own Blasphemous Act). Seed 50's bench timeout (Lathliss pumps and Scavenging Ooze exiles, a full search each, on a seventy-permanent board) is what found both. Between a batch's activations it asks only that ability's offers (`Game.legalActivationsOf`, `legalActions` narrowed to one source — the whole list plans a payment for every ability on the board, and asking it was a quarter of seed 50's turn 40). A cantrip or a tutor used to score a wash against passing and be cast by a rule (`isCantripDue`); since 2026-11-10 `handQuality` (`bot/hand-worth.ts`) prices the card and the search casts it. Two plays still score a wash and are cast when the search would pass: a **sacrifice for cards** — Deadly Dispute, Village Rites, a Treasure beside the cards counting as nothing given up — when the cheapest thing it would pay with is spare (`isSacrificeForCardsDue`; `isSpareFodder`: a token, or a nontoken creature, not a commander, of 4 power and toughness together or less whose only abilities are keywords and a trigger on its own entering), since the evaluation prices a Grizzly Bears at two and a half cards, an instant at the end of the turn before ours (`isEndOfTurnBeforeOurs`, shared with `isCreatureFetchDue`), a sorcery in our own main phase; and a suspend (`isSuspendDue`). A window where passing is the only move it would consider costs no simulation at all (most windows in a game; it was a third of the bot's CPU). `EvalBotController.lastDecision` records what the last decision cost, for tests and measurement, and whether the search stopped with something unscored (`cut`: out of simulations, past the clock, or predicted not to fit another simulation before it). A decision's rollouts search a further decision of our own one level deep only when it's a "you may" (`NESTED_DECISION_KINDS`: `choose-modes`, `cast-now`); every other decision inside a rollout takes v1's answer, as priority rollouts' do — searched, nested targets and choices cost more than the top-level decisions did (2026-10-14). `bot/capture.ts` turns a position saved from a live game (see the server's `capture.ts`) into a training scenario: `captureOptions` (everything the player could do there, ranked as the search ranks it, described by `describeMove`), `sameMove` (action equality, whatever order the fields were built in) and `scenarioFromCapture`; `bot:scenarios` and `bot:fit-scenarios` add every open capture (`captures/*.json`) to the training scenarios and every resolved one (`captures/resolved/`, each with a `resolved` record: when, at which commit, what fixed it) to the gate, so a fixed blunder that comes back fails it (`engine/scripts/captures.mjs`). `npm run bot:captures -w engine` lists them all with v2's answer today; `-- resolve <file or name part> --note "…"` stamps and moves one once v2 gets it right (refused while wrong, unless `--force`), `-- reopen` moves one back. Captures are git-ignored, so a fix also gets a committed gate scenario. `castCandidates` tries only a spree offer's `modeSets`, the largest set of fillable modes first. `combat-math.ts`'s `canBlock` reads shadow the same way. `trackRecord` (`features.ts`'s `trackRecordOf`, weight 3) prices that settled tally as cards a round past the printed draw rate (draws, and life taken at a card per 4), from one card's worth of evidence up, capped at 3; `engineScore` (printed draw rate, half the token rate, and the track record) is what `eval-bot.ts`'s `rankValue` adds to `targetValue` (6 a card a round) when `aimOffer` ranks an offer, through `rankTargets`' optional `value`, so an engine is among the target combinations the search plays out. **`activation-cap.ts`** is a backstop on how often v2 activates one ability of one permanent in a turn (`ActivationCap`, `EvalBotController.activationCap`): 4 times for one with no cost at all (`isFreeCost`), 40 for a paid one, read where candidates are listed and again in `act`, since v1's pick and a turn plan's step reach `act` by other roads. A free activation can measure better than passing every time, and a bot that never passes hangs the game: real decks had it move Lightning Greaves' equip {0} between two creatures for as long as the game ran (2026-11-09, `test/bot-equip.test.ts`). v1 has its own (`MAX_ACTIVATIONS_PER_TURN`), counted where an activation is handed to the engine (`noteActivation`): v1's `act` is `propose` plus that count, and v2, which asks `propose` at every window, counts what it plays (2026-11-10; a proposal v2 turned down used to spend one of the four). A turn plan's step is replayed only while its targets are still among the offer's options (`targetsStillOffered`, besides `boardKey`, which leaves out the graveyard): the plan is made in rollouts, and a step aimed at the card Goblin Engineer's search put in the graveyard there was replayed at that card still in the library here, and refused (2026-11-09, `test/turn-plan.test.ts`). **Since the bot audit's step 1 (2026-11-10):** v2's payoff and landfall picks are no longer returned before the search but scored first in it, and with a landfall permanent castable the land rules (`landRule`: fetch, only land, land search) wait for that search and answer after it if it passed; `withSacrificeChoices` offers `cheapestFodder`'s pick and, where it differs, the plain cheapest; the planner's first moves leave out `lastUnsafe`, a line is taken only if each step passes the crackback check on the board it is played on (`lineSafe`, `PlannedLine.before`, up to `PLAN_SAFE_LINES` lines), and `continueTurnPlan` checks a replayed step again on the real board; `UNSCORED_COUNTERS` (`features.ts`) takes `target-polarity.ts`'s `HARMFUL_COUNTERS` and `age`. **The audit's step 5, sub-step 5 (2026-11-10), which overrides what's said above:** the sequencing rules `reducerFirst`, `hideawayFirst`, `payoffBeforeWipe` and `holdsForNextSpell` are **v1's alone** now (`controller.ts`; v2 no longer asks them, its turn planner and search finding those plays), `holdsTapForCombat` and `simulate.ts`'s `simulateAfterCombat` are deleted (the planner's lines pass to combat and play after), and so are `followUps`, `scoreFrom`, `FOLLOW_UP_SIMULATIONS` and `EvalBotOptions.followUps`: `plan: "off"` is the one-move search alone, and `lastFollowUp` is written by `planLines` only. The "only land" rule stays, load-bearing (off, a land went down after the turn's spells or not at all).
- **Dredge and kept mana** (`game.ts`, 2026-10-07): before each draw, a player with a dredge card in their graveyard and enough library (`CardDefinition.dredge`, rule 702.52) is asked a `choose-modes` with `dredgeFor` — draw, or mill N and return that card — that the engine answers itself; draws still to come in the same instruction wait in `GameState.pendingDraws` and are made one at a time by `prepareForPriority`, each offered dredge again (the ruling). Mana a static's `keepsUnspentMana` names (Leyline Tyrant) isn't emptied as steps end, cleanup included (`keptManaTypes`); with `unspentManaBecomesColorless` (Kruphix, God of Horizons) or `unspentManaBecomes` (Omnath, Locus of All's black) what would empty stays as that type, restrictions and all, the newest of several (`unspentManaBecomesFor`); `keepsUnspentManaEachPlayer` (Upwelling) has `keptManaTypes` read another player's static, and a `PlayerEffect`'s `keepsUnspentMana` (a `player-effect`'s, The Last Agni Kai's "until end of turn") is its owner's for as long as it lasts, and `manaLossCausesLifeLoss` (Yurlok) costs each player 1 life per mana actually emptied (`manaLossLifeLossCount`, once per such permanent). A `revealsFirstDrawEachTurn` static (Keranos) has `drawCardRaw` reveal its controller's first card of each of their turns (`revealCards`), the `revealsDraws: "chosen-player"` static (Booby Trap — `revealsEachDraw`) every card its chosen player draws, and a `draws` trigger's `filter` narrows on the drawn card (read with the permanent as its source, so `chosenName` is the trap's). The state-based sweep's `removeNoncreaturesFromCombat` takes an attacker or blocker that stopped being a creature out of combat (rule 506.4 — a God whose devotion fell). A resolution's `powerOf` / `toughnessOf` read 0 for a noncreature permanent, live or as it left (rule 208.3 — Phenax). A `may`'s `xColor` pays its `{X}` in one colour (`concreteX`). A `may`'s `costEnergyX` (`"one-or-more"` / `"any"`, rule 107.14) pays an X of energy chosen on `choose-modes`, its offer's `xCost` marked `energy` with `minX`, capped at the energy the chooser has (`beginModesChoice`); `applyModesChoice` removes it and runs the mode at that X alone — never the spell's own — and an "any amount" declined runs at X 0. A `cast-now`'s `byPayingEnergy` (Amped Raptor) is a free cast with the spell's mana value in energy paid (`castNowEnergyOf`). An announced modal's `optional` adds `orNone` to its `choose-modes`, and `eachTargetsDifferentPlayer` builds `other` relations into its slots (`triggerTargetSpecs`).
- **`mana-payment.ts`** — `planMatchingPips` is `planManaPayment`'s last try, where every pass found nothing: `matchPips` matches the coloured pips to sources (augmenting paths), so a Fellwar Stone isn't spent on the {W} a Silent Clearing could have paid. `ManaOption.first` (and the plan step's) carries `add-mana`'s `first` through the plan beside `rider`, for `useManaSource` to apply before the mana. `withLifeForPips` (K'rrik's static read by `planPayment`, `ManaPlanningView.lifeForPips`) makes each pip of a colour its colour or 2 life, `rather` so `ManaPayment.lifeRather` tells compleated it paid no Phyrexian symbol; `resolveHybridCost` puts life by choice first on pips mana couldn't pay; `planManaPayment` tries again, when a plan fails, with only the richer of a source’s options where one makes all another does and more (a Swamp’s {B} and an emblem’s {B}{B}{B}{B}): the first try takes the narrowest that covers a pip and doesn’t come back. `optionsFor`: of a source's options making several units that make the colour wanted, the one covering the most of what's still needed first, so {U} for {U}{B} doesn't commit Firemind Vessel to {W}{U}. `standaloneManaChoices`: an offer per pairing of `different`. `optionKeepingExtras`: extra units a payment can't spend (`spendOnlyManaFrom`, an extra's own restriction — `ManaExtraUnit.tag`, stamped from the step's `extraTagOf`) leave the option's mana for its `floating`, made and left in the pool as the source is tapped, unless they were the player's to decline (`ManaExtraUnit.may`). `ManaPurpose` also has `kicked`, and has `{ kind: "special" }` (turning face up, unlocking, another cost with `{X}`) and a cast's `via`/`xCost`; `pipsAsSpendable` widens a pip a `SpendUnitAs` covers to a hybrid one before `resolveHybridCost`. The mana solver, lifted out of `game.ts`: `planPayment` (the entry point `Game.payMana` wraps) = `resolveHybridCost` (hybrid/twobrid/Phyrexian pips → a concrete cost + the life they owe) → `planManaPayment` (a greedy solver: floating mana first, then coloured pips, then `{C}`, then generic, tapping a fresh source only when the already-tapped ones can't cover it; a converter — a Signet, whose own `{1}` another source pays, or a Shadowmoor filter land, whose two-colour hybrid `{G/U}` one does (`ManaOption.hybridCost`) — is reached for after the plain sources and before a last resort (a Treasure, a counter cost), and when that plan fails a second pass funds the converters first, so two lands and a Signet pay a `{3}`, and a third keeps the Treasure among the plain sources, as before 2026-10-10; a converter's cost is funded from floating mana the payment doesn't need before any source, handing back to the sources a unit of generic it was counting on (a bug report, 2026-10-10: {R} floating and Mossfire Valley weren't seen to pay Rampant Growth, so the spell wasn't offered and the server passed the window as mana-only). A source is opened with the first of its options (`optionsFor`) whose cost can be funded, each failed one rolled back — the tapped sources' units it took included — before the next, so a filter land falls back on its free `{T}: Add {C}`; a source with any paid option (`hasCostedOption`, a filter land beside its `{C}`) also triggers and leads the converters-first pass; a source opened *to fund* one uses only its free options; and a converter may be funded by another's leftover, or by borrowing a unit already paying the cost (`Tapped.lendable`) that it pays back out of its own output, the converters activating in the order they were opened; a colour need tries the sources making it for free before one that must be funded, within its group, and only a converter making more than it costs (`gainful`) leads the converters-first pass, so a colour fixer (Prismatic Lens, Celestial Prism) is reached for only when nothing makes the colour freely (a bug report, 2026-10-13: Mystic Gate didn't pay Think Twice beside an Island, nor Glorious Anthem beside a Plains and a Swamp, and two gates alone cast Opt — one funding the other through a paid option nobody paid). A `reserved` source — a hideaway land with its card waiting (`Game.holdsFreeSpell`: a {T} ability that plays a card exiled with it) — comes after every other group, the last resorts included, and is still taken if nothing else pays: Utvara Hellkite's auto-pay took Mosswort Bridge ahead of Birds of Paradise and two Treasures (capture AN5SU t22). Generic mana goes to the source the rest of the hand needs least — each colour weighed against the sources this payment still leaves making it, read afresh unit by unit, so a `{3}` out of three Forests, two Islands and two Mountains leaves a source of each colour a `{1}{G}{U}{R}` in hand needs rather than every Forest gone, and a cost reducer being paid for (Urza's Incubator) weighs the cards its filter takes at their reduced cost (`Game.otherCardCosts`) — then the one making fewest colours (Sol Ring's colourless before two coloured lands, even with nothing else in hand to weigh), ranked behind two harder rules: a source that costs life whatever it makes (Mana Confluence — a painland's free {C} costs none) after every one that doesn't, creatures among them, and creatures (`ManaSource.isCreature`, whose tap costs an attack) after the rest within each; the `avoid` source (a man-land paying its own animation) last of all; a coloured pip, too, is paid by a source making it without life before one making it with life (Llanowar Elves before Confluence, a bug report, 2026-10-15). A board with a source costing life also has a `lifeLast` plan: whatever would cost life for the mana wanted comes behind every free source, a Treasure included (`spentBeforeLife`: a non-land, non-creature source sacrificed for one mana; Black Lotus and the other last resorts stay behind life). At low life (`ManaPlanningView.lowLife`: a quarter of the game's starting life or less, 10 in Commander, the user's call on 2026-11-03) that plan comes first, and the orders above run as they were only where it finds nothing, so a Treasure that must make the {R} leaves the {W} to the painland. Above that, the point of life goes before a Treasure: the `lifeLast` plan replaces the ordinary one only if it pays less life and spends no more (`planToll`, `planSpends`), which still fixes a bug report of 2026-11-03 at any life total, Boromir's {W} taking a life from Talisman of Hierarchy while the same Treasure paid its {2}. A sacrificed source's step comes after the plain ones' (`stepRank`), so Spire of Industry's "only if you control an artifact" holds beside the Treasure that is its only artifact. Where a cost leaves the colour open (a generic pip off a dual land, an "any colour" unit), it makes the first of `ManaPlanningView.preferred` — `Game.deckColors`, the payer's colours most-played first, held to their commander's identity — and an "any colour" unit is always a colour, never `{C}`. A generic cost reduction that outran the generic part rides on `ManaCost.twobridReduction` (`mana.ts`'s `reduceManaCost`, which `castingCostOf` applies) and comes off only the twobrid pips `resolveHybridCost` ends up paying generically — the Spectral Procession ruling. Also `ManaOption`/`ManaSource`/`ManaPlanStep`/`ManaPayment`/`ManaPurpose` (`ManaOption.extras`: the units triggered mana abilities add, the planner tracking each through `Tapped` so a step lists its own units first and the extras — `extraFrom` — last), `multipliedManaOption` (a `manaProduction` multiplier on one option) and the `standaloneManaChoices`/`manaCombinations` enumerators `Game.manaSources` builds options with (and `standaloneManaSplit`: a live X or a printed number too large to list every split of, offered by hand as one more variant carrying `manaSplit`, whose split the player picks). **Everything here is pure** — it returns a *plan* and never applies one, so `Game` stays the only writer of `GameState` (`useManaSource`/`executePayment` are still methods). What the planner may read is declared by `ManaPlanningView` (pool, life, sources, a `canPay` closure for rule 106.6b, and `spendAs` — `SpendAs`, "spend mana as though it were mana of any color" / "mana of any type can be spent", rules 118.14 and 609.4b: `costAsSpendable` folds the pips any unit may pay into generic, every coloured pip and with `"any-type"` every `{C}` too, as `planManaPayment` starts and on the `resolved` cost `spendFromPool` deducts, so the planner and the spend agree and the mana actually spent is still recorded as itself; `Game.spendAsFor` works it out per payment, from the payer's `spendManaAs` statics and, for a cast, the permission it's cast under — read while the card is still where it's cast from, and as the face being cast, `ManaPurpose.face`) rather than being whatever `this` exposed; `Game.manaPlanningView` builds it, which also resolves `manaSources` once per payment instead of once per hybrid-pip trial. `cantPayLife` (rule 119.8 — "can't lose life") stops the planner paying a Phyrexian pip with life or taking a `Pay N life` mana option; a painland's damage is still allowed. An option's (and a step's) `counterCost` is an `AbilityCost.addCounter` the payment pays (Wall of Roots).
- **`decisions/`** — one module per `AwaitingDecision` kind (32; the newest, `replacement-order`, is rule 616.1's "which of these applies first": asked only when the order can change what happens, raised by `Game` where the event can still wait — `damageOrdersAsked` goes through a damage effect once without dealing anything, `combatDamageOrderAsked` before a combat-damage pass, and likewise an effect's counters (`counterOrderAsked`) and life gain (`lifeOrderAsked`) — the answers waiting in `GameState.replacementAnswers` until nothing is waiting on anyone), each owning that kind's *answer* half: its `LegalAction` projection, its `whyCannot` validator, its `ask` (one arm of `controller.ts`'s `answerAwaited`), its bot `candidates`, its `randomAnswer` (the fuzzer's arm, reached via `randomAnswerFor` — `RandomController` keeps only the six priority arms), and the per-kind predicates `hasSource`/`mayAct`/`autoAnswer`. `contract.ts` holds `DecisionModule`, `DecisionReadCtx` (what a module may read), `DecisionHost` (`Game.dispatch`'s decision arms transposed — every one was already a one-line delegate, so **no `apply*` body moved**) and the two total `as const satisfies` tables `DECISION_ACTIONS`/`DECISION_OFFERS`. `registry.ts` is the table plus `decisionFor`/`decisionForAction`/`decisionForOffer`/`mayActOn`/`decisionHasSource`/`autoAnswerFor`/`randomAnswerFor`; `define.ts` holds `defineDecision` **in its own file on purpose** — the registry imports every module, so a module importing the registry back is a value cycle whose symptom is `TypeError` at module init, not a build failure. `shared/` holds `picks.ts` (the four checks a validator keeps rewriting), `subsets.ts` (`combinations`, `subsetsBetween`, and `multisets`/`modeChoicesBetween` for modes that may repeat, `budgetedModeSets` for pawprints, rule 700.2i), `target-combos.ts`, `mulligan-math.ts`. `choose-from-zone` also checks an offer's `together` — a rule over the picks as a set, from `../zone-choice-together.ts` — in `whyCannot`, keeps only legal sets in `candidates`, and turns a controller's or a random pick into the nearest legal one (`completeTogether`) in `ask` and `randomAnswer`. **Nothing under `decisions/` may import `game.js`, and `state.ts` may not import from `decisions/`** (`choose-creature-type.ts` imports `printedCardName` as a value, so the reverse closes a real ESM cycle). To add a kind: write the module (including a `randomAnswer` — optional on the contract, but the fuzzer stalls without one, so `registry.test.ts` requires it), register it, add it to both tables, add a fixture to `test/decisions/registry.test.ts` — the compiler names every step you skip, but not the raise, which is still hand-placed (`pay-life-for-untapped` has no `begin*` at all; its raise is inlined in `moveObject`). `choose-targets` validates and applies a triggered ability's `division` (`divisionOf`) when its offer has a `divide`; `shared/target-combos.ts` leaves out an empty tail for a group with a `min`. A `choose-from-zone` offer carries `order: true` when only an order is asked (library ordering — every card picked, in the order they go); its bot candidates for an `"exile"` destination (a graveyard-exile cost) rank the least valuable first, as a sacrifice's and a discard's do. `shared/random-cast.ts` casts a spree offer with one of its `castModal.modeSets`. `choose-permanents` also asks which creature blights (rule 701.68a — the awaiting's and offer's `blight`): exactly one, its bot candidates each creature, the least valuable first. `choose-from-zone` also asks a behold (rule 701.4a — `Game.beginBehold`, the awaiting's `behold`, the offer's `behold: true`): its `ids` span the chooser's hand and their permanents, `destination` `"behold"` (nothing moves) or `"exile"` (the Champions, ranked least valuable first); `applyBehold` carries it out. `choose-number` also carries an assist's helper (rule 702.132a): with `assist` (an `AssistAsk`) instead of `then`/`resume`, its answer is how much of a spell's generic mana that player pays — the same offer, controller arm and client control, the spell visible to every seat meanwhile (`view.ts`). `choose-modes` also pays a waterbend cost (rule 701.67a — an `unless` option's `waterbend`, Waterbending Lesson): the awaiting's `costWaterbend`, the offer's `waterbend` (a `WaterbendOffer`, from the read ctx's `modesWaterbendOffer`), the answer's `waterbend` taps checked by `whyModesWaterbendIsWrong`; named none, `Game.modesWaterbendPayment` taps the fewest that pay.
  - **Opening-hand actions (rule 103.6a)**: once the mulligans end (or the hands are dealt), `Game.beginOpeningHandActions` asks the starting player, then each other player in turn order, which of their hand's `beginsOnBattlefield` cards (the Leylines) to put onto the battlefield — a `choose-from-zone` flagged `openingHand` (`{ next }`, the next seat to ask), its ids hidden from the other seats in `view.ts`, answered by `applyOpeningHand` and never searched by the bots (`candidates` returns none, so v1 takes them all). The definition's object form is more of the same permission (Gemstone Caverns): `notStartingPlayer` isn't offered to the player who starts, `counters` go on as it arrives, and `thenExileFromHand` asks that many of what's left of the hand before anyone else is asked — the same flag with `destination: "exile"`, where v1 gives up what a mulligan would bottom. Then the same player is asked which `openingHandReveal` cards (the Chancellors, rule 103.6b) to reveal — `destination: "reveal"`, `next` counting two questions a seat — and each revealed card stays in the hand and sets up its delayed trigger (`createDelayedTrigger`, `at` a first upkeep or main phase). A player holding none isn't asked; turn 1 begins after the last. **While a player could mulligan (rule 103.5b)**, a hand card's `mulliganHandAbility` (Serum Powder) is offered beside keep and mulligan as its own `mulligan-hand-ability` offer and action of the `mulligan` decision (three actions on one kind; `applyMulliganHandAbility`): the hand goes to exile, that many are drawn, `MulliganHandState.redraws` goes up, `taken` doesn't, and they declare again; bots use it on a hand they would have thrown back. Mulligans stop at an opening hand of zero (rule 103.5): `mayTakeMulligan` in `decisions/shared/mulligan-math.ts`, the offer's `mustKeep`. A hand a short library left under what's owed gives what it has.
- **`combat/`** — `eligibility.ts`'s `attacksAsThoughHaste` (2026-11-11) reads `StaticAbility.attackAsThoughHaste` where summoning sickness would stop an attack in `whyCannotAttack` (rules 302.6, 702.10b), per defender for `"your-opponents"` (Frenzied Saddlebrute); it grants no haste. A blocker needing company with none who could stand beside it (`company.ts`'s `hasBlockCompany`, attackers holding one creature each) isn't eligible at all (509.1b), and two different "must be blocked by" filters on an attacker are two requirements (the offer's `mustBeBlockedByEach`, `blocking.ts`'s `requirementsOn` and `ifAblePlan`). `eligibility.ts`'s `whyCannotAttack` refuses a creature an `additional-combat`'s `PlayerEffect.combat` covers (rule 508.1c — Bumi, Unleashed: `additional-combat`'s `onlyAttackers` rides on the queued `AddedPhase`, and `Game.bindForcedAttackers` pushes the effect as that phase begins, ending at its end of combat). `eligibility.ts`'s `blocksDespiteLandwalk` is the blocker's side of rule 702.14 (`StaticAbility.blocksDespiteLandwalk`, Street Savvy), read for both named and filter landwalk, and by the bots' `canBlock`. The combat predicates as free functions over `(state, registry, …)`, so decision modules and the client's public seam can ask without holding a `Game`. `eligibility.ts`: `whyCannotAttack`, `whyCannotBlock`, `legalDefenders`, `creatureDef`, `landwalkStopsBlocksBy` (rule 702.14c, a landwalk of a kind no keyword names, its land a filter off `Characteristics.landwalk`, unless a `blockableDespiteLandwalk` names `"landwalk"` — what a block and the bots' `canBlock` both ask), `attackRequirementsForbid` (rule 508.1d — the most attack requirements obeyed, per creature: a goad's "a player other than [the goader]", which a planeswalker doesn't satisfy, encore's "that opponent", a `must-attack` record's; `mustAttack` is whether any can be), and **`defendersForAttacker`** — which defenders *this* attacker may be sent at, not the union, a distinction goad (701.15b) and Vow of Duty make load-bearing. `whyCannotAttack`'s `AttackAsOf.nextTurn` asks as of its controller's next combat instead of now, for `playersAttackableNextTurn`. `damage.ts`: `standardAssignment` (the one copy of the default split — kill as many blockers as possible, the ones needing least first and indestructible ones last, then trample the rest over or leave it on a blocker — shared by `Game`, every controller and the client's damage bar), `lethalFor`, `liveBlockersOf` (still on the battlefield *and* still blocking — an id outlives its object, so a blocker that died and came back before damage, Reassembling Skeleton, isn't one: rules 400.7, 506.4; a zone change or a change of control runs `Game.removeFromCombat`, which also strikes it from its attacker's `blockedBy`), `needsDamageAssignmentChoice`, `damageAssignmentViolations` (rule 510.1c). `blocking.ts`: `blockingViolations` — the set-level rules (menace, Lure, "must be blocked if able" via `ifAblePlan`, and a creature's own requirement to block — `block-requirement`, the offer's `blockRequirements` from `must-block` records on the blocker's modifiers (the `restrict` effect): provoke — whose blocker may obey a Lure instead, rule 509.1c counting both alike) that can only be judged once a whole declaration is known, checked **against the offer** rather than the board so the engine's validator and the client's Confirm button cannot disagree; `obeyingLure` is the fix-up every bot policy finishes its blocks with. `company.ts` holds what the restrictions keyed to the other creatures in combat ask ("can't attack or block alone", "can only attack alone", "at least two others"): `attackCompanyOf`/`blockCompanyOf` read a creature's combat words (`restrictionsOf`), and `ableInCompany` says who has any legal group to be in (the offers and `Game.declareAttackersStep` leave out the rest); `attacking.ts`'s `attackCompanyViolations` (with the most requirements obeyed, 508.1d, `mostRequirementsObeyed`) and `blocking.ts`'s `blockCompanyViolations` are checked against the offers' `company`/`blockCompany` by the validators, and `withinAttackCompany`/`withinBlockCompany` make a driver's declaration legal, with no draws. `attacking.ts` is its attack-side twin: `attackingViolations` — a creature that must attack if able ("attacks each combat if able", goad, encore — the offer's `mustAttack`) left out of the declaration, which the validator refuses (rule 508.1d) and the client won't confirm — and `withRequiredAttackers`, which sends a left-out one at its first defender for a driver that doesn't choose (a bot, a test's scripted player, via the decision's `ask`). `stack-counts.ts`: a declaration's `count` — how many of a compacted token stack attack or block with one entry (rules 508.1a / 509.1a: each token's choice), several entries naming one stack when each gives a count, an entry without one the whole stack; `whyCountsInvalid` is both validators' check, `declaredTokens` what the must-attack check reads (a stack that must attack attacks whole), and `blockingViolations` weighs an entry by its count for menace and Lure. `Game.materializeStack` wakes exactly that many and leaves the rest compacted (an attack past the wake-up cap is one counted attacker instead, `attackingStackPart`). A static's records (`cant-be-blocked` `by`, `cant-block` `attackers`, `cant-attack` `at`) are read in `whyCannotBlock`/`whyCannotAttack` rather than folded into characteristics, so their scopes may ask about power; a block filter's `{ amount }` operands read its static's own source (`goad.ts`'s `sourceAmount` — Champion of Lambholt's "power less than this creature's power"). `Game` keeps one-line delegates for its own callers. Skulk (rule 702.118b: no blocker with greater power) is in `whyCannotBlock` and the bot's `combat-math.ts` `canBlock`. `whyCannotAttack` hands `canAttackDespiteDefender` the defender, so a permission that names only some defenders (Weathered Sentinels) is judged per defender. Shadow (rule 702.28b) is one of the block checks: a creature with it blocks and is blocked only by creatures with it. `blockedByAtMostOne` (eligibility, a `byMoreThan: 1` record, read at declaration) feeds the blockers offer's `singleBlockerAttackers`; `blockingViolations` reports a `single-blocker` violation, `withinSingleBlockers` trims a self-built declaration (and `obeyingLure` ends with it), and a Lured attacker that can be blocked by only one creature is offered as `mustBeBlockedIfAble` (rule 509.1c). **Battles** (rule 310): `legalDefenders` offers each battle whose protector (`GameObject.protector`, chosen as a Siege enters — `Game.askEnterChoice`'s `protector`, reassigned by `Game.protectorValid` as a state-based action) is an opponent; `defendingPlayerOf` is a battle's protector; a battle can't attack or block. Damage to one removes defense counters (`Game.dealDamage`), and one with none — or no legal protector — goes to the graveyard (`stateBasedGraveyardMoves`, a Siege waiting while its own trigger is pending). **Banding** (rule 702.22) is `banding.ts`: `whyBandsInvalid` (the attackers validator's check of `AttackerDeclaration.band` — banding and up to one without, or a shared quality one member "bands with other" of, one defender, a stack waking whole), `bandMatesOf`, `blockedAttackersOf` (the attacker a blocker was declared on plus `GameObject.blockingAlso`, the rest of a band it blocked), `damageAssigner` (who divides a creature's combat damage: the other side when banding is among the creatures it's blocking or blocked by — 702.22j-k) and `opposedAssignment` (that side's default: nothing dies if it fits, else only the smallest). `damage.ts`'s `damageQuestionFor` is the whole question for any creature dealing damage — an attacker among its blockers or a blocker among the attackers it blocks (510.1d, `assign-combat-damage`'s `blocking`), with `forOpponent` — and `defaultAssignment` every controller's default answer. `damageAssignmentViolations` takes nothing as the answer for a blocked creature with no blocker left and no trample (510.1c). "As though it weren't blocked" (2026-10-30): `characteristics.ts`'s `unblockedAssignmentOf` (`"may"`/`"must"`, its characteristics plus `GameState.turnUnblockedAssignments`) decides it; `Game.runCombatDamageSubPass` always asks a blocked attacker that may (the decision's `mayUnblocked`, or `unblockedOnly` first when banding hands the division over), records a yes in `CombatDamageState.asThoughUnblocked`, and `dealCombatDamage` sends such an attacker's damage, or a must one's, to what it's attacking (510.1b); `controller.ts`'s `prefersUnblocked` is the automatic answer. `stack-counts.ts` exports `MAX_MATERIALIZED`, which `Game` and the band check share. `eligibility.ts`'s block filters also read granted statics (`grantedStatics`) and a `restrict`'s `cant-be-blocked` record — on the attacker's modifiers or a turn-wide rule it matches (B253). `cantAttackUnlessDefender` / `cantAttackIfDefender` / `cantBlockUnlessAttacker` are a permanent's own statics, printed or granted (`ownStatics`) testing a `PlayerTest` (`playerTestHolds`: controls, `holds` — any condition read as that player, "you control more") of the attack's defending player or the block's attacking player (2026-10-29). **Block minimums** (2026-10-30): `minimumBlockers` — 2 for menace, N for a static's `blockedByAtLeast` ("except by three or more creatures"), the largest — makes the offer's `menaceAttackers` every attacker needing two or more and `minBlockers` the ones needing more; `blocking.ts`'s `leastBlockers(offer, attacker)` is what `lurePlan`, `ifAblePlan` (each attacker's smallest groups of entries making N), `requirementTargets`, `obeyingLure` and the `menace` violation (now with `needs`) read in place of "two with menace". `blockedOnlyByAll` (Tromokratis): `whyCannotBlock` — now `whyThisCannotBlock` judged per creature, then this check — refuses every one of the defender's creatures when one can't block it, the offer lists it as `blockedByAll`, and a partial block is the `blocked-by-all` violation. A blocker's modifier `cantBlock` (`restrict`'s `cantBlockAttacker`, held by the attacker's `zoneChangeCount`) bars it from one attacker; the `"must-block"` restriction joins the offer's `blockRequirements`. `goad.ts`'s `sourceAmount` answers a plain `{ countOf }` too (Kraken of the Straits). Attack-requirement rules (2026-10-31) bind by `attackRequirementRulesOf` (active, `object`, `controlledBy`, filter); one with `attacks` is an aimed requirement beside encore's (`aimedRequirements`), counted by `aimedRequirementsObeyed` and binding only while its defender can be attacked. `tax.ts` (2026-11-01): what particular creatures cost to attack and block (`StaticAbility.combatTax`, the `blockTax` player effect, rules 508.1h, 509.1d) read off the board, `creatureAttackTax(es)` and `blockTaxRates`, feeding the offers' `attackTax.perAttacker` and `blockTax` and `Game`'s payment; `blocking.ts`'s `blockTaxTotal` adds a declaration up (a blocker once, however many it blocks; a tax on an attacker per creature on it), `blockIsFree` keeps a taxed block out of `lurePlan`, `ifAblePlan` and `requirementTargets` (509.1c), and `withinBlockTax` cuts a driver's. `whyCannotAttack` reads who a "can't attack you" covers through `tax.ts`'s `attackCovers`: `at: "you"` the player alone, `"you-and-planeswalkers"` their planeswalkers too, neither a battle; also the `cantAttack` player effect and `cant-attack-player-attacked-this-turn` (`GameObject.attackedPlayersThisTurn`, set at the declaration). Requirements only a whole declaration answers (rules 508.1d, 509.1c): the blockers offer's `mustBeBlockedByOneOf` and `mustBeBlockedByExactlyOne`, read by `blockingViolations` and `ifAblePlan`, and its `blockRequirements` from a static's `must-be-blocked` record or a `PlayerEffect.combat` `must-block` record's `attacker`; the attackers offer's `attackIf` (`attacksIfOthersAttack`, `attacksIfAttacks`), read by `attackingViolations`, which a limit on attackers bends; a token stack bound to block wakes as blockers are asked for (`Game.wakeStacksBoundToBlock`), so a requirement each token answers for reaches each. `whyCannotAttack`'s `cant-attack-owner-planeswalkers` is Xantcha's half of "its owner or planeswalkers its owner controls". `canBeAttacked` (2026-11-04) reads the `cant-be-attacked` restriction on what's attacked (The Aetherspark), for `whyCannotAttack` and the attackers offer's `defenders`, and stays out of `legalDefenders`, which also answers what a creature put onto the battlefield attacking may choose (rule 508.4c).
- **`primitives.ts`** — branded `PlayerId`/`ObjectId`, seeded `Rng` (mulberry32), `shuffle`.
- **`auto-settle.ts`** — `isSettled(state)` / `autoSettle(game)`: cascades past every "dead" priority window in one go, and past every zone choice with only one answer (`forcedZoneChoice(state)`: every card shown taken, nothing to order, split or pick for someone else, going to the hand, battlefield or graveyard — Dark Confidant's revealed card), which `Room.autoAdvanceHumanSeat` answers the same way for a human seat. `Room` uses `isSettled` as its per-hop predicate but deliberately never `autoSettle` — it has to stop at every bot action to publish a frame, and per-seat auto-pass/skip-mana-only are one-shot-per-call, neither of which survives a cascade. `autoSettle` itself is now only used by tests/scripts that want a game settled in one call. `forcedZoneChoice` also answers a forbidden search with nothing else to find.
- **`stack-copies.ts`** — `stackCopyKey(object)`: abilities on the stack that are copies of one another as a player sees them — one controller, a card of the same name, the same ability index, no targets, no X, no modes (Mesmeric Orb's trigger for each permanent that untapped). Reads only fields a `GameObject` and a `VisibleObject` share, so both sides use it: the client draws a run as one stack entry and plays its exits as one, and the room resolves a run asked to resolve all in one frame. Each is still its own object, resolving with priority between.
- **`stack-preview.ts`** — `wouldAffectPlayer(state, registry, object, player)`: whether a spell or ability on the stack would affect a player when it resolves — played on a copy (`Game.fromSnapshot`, every seat the bots' combat stand-in, v1, answering what is asked), the player's side printed when the object reaches the top and again once it has gone: their player record, their zones, their spells on the stack, and each permanent they control as it is and through the layers. `null` when it isn't on the stack or leaves without resolving; an error reads as `true`. The server's auto-pass asks it of an opponent's cast or activation (the user, 2026-11-11). Exported from the barrel and `engine/local` (the browser's solo rooms run the same room code).

Snapshot/restore: `game.snapshot()` → `cloneGameState(state)` (`state.ts` — a plain-tree deep copy, ~2× faster than `structuredClone`, which stays the reference in `test/clone-state.test.ts`; an object's snapshots (`lastKnown`, `earlierLastKnown`, `lastOnStack`) are shared, not copied — only ever replaced whole, never written into, 18% of a late four-player state; `setFreezeSharedSnapshots` (the fuzzer's `MTG_FREEZE_SNAPSHOTS=1`) freezes them so a write would throw; bot search clones a state per simulated candidate, so this is the "candidates per decision budget" path); `Game.fromSnapshot(snap, env)` rebuilds (re-supply `registry`/`controllers` via `env`). Determinism: same seed + same controllers ⇒ identical replay.

## Engine commands, in full

- `npm run token:art -w engine` (`-- --write` to patch) — finds a real Scryfall printing for every `cards/tokens/` entry. `test/art-pins.test.ts` fails on any token left unpinned that isn't on its list of tokens Scryfall has no printing of, so a new token can't ship without art. A token can't resolve art by name the way a card does: ours are named for the engine key ("3/3 Beast Token") and Scryfall names printings after the creature alone ("Beast"), so `CardImage` skips the lookup for a token with no `art` and draws a text panel — which made tokens render hit-or-miss. The link comes from Scryfall's `all_parts` (the "Tokens" list on a card page) reached through the pool's own `create-token` effects, then matched on P/T, colours and subtypes. Keywords **rank** rather than filter, because both directions occur: Wurmcoil Engine's two Wurms differ only by deathtouch vs lifelink, while Chandra, Acolyte of Flame's Elemental is a vanilla printing whose haste comes from the spell. A printing carrying *fewer* subtypes than the definition (Oracle errata the physical token predates — "Wurm" vs "Phyrexian Wurm") matches at a lower tier and is flagged for eyeballing.
- `npm run gen:edhrec-rank -w engine` — rebuilds `cards/edhrec-rank.ts` from Scryfall (checked in; rerun as the pool grows, or to refresh the ranking).
- `npm run gen:release-dates -w engine [-- --refresh]` — rebuilds `cards/release-date.ts` from Scryfall's first printings (checked in; two to three minutes uncached; rerun after `gen:oracle` or a batch of new cards)
- `npm run gen:missing-cards -w engine` — writes `client/public/missing-cards.json`, every Commander-legal card in the Oracle snapshot the pool lacks, with its Oracle rules text for the search, for the library's "Not in the pool yet" filter (runs on `dist/`; verify's marks step reruns it, so commit it when it changes).
- `npm run gen:salt-score -w engine [-- --online]` — rebuilds `cards/salt-score.ts` from the checked-in `data/edhrec-salt.json` (rerun as the pool grows); `--online` first re-walks EDHREC's salt list into that snapshot (~250 requests, one a second).
- `npm run gen:flavor-names -w engine` — rebuilds `cards/flavor-names.ts` from Scryfall (checked in; rerun when a set with flavor names comes out).
- `npm run card:brief -w engine -- "Card Name" [--analogs N] [--width N] [--no-rulings]` — everything known about a card before authoring it (`engine/scripts/card-brief.mjs`): Oracle text and rulings, its status (pool, sample-deck stand-in, backlog ranks), every triage record with each need read through the need vocabulary and flagged when built since the record, and per Oracle line the closest abilities already in the pool — TF-IDF over normalised text (own name and "this creature" → `~`, numbers → `N`, `X` kept, reminder text dropped, words and word pairs) against every ability with its own `text` in the pool, printed with its file and authored shape. The abilities, and the pool's card names, come from the pool index (below), so it needs no build.
- `npm run card:find -w engine -- "<phrase>" [--kind K] [--json S] [--limit N] [--width N] [--tokens]` — the pool's abilities that read most like a phrase (`engine/scripts/card-find.mjs`), in well under a second: ranked by the same TF-IDF as `card:brief`'s analogs, one per card, each printed with its card, file, kind, text and authored shape; a phrase no ability's words match falls back to a substring search of text and shape. `--kind` keeps one kind (triggered, activated, static, spell, keyword, or the field an ability sits under — `modes`, `chapters`, `grantsTriggered` — a prefix is enough); `--json` keeps abilities whose shape contains a substring, ignoring case (and quotes, when the substring has none: `on:dies`), and with no phrase lists them smallest shape first; `--tokens` adds token definitions.
- **The pool index** (`engine/scripts/pool-index.mjs`) — what `card:brief`, `card:find` and `card:vocab` read instead of importing ~15,000 card modules on every run (8 s and 6.4 s warm before it; minutes on a loaded machine). Two gitignored files in `engine/.cache/`: `pool-index.jsonl`, a header (version, build time, card-file count, `poolNames()`'s name → file map) then one line per ability `{ card, file, kind, where, text, grams, spec }` (pool and tokens, keywords as their own lines), and `pool-vocab.json`, each definition's vocabulary terms in walk order plus its size, so `card:vocab`'s counts and smallest examples replay exactly. Built from the sources, not `dist/`: rolldown bundles one entry importing every card file (~3 s), so no build is needed. `gen:cards` (and so `build`/`typecheck`) writes it when stale, except under `CI`; each tool's load stats every card file and `src/cards/*.ts`/`src/*.ts` on a worker thread while it parses the index, and rebuilds when any is newer than the build, a card file was added or removed, or `VERSION` changed.
- `npm run decks:stand-ins -w engine [-- --implemented | "Card" …]` — the sample decks' stand-ins (`engine/scripts/stand-ins.mjs`): with no arguments, what's left deck by deck with each original's triage needs; `--implemented` deletes every `sub(…)` whose original is now in the pool (what `sample-decks.test.ts` demands), names delete those; either rewrites `BACKLOG.md`'s "precons' N stand-ins" to the count left. `card:brief -- --test` prints a `describe` on the harness with an `it.todo` per ability and per ruling.
- `npm run card:vocab -w engine -- <term> | --unused | --list [prefix]` — the definition vocabulary, generated (`engine/scripts/card-vocab.mjs`): every term the registered cards and tokens use (`kind=…`, `on=…`, `<owner>.<field>`, enum-like `…=value`) with counts and the three smallest example cards, joined to the declarations in `effects.ts`/`abilities.ts`/`target.ts`/`cards/define.ts`/`replacements.ts`/`filter.ts`/`mana.ts` (each `kind`/`on` union member and `readonly` field with the doc comment above it) and to `cards/AUTHORING.md`'s matching lines. `--unused`: declared kinds no card uses. The usage comes from the pool index (above); the declarations are read from the sources each run.
- `npm run card:digest -w engine [-- --out <file>]` — the same vocabulary as one page (`engine/scripts/card-digest.mjs`, default `engine/.cache/authoring-digest.md`, ~125 KB): the card fields, every `EffectSpec`, `EffectAmount`, `TargetSpec`, `TriggerSpec` and `StaticCondition` member and the `CardFilter`, `ActivatedAbility`, `AbilityCost`, `TriggeredAbility` and `StaticAbility` fields, each with its own fields, its doc's first sentence, its card count and smallest example. Parsed from the sources' declarations (a small brace/string/comment-aware scanner splitting each union at its top-level `|` and each object at its top-level `;`), joined to the pool index's usage. Written for authoring agents, who otherwise grep the sources one option at a time while triaging; the author-by-set lead stages it with their briefs.
- `npm run cards:goal -w engine [-- --rank 10000] [--size small|major|unsized] [--keys] [--json]` (`scripts/rank-goal.mjs`): every card to an EDHREC rank that is not in the pool, best first, with its latest record's open need keys and the size that record gave (B297 on); `--keys` groups by key, one build unit each.
- `npm run cards:needs -w engine -- --rank [--top N] | --stale | --feature <key> | --next [N] | --check | --audit | --greedy [--groups] [--top N] [--family F] | --brief <family> [--open-only] [--page N] | --asap [--prune]` — the cards the user flagged "Build ASAP" in the library's dev view (`engine/data/build-asap.json`, `loadAsap`) come first in `--by-rank`, `--next` and `--stale --next`, whatever their rank or Commander status, marked `[ASAP]`; `--asap` lists each flag as landed, untriaged, every need built, or blocked on its open keys, and `--prune` unflags the landed ones (the `author-flagged` skill). Otherwise: the triage records (`blocked` lists in `engine/data/sweep-2/`, `sweep-3/`, and the commanders in `top-commanders-gaps.json`) read through one need vocabulary (`engine/scripts/needs-vocabulary.mjs`): `engine/data/needs-vocabulary.json` aliases every raw key a record used onto a gaps-file feature or onto a narrower feature of its own (with a `family` from the gaps file). `--rank` needs by unimplemented cards waiting (and how many each fully unblocks), rolled up by family; `--stale` cards with a need built since their record (a key's build date is the commit that first added it to the gaps file's `built`, a record's date its file's first commit); `--feature` the cards waiting on one need; `--next [N]` the next N Commander-legal cards by EDHREC rank (Oracle snapshot) that are neither in the pool nor triaged. `--audit` counts what to fix before building from the records (every need built, since triage or before; cards whose records disagree; open keys with no description or example; merge candidates — the same suffix under two prefixes, or in one family one suffix inside another or most tokens shared) and writes the lists to `needs-audit.txt` in the OS temp dir; `--greedy` the build order over every record, a pair of keys as one step when two free more together than the best single key would per key, with milestones and the two-key pairs (`--family` builds only one family's keys); `--greedy --groups [--top N] [--family F]` the same order with the vocabulary's generalisation groups as build units (a group's open keys built together, beside every open key alone), each step naming its family and the group's shape (Phase 2's order, the `build-family` skill); `--brief` the compact text a model judges a family from (open keys with cards waiting, description and two whys, then the family's built keys), paged at ~4K tokens. `--recheck [--size N] [--batch K] [--freeze]` lists the cards worth attempting again (`--freeze` writes every batch to `engine/data/recheck/R<K>.txt`, the frozen lists a parallel recheck round assigns, since `--batch K` shifts as batches land): no open need, or every open need judged to exist (a feature's `checked: "…: exists"`), sorted by need, in batches. The vocabulary JSON also holds `groups` (generalisation groups from the merge, `{ keys, shape, from }`), `mergedInto` on a feature aliased away (left out of `features`, resolving through its alias), and `checked`, `wasDescription` and `existsEvidence` from the existence check. The prompts the plan's Haiku agents run on `--brief`'s output are `engine/scripts/needs-prompts/exists.md` (does the engine already do this?) and `merge.md` (aliases and generalisation groups). `poolNames` reads a card's name from a helper's first argument as well as from `name:`, and matches a multi-face card authored under one face by its full name and every face that names no other card (the Oracle snapshot; a prepare card's back face can be a card of its own). `test/needs-vocabulary.test.ts` fails on a record need the vocabulary doesn't know.
- `npm run cards:sets -w engine -- --sets [N] | --set <code> | --next [N] [--top P] [--ranked-only] [--oldest] [--refresh]` — the card backlog set by set (`engine/scripts/set-backlog.mjs`; `--oldest` walks from the oldest set): Scryfall's paper expansions, core sets, Commander products, draft-innovation, masters and starter sets released by today and the ones not out yet through their previews (the soonest first, a main set before its Commander set, ahead of the released ones; `--released-only` leaves them out), then newest first (`--oldest`: oldest first, all of them); each set's Commander-legal first printings (`e:<code> is:firstprint legal:commander`, or `game:paper` for a set not out yet, whose previews aren't legal until release; ordered by EDHREC), kept when in the set's top P% by EDHREC rank (default 100, the not-yet-ranked last unless `--ranked-only`); each marked done (a pool card, a double-faced card by its front face too), triaged (named by a `data/sweep-*/` record) or todo, and flagged when the Oracle snapshot lacks it. Scryfall's answers are cached three days in `engine/.cache/scryfall/sets/` (`--refresh` refetches); a rate-limited fetch waits out Scryfall's `Retry-After` and tries again.
- `npm run card:lookup -w engine -- "Card Name"` — the card's authoritative mana cost/type line/Oracle text/rulings and the tokens it makes (`engine/scripts/scryfall-lookup.mjs`), read from the checked-in **Oracle snapshot** (`engine/data/oracle/cards.jsonl` — every Commander-legal card and token, one line each, rulings folded in), so it works offline and in cloud sessions; only a card the snapshot lacks goes to Scryfall. Run this before authoring a new card — see `cards/AUTHORING.md` §1.
- `npm run gen:oracle -w engine` — rebuilds that snapshot from Scryfall's bulk data (Oracle Cards + Rulings, downloaded whole into the git-ignored `engine/.cache/scryfall/`, ~40s); rerun when a set comes out. A paper set not out yet has its previewed cards kept too (Scryfall calls them not Commander-legal until release): `"commander": "legal"` with a `"preview"` field naming the release date, which the first refresh after release drops — so a set can be authored the week before (Star Trek, 2026-11-06).
- `npm run card:scaffold -w engine -- "Card Name" | --next N [--cards] | --auto-scan [--all]` — writes card files from the snapshot into `engine/src/cards/scaffold/`: the stat block, `text`, rulings, keywords, pairing and tokens, and then whatever `scripts/oracle-parse.mjs` reads of the rules text. It reads two tiers: an ability's structure (cost or trigger, targets, restrictions) and its effect, whenever a whole sentence matches a template. Each line it can't read is left as a `// TODO(scaffold):`. A card whose every line was read goes, finished, into `engine/src/cards/review/`. `--report` writes nothing and lists the unparsed lines that recur most and those that are a face's only unread line (`--sole N`). Keyword lines become the engine's helper output (`dist/cards/helpers.js` — crew, saddle, undying, unearth, …); "whenever ~ attacks while saddled" is an attack trigger with the saddled `whileCondition`, "becomes saddled [for the first time each turn]" a `becomes-saddled` trigger, "Enlist" the `enlist()` static and "whenever ~ enlists a creature" an `enlists` trigger, "whenever ~ crews a Vehicle / saddles a Mount [during your main phase], that Vehicle …" a `crews-or-saddles` trigger, "target creature that crewed / saddled it this turn" `crewedSource` / `saddledSource`, "enchanted creature can't attack, block, or crew Vehicles" the `cant-crew` restriction, "~ [saddles Mounts and] crews Vehicles as though its power were N greater" / "…using its toughness rather than its power" a `crewsAs` static or card fields (convoke, morph, disturb, suspend, landcycling, affinity); "~ becomes a copy of [another] target … [until end of turn][, except it has this ability]" (and "you may have ~ become …") a `become-copy` of the source, "this ability" named by the card and the ability's place in its list (`THIS_ABILITY`, filled in at the end of `parseFace`); a target may carry modifiers (`modifiedTargetOf`: tapped, attacking, nonbasic, "with flying", "with power N or greater"); a few intervening-ifs become `condition`s (`INTERVENING_IFS`), and a static's trailing "as long as …" on a line the rest reads whole its `condition` (`STATIC_CONDITION_SUFFIXES` — celebration's, both ways); "it" is the source or the trigger's object in an effect's first sentence (`itOf`). Moving counters reads ("move a +1/+1 counter from ~ onto …", "move all counters from ~ onto …", "move a counter / any number of counters from target … onto a second target …") as `move-counters`, graft N as its replacement and trigger, and on a dies or leaves trigger (`departedOf`) "put its/those counters on …" as `put-counters-of` and "if it had counters on it," as a `self-counters` condition or a `counters` clause on each trigger's filter; "whenever ~ or another X you control dies / leaves the battlefield" is the two triggers `thisOrAnother` writes, and "is put into a graveyard from the battlefield" a `dies` trigger. On 2026-10-11 that took the cards read whole from 252 to 1,686 of the snapshot's unimplemented ones. `npm run card:parse-check -w engine` compares everything the parser claims — abilities, a spell's effect and modes, statics and card fields — with the hand-authored pool, equivalent spellings counted as one (`canon`), and `test/oracle-parse.test.ts` requires zero disagreements. Since 2026-10-31 the command runs `scripts/parse-check.mjs`, not a build: it hashes every source (in parallel, ~2 s), runs `gen-cards.mjs` only when its inputs' recorded outputs aren't what's on disk, bundles the card barrel and `cards/helpers.ts` from `src/` with rolldown (the parser's `dist/cards/helpers.js` import redirected to that bundle by a module hook), and caches the bundle and the report in the OS temp dir under content-hash keys — the same report byte for byte as `node scripts/oracle-parse-check.mjs` after a build (which still works, against `dist/`), but no type-check (~106 s → 13 s after an engine edit, 11 s after a parser edit, 2.5 s unchanged, beside another agent's work; `--no-cache` ignores the caches). **Neither folder is registered** — authoring (or checking, with `card:verify -- --dir review`) and then moving the file into `pool/` is what puts a card in the game. `pool.test.ts` fails on a `TODO(scaffold)` in `pool/` or `tokens/`. See `cards/AUTHORING.md` §1.
- `node scripts/batch-review.mjs --since <commit> [--out <file>] [--all]` (from `engine/`) — the lead's review aid for an agent batch (`.claude/authoring/agent-batches.md`): every pool and token file changed since `<commit>` (and untracked pool files) written beside its Oracle text to one file, and on stdout only the cards worth reading in full — untested ones with a condition, a replacement, a cost paid as it resolves, a delayed trigger, a library choice or several target slots, dense tested ones, and stats or a mana cost that disagree with the snapshot — so a batch's review doesn't print every card into the conversation.
- `npm run card:verify -w engine` — diffs every `cards/pool/` card's mana cost/colors/types/subtypes/P-T/loyalty against real Scryfall data, batched (`engine/scripts/verify-cards.mjs`). Structural fields only, not ability text. See `cards/AUTHORING.md` §16. `-- --offline` (with `card:text` too) answers from the Oracle snapshot (`data/oracle/cards.jsonl`) instead of the API, for a session whose network can't reach Scryfall. **Every card in `pool/` is a real Magic card** — the pool's 27 invented placeholders were replaced with real printings of the same mechanic (see `neededCards-features.md` "E2"), so a `NOT FOUND` from this script now means a made-up or misspelled card, not a known exception. `tokens/` is exempt (token names aren't unique on Scryfall).
- `npm run card:text -w engine` — the same script's `--text` mode: the **rules-text** audit. It splits each card's `text` and Scryfall's `oracle_text` into clauses (one per line, then per sentence), matches every clause of the real card to its closest clause in ours by a set-similarity score over normalised tokens (the card's own name, "this creature", punctuation and reminder text are all normalised away first), and reports what didn't match — `MISSING` for a clause the pool dropped, `EXTRA` for one it has that the real card doesn't; `--similar` also shows the loose matches. Two false-positive classes are suppressed: a bare keyword line already on `def.keywords` (the client renders those from the keyword list) and a typed dual land's `{T}: Add` (implicit in Scryfall's type line, rule 305.6). **`cards/AUTHORING.md` §0 ("faithful, or not at all") is the standard this measures against**: a card whose printed behaviour the engine can't run is not authored at all, so every `MISSING` line should be either a paraphrase of `text` or one of the pre-rule cards on §15's "Known exceptions" debt list — never a new omission. It is still not a hard gate, and it is **blind to a clause that is present but wrong** (Saw in Half's tokens were the wrong size inside a clause it scored as matching), so read the output rather than counting it. `--lines` is the older, narrower check that compares only the number of text lines (the client renders `text` verbatim, so a missing `\n` runs two abilities together).
- `npm run test -w engine` — vitest, single pass (CI mode); `-- path/to/file.test.ts` for one file, `-- -t "name"` for a name match
- `npm run test:watch -w engine` — vitest watch mode
- `npm run build -w engine` / `npm run typecheck -w engine` (the source, then the tests through `tsconfig.tests.json`; `typecheck:tests` is the second half alone) — the build is `tsc`, then `scripts/bundle-shards.mjs`: each `dist/cards/shards/shard-NN.js` bundled (rolldown, pinned as the engine's own devDependency) with its card files inlined and every shared module left an import — and the card barrel, `dist/cards/generated.js`, the same way, so a Node import of the pool (the server, its tests, the fuzzer, the bots' benches) loads one module, not 16,000 (5.1 s → 0.5 s; the server suite 148 s → 19 s, 2026-10-20). Vite's dev server doesn't bundle, so without it the library and deck builder made one request per card (~5,800) and took 39 s to open after the dev server started; with it, ~160 requests and under 1.5 s. A production client build comes out the same either way. Only the client loads shards, so nothing else sees the difference; a `tsc --watch` build leaves them unbundled, which still works, just slowly. **The `tsc` is incremental** (2026-10-31; its state in `dist/.tsbuildinfo`, so deleting `dist` resets it): ~140 s cold, ~4 s warm, ~8 s after a one-line `game.ts` edit (the whole build ~11 s warm; `gen:cards`'s pool index adds ~23 s when an engine source is newer). `tsc` never checks that its outputs still exist, so `scripts/dist-complete.mjs` runs first and deletes the build info (a full emit) when any `.js`/`.d.ts`/map for a source in `src/` is missing; and since `tsc` re-emits a shard or the barrel only when its own source changes, not a card it inlines, `bundle-shards.mjs` bundles them afresh every build from `tsc`'s raw emit, which it keeps in `dist/.raw/`.
- `npm run play:random -w engine` — `engine/scripts/random-demo.mjs`: N random-vs-random games (`-- --with-file <path>` forces a list of cards, one per line; more than 30 split into groups sharing a colour identity (`fuzz-decks.mjs`'s `forcedChunks`), each forced into games of its own, at least one game per group — how `npm run verify` fuzzes a large sweep; `-- --per-deck N` instead packs the list into ceil(count / N) near-equal groups of like colour identity (`packedChunks`), seed s playing group (s − 1) mod groups — how verify's new-cards fuzzer keeps a long list of new cards from making every deck a pile of them; `-- --games 300`, `-- --log`, `-- --players 3|4`, `-- --progress` to announce each seed on stderr before playing it, `-- --seed 19` to replay exactly one seed, `-- --timeout 60` to set the per-game wall-clock limit in seconds, default 30; `-- --max-turns 60` to stop a game as turn 61 begins, reported as a draw by turn cap and counted apart from real draws — `npm run verify`'s new-cards games use it, since a random game's length is mostly its seed's luck). This is the engine fuzzer — if `legalActions` ever offers something `dispatch` refuses, or an unbounded loop/object-count blowup happens, it reports `FAILED seed N` and exits non-zero. **Each game runs in a worker thread (`random-game-worker.mjs`) that's killed if it overruns the limit**, and reported with the worker's last once-a-second heartbeat — the turn and step, the stack's size, the battlefield's size and its three most repeated names — so a game running long (161 Phoenix Fleet Airships, batch 174) is told from a hang without a replay: a runaway loop *inside* one `tick()` never yields, so neither a timer in the same thread nor `Game.advance`'s tick budget (which only counts ticks that return) can stop it, and before this a single stuck game stalled the whole run silently. A failure no longer aborts the run — every failing seed is collected and listed at the end. **Every seed deals each seat its own 100-card Commander deck from the whole pool** (`engine/scripts/fuzz-decks.mjs`): a commander, then the next window of a fixed walk through the pool's spells and nonbasic lands (the commander chosen, from a random dozen, as the one whose identity fits most of that window), then basics. So a run of seeds sweeps the pool, and a new card is fuzzed with no edit anywhere. A seat only takes the window's cards inside its commander's identity, so at ~5,400 deckable cards 150 two-player seeds reach all but a few dozen, and CI's 68 games (60 two-player, 8 four-player) about seven in eight. `-- --with "Card Name"` (repeatable) puts a card in every seat's deck, and `-- --coverage` plays nothing and reports how much of the pool the seeds reach. A few cards that mean nothing alone bring a partner along (`PACKAGES`: Skullbriar brings Reanimate). These replaced four hand-kept lists that had grown to 150-500 cards each, where a game drew a given card one time in six and never ran out of library.
- `npm run play:situations -w engine` — `engine/scripts/situation-fuzz.mjs`: the **situation fuzzer**, the per-card check `npm run verify` runs on every new card (`-- --with "Card"` repeatable, `-- --with-file <path>`, `-- --scenarios N` per card, default 60, `-- --seed S` the first seed, scenario k being seed S + k, `-- --players 2|4` (default: the seed's pick), `-- --workers N` (default the cores less one, at most 6: on the 13600K four and eight ran the same scenarios a second and twelve fewer, the engine being bound by memory, not cores), `-- --timeout S` per scenario, default 30, five times that with the cache check, which made one board 56 times slower; `-- --cache-check-every N`). Why: a full random game runs 80-130 turns to play a new card a handful of times, and a card's bugs nearly always show when it is first cast, activated or resolved. Each scenario (`situation-scenario.mjs`, run in `situation-worker.mjs` threads through `worker-pool.mjs`, killed past its time like a fuzzer game) builds a small board **through the engine's own entry paths only** — `Game.create` (libraries of basics and a few random spells, no opening hand), `debugSpawn` (silent, or announced for the card's own entry), `debugApplyEffect` (life lost or gained, a token stack, a token copy, an Aura or Equipment attached) and the seats' own answers — so every board is reachable (`debugSpawn` doesn't ask what an Aura enchants, so the generator attaches it to something it can enchant as it enters, rule 303.4f, or puts it in the graveyard when there's nothing — an Aura attached to nothing was its first impossible board): 2 or 4 players, a few random pool permanents a side (some tapped), a hexproof/shroud/ward/protection creature, a token stack, an Aura, Equipment, a copy, cards in hand and graveyard, now and then an opponent's copy of the card and an instant some seat casts at the same time (a spell on the stack). The card goes in alice's hand with lands for its cost and a few more (snow-covered when its text says snow), on the battlefield (its entry announced), in the graveyard or exile when it does something from there (flashback and its kin, abilities and triggers from that zone), or in the command zone (a commander, 15%); the moment is alice's first main phase or (30%) a step of bob's turn where alice holds priority. Then three stages, each with a step cap (`STEP_CAPS`; reaching one is a failure): **setup** (every seat passes, answering any decision at random, while the moment is reached and the board settles), **focus** (alice's `SituationController`, a `RandomController` held to a script, acts with the card — cast, play, activate, mana abilities only when nothing else — up to three times, every choice the `RandomController`'s, while the others pass or answer; it ends once the card has acted and the stack is quiet, or it had nothing to do in alice's own main phase), and **free** (the rest of the turn and two more at random — with stand-alone mana abilities left out but the card's own, and with an empty stack outside a seat's own main phase a seat passes four times in five without scanning its actions: those were most of a scenario's time). After the focus and free stages the state is checked: plain data (no function, class instance, non-finite number or cycle), `structuredClone`-able, a JSON round trip restoring a game (`Game.fromSnapshot`) that offers every seat the same legal actions, and every seat's `viewFor` showing no card in another player's hand or any library unless something shows it (a reveal this turn, a revealed library top, a decision the card is in) — with the computed-value cache checked on every hit (`setComputedCacheCheck`) in one scenario in ten by seed (it costs ~5x). A blank target slot left `undefined` (JSON reads it back as `null`) is reported apart and doesn't fail, until BACKLOG's "Blank target slots don't survive JSON" is fixed. A failure prints the card, seed, stage, the board as laid out, the card's actions, the stack and the one-line command that replays exactly that scenario; failures are grouped by message, object ids blanked. ~170 ms a scenario alone, ~0.4 s each at six workers (~15 a second): a 10-card batch at the default 60 in about 40 s.
- **Bot measurement** (all in `engine/scripts/`, all `worker_threads` pools over `SAMPLE_DECKS`; see `docs/plans/smarter-bots.md`). Seeds come in blocks of `--players` games, one seating per block with the measured seat moving round the table, so deck strength and play/draw cancel (`bot-seating.mjs`, shared). `npm run bot:bench -w engine` — a weight vector's win rate against v1 (`-- --opponent <champion id>|mixed|gauntlet`, `-- --players 4`, `-- --weights JSON`, `-- --games N`, `-- --candidate-options JSON` for `EvalBotOptions` on the measured seat only — how the `"acting"` rollout was benched against the same build). `bot:tune` — a (1+1)-ES over the weights, head-to-head against the incumbent with the gauntlet as a veto. `bot:harvest` then `bot:fit` — harvest labelled positions from self-play, then fit weights to them by logistic regression (the ES buys one accept/reject bit per 200 games; the same games hold ~150k labelled positions). A fit is a lead, not a verdict: it raised `power` to 2.03 where the four-player sweep peaked at 0.5 — a big board predicts a win, which doesn't make a bot that values power highly win more. **Shipped weights are set by four-player sweeps and benches**, or — where a term exists to cancel another, like `idlePower` against `power` — by that structure, with a scenario to show it. `bot:scenarios` — the opponent-independent gate any candidate vector must pass, exits non-zero on a failure, and reports the training scenarios beside it. `bot:benchmarks` (`bot-benchmarks.mjs`) — the lookahead benchmarks with v2 (or `--bot v1`) as Alice, which it solves and how long each took, by kind of lookahead; `--sims`, `--time-ms`, `--rollout`, `--horizon`, `--rollout-decisions`, `--no-follow-ups`, `--plan turn|next-turn` and `--plan-sims` set `EvalBotOptions`, `--only` filters by name; always exits 0. `bot:fit-scenarios` — for each scenario a vector gets wrong, every single weight that would fix it, the nearest value that does and what it breaks; then the vector those levers add up to, pulled one at a time and played for real (`--out` writes it for `bot:bench --weights`; `--joint` adds a coordinate-descent fit, untrustworthy on a small corpus). **Diagnostics, which is where most of the real findings came from**: `bot:behaviour` plays a few dozen self-play games and counts what a watching player would notice — targets aimed at the wrong side of the table (judged by `target-polarity.ts` with each seat's deck bias; harm at its own permanent with a "when this dies" trigger, a Dragon Egg, is tallied apart as a play), own turns that end with a sorcery-speed spell castable and unplayed, skipped land drops, and time by kind of window against the room's budget (`--budget 300` by default, on 8 workers because contention changes what a wall-clock budget buys; `--budget none` is exact and takes every core; `--weights JSON` compares two vectors on the same seeds); `bot:crackback` (`bot-crackback.mjs`, 2026-10-05) logs every v2 self-play attack declaration — everything able to attack sent, some or none — with what the table showed (the next opponent, every opponent all-in, and the bot's own check, through `combat-math.ts`) on three boards (holding everything back, the declaration, and the handoff: the board the opponents got once its turn ended), and whether it died before its next turn and to what (creatures on the board at the swing or arriving after, noncombat, haste native or granted, haste enablers showing); `--weights JSON` plays every seat on another vector, resumable NDJSON in `.scratch/crackback-v2-<n>p.ndjson`, `--report FILE` reprints one; `bot:audit` prices a card by spawning it on a fixed board and printing the score change (`-- --rollout` adds what it's worth once the next turns are *played out*, and the gap between the two columns is the enablement a static score can't price). Every one of these takes `--champion <id>` where it makes sense. **Across commits** (`baseline-build.mjs`: the engine at a git ref built from `git archive` into the git-ignored `.scratch/baseline/<sha>/`, beside a frozen copy of the working `dist`, one table seating both since `GameState` is plain data): `bot:ab` benches the working bot in one seat against the baseline's in the rest (`--base origin/main`, `--bot v1|v2`, resumable NDJSON in `.scratch/`, `--budget MS|live` for a wall-clock budget a decision on every seat, at 8 workers or fewer), and `bot:diff` plays every seat on the working build while asking the baseline's bot each same question, listing every disagreement in words — what a change changed, where a bench only says whether it won. `bot:replay` plays one seed (`--snapshot-at TURN` saves `.scratch/replays/seed-N-tTURN.json`; `--from FILE` restarts there, from a snapshot or a `captures/` capture or bug report; `--budget MS|live` gives v2 a wall-clock budget) logging slow decisions, activations and each turn's time. **Decision cost** (2026-11-10, the bot audit's probes, `docs/plans/bot-audit-fixes.md` step 0): `bot:anatomy -- --from FILE` (`bot-anatomy.mjs`) plays on from a saved position at count budgets and prints, for each searched decision, its milliseconds, simulations, ticks, clones, `legalActions` and v1 calls, then the position's unit costs and ms a simulation and ms a tick — the yardstick for a speed change, on the fixed positions in `data/bot-positions/` (bench-deck boards; its README says where each came from); `bot:timings` (`bot-timings.mjs`, not `bot:harvest`, which is the fit's training set) plays bench games at the live budget (`LIVE_BOT_BUDGET_MS`, `eval-bot.ts`, which `room.ts` seats bots with and `bot:behaviour` defaults to) and prints what searched decisions cost by board size and kind, the one-simulation searches, and what v1 would have played where the clock cut a priority search to its baselines (`--snaps` saves big-board positions; `--sum DIR` prints a run again; `--pass-memo check` searches at every pass-memo hit and counts the ones the search answered differently), and the share of priority searches under the `"acting"` rollout. `EvalBotOptions.now` is the clock a test drives. **Deck strength** (2026-10-02): `bot:decks` (`deck-winrates.mjs`) seats the same bot in every seat over all of `SAMPLE_DECKS` — each round deals every deck once into tables, each table played once per seat rotation — and prints each deck's win rate (Wilson interval), average finish and winning turn; resumable NDJSON in `.scratch/deck-winrates-<bot>-<n>p.ndjson`. It's what decides a deck's `bench` flag, and beside a v1 run, which decks the search helps. `bot:autopsy` (`deck-autopsy.mjs`) replays one of its games exactly and prints each turn: every player's life, hand, library, graveyard, lands and board (`--hands` adds the hands), and the plays — lands, spells, activations, attacks and their targets, damage to players, mills, mulligans. The `ab-bench`, `decision-diff` and `replay-seed` skills drive them.
- Each major feature has its own dedicated engine test file rather than a standalone demo script (commander, mulligans, legend rule, multiplayer, bounce, X-spells, sacrifice, hand disruption, fight/keywords, counterspell, flash/prowess, proliferate, combat-damage triggers, control-change, base-P/T CDA, clone, animate, text/color, replacements, modal, filters/scopes, tutors, static restrictions, ability costs, ward, cost modification, protection, and one file per "needed-cards" pass — dual-lands, graveyard-recursion, landfall-payloads, count-scaled, trigger-value, token-for-target, token-copy, sacrifice-cost, intervening-if, additional-costs, token-stacking, mdfc, transform, phase10-misc). Read the relevant one for a runnable example of how a mechanic works.
